Cacti are severely threatened by drought in a warming world

Cacti are severely threatened by drought in a warming world. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The paper examined how climate change threatens cactus species globally, with a particular focus on whether including extreme drought events changes projected outcomes compared to models based only on long-term climate averages. The researchers assessed suitable climatic area for nearly all described cactus species under future climate scenarios, comparing models that incorporate severe drought against those that do not. They found that a substantial majority of cactus species are projected to lose large portions of their suitable climatic area by the end of the century, with a notable fraction facing near-total loss. The study concludes that conventional climate models relying solely on average conditions substantially underestimate the threat that climate change poses to cacti, highlighting the importance of incorporating extreme weather events into biodiversity forecasts.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it reveals that standard biodiversity forecasts, by ignoring extreme drought events, may drastically underestimate climate change's threat to cacti. When severe droughts are factored in, projections show that the vast majority of cactus species face massive losses of suitable habitat by the end of the century.

Proteins that form the nuclear pore barrier move like liquids

Proteins that form the nuclear pore barrier move like liquids. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The study investigated the physical behavior of intrinsically disordered proteins that form the selective barrier within nuclear pores, which regulate the passage of molecules between the cytoplasm and nucleus. Using measurements in living cells combined with molecular-dynamics simulations, the researchers examined the mobility of these flexible protein chains, focusing on the nucleoporin NUP98. They found that these barrier-forming proteins are highly mobile and move in a liquid-like manner, and that this dynamic state is maintained by a specific sugar modification and the transport carrier protein importin-β. The work reveals that the nuclear pore's selectivity barrier is not a static structure but rather a fluid, regulated system whose properties are actively controlled by post-translational modifications and transport receptors.

Why it is getting attention: This work draws attention because it reveals that the protein chains forming the selective barrier of nuclear pores behave as highly mobile, liquid-like structures rather than static ones, overturning assumptions about how this critical gateway operates. The findings, combining live-cell measurements with simulations, further identify specific molecular factors that maintain this dynamic state, offering new mechanistic insight into how cells regulate traffic into and out of the nucleus.

Earth’s early history told by 3.9-billion-year-old rocks from northern China

Earth’s early history told by 3.9-billion-year-old rocks from northern China. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The study examined ancient rocks from northern China to investigate conditions on early Earth. By analyzing these rocks and zircon crystals they contain, the researchers reconstructed the geological processes occurring billions of years ago. They found that by approximately the time these rocks formed, surface-altered oceanic crust had been rapidly buried to depths exceeding fifty kilometres and partially melted, indicating that subduction-like processes were active very early in Earth's history. The source of this crust was traced to a mantle reservoir that had already differentiated from the early mantle hundreds of millions of years prior. These findings push back the timeline for crustal recycling and deep burial processes on Earth significantly.

Why it is getting attention: These ancient rocks are attracting attention because they provide direct evidence of very early plate-tectonic-like processes, showing that oceanic crust was being rapidly subducted to great depths far earlier in Earth's history than previously understood. They also push back the timeline for the separation of Earth's crust from its mantle to an exceptionally early point in the planet's history.

Proposed approach for diagnosing obesity outperforms old method

Proposed approach for diagnosing obesity outperforms old method. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The paper studied whether a recently proposed diagnostic framework for obesity performs better than conventional body-mass-index-based criteria. The new framework incorporates waist circumference and related measures, and distinguishes clinical from preclinical obesity based on evidence of obesity-induced organ dysfunction. Using a large international multi-cohort study of adults, the researchers compared the two approaches for diagnosing obesity and stratifying mortality risk. They found that combining body measurements with organ-function metrics outperformed body mass index alone in both classifying obesity and predicting mortality.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because a newer obesity diagnostic framework—which incorporates waist circumference and distinguishes clinical from preclinical obesity based on organ dysfunction—was shown in a large international multi-cohort study to outperform the long-standing body-mass-index-based approach in both diagnosing obesity and predicting mortality risk.

Human replisomes direct a dedicated leading-strand primase for fork restart

Cristian Polo Rivera, Johann J. Roske, Emma Langdale Hands et al.. Human replisomes direct a dedicated leading-strand primase for fork restart. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigated how stalled DNA replication forks are restarted in human cells, focusing on the role of the primase-polymerase PRIMPOL during replication stress. Using purified human proteins to biochemically reconstitute fork stalling and restart, the researchers examined how PRIMPOL activity is coordinated by the replisome machinery. They discovered that the CMG helicase directly binds PRIMPOL and directs its activity specifically toward the leading strand, a finding supported by a cryo-electron microscopy structure showing the molecular basis of this interaction. Contrary to prior assumptions, the interaction between PRIMPOL and replication protein A was not required for fork restart in this reconstituted system. Disrupting the CMG–PRIMPOL interaction eliminated fork restart in vitro and impaired DNA damage tolerance in human cells, establishing PRIMPOL as a dedicated, integral component of the human replisome rather than an accessory factor recruited independently.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it reveals a previously uncharacterized direct interaction between the core replication machinery and a specialized primase, explaining at a structural and biochemical level how cells restart stalled replication forks and prevent potentially dangerous gaps in newly synthesized DNA.

Bureaucratic incentives shaped effectiveness of the one-child policy in China

Lingsheng Meng, Han‐Mo Yang, Grant Miller et al.. Bureaucratic incentives shaped effectiveness of the one-child policy in China. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The paper examined why China's fertility decline was delayed after the one-child policy was introduced, focusing on how bureaucratic enforcement mechanisms shaped policy effectiveness. The researchers investigated the role of the "one vote veto" policy, which barred local officials from career advancement if they failed to meet birth control targets, using provincial variation in when this stricter enforcement was implemented. They found that this strengthening of bureaucratic incentives was instrumental in driving the sharp fertility decline observed in the early 1990s, accounting for nearly half of the total fertility rate decline during that decade and pushing fertility below replacement level. Increased official promotion of intrauterine devices as a contraceptive method was identified as a particularly important pathway through which the policy operated. The findings challenge earlier research suggesting population policies have limited demographic impact, arguing instead that stringent enforcement mechanisms can make such policies substantially more effective.

Why it is getting attention: This work draws attention by revealing that bureaucratic incentive structures — rather than the one-child policy itself in isolation — were a major driver of China's fertility decline, with stronger enforcement mechanisms accounting for nearly half of that decline during a key decade.

Validation of clinical and preclinical obesity across global cohorts

An Pan, Shengping Yang, Rafael Ramos et al.. Validation of clinical and preclinical obesity across global cohorts. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The paper evaluated a new diagnostic framework proposed by a major medical commission that distinguishes clinical obesity, characterized by organ dysfunction due to excess adiposity, from preclinical obesity, where excess adiposity exists but organ function is preserved, while also recommending anthropometric measures beyond body mass index alone. Using prevalence, incidence, and mortality data from hundreds of thousands of adults across cohorts in China, the United Kingdom, the United States, and Spain, the researchers compared this framework against conventional body mass index-based classification. They found that body mass index alone both overestimated and underestimated obesity in meaningful proportions of participants, and that incorporating waist circumference and waist-to-height ratio increased overall obesity prevalence estimates compared to body mass index alone. Clinical obesity represented roughly a quarter to a third of all obesity cases, and the large majority of individuals with preclinical obesity did not progress to clinical obesity over a decade of follow-up. Critically, clinical obesity was associated with substantially higher all-cause mortality risk than preclinical obesity, and the combined anthropometric definition predicted mortality better than body mass index alone, supporting broader adoption of the new diagnostic framework in clinical practice and health policy.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it validates a proposed diagnostic framework that moves beyond BMI alone by incorporating additional measures like waist circumference and organ function to better classify obesity. The findings suggest this more nuanced approach offers greater clinical utility than traditional BMI-only classifications across diverse global populations.

UNC13A splice correction rescues synaptic dysfunction induced by TDP-43 loss

Matthew J. Keuss, Peter Harley, Eugeni Ryadnov et al.. UNC13A splice correction rescues synaptic dysfunction induced by TDP-43 loss. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigated whether loss of TDP-43 nuclear function, a hallmark of amyotrophic lateral sclerosis and frontotemporal dementia, impairs synaptic activity in neurons. The researchers found that TDP-43 depletion induces a predegenerative state in which neurons remain structurally intact yet exhibit profound breakdown in synaptic communication, and they identified aberrant cryptic splicing of the UNC13A gene as a key driver of this dysfunction. To address this, they developed splice-switching antisense oligonucleotides capable of correcting UNC13A cryptic splicing in human cortical and spinal motor neurons as well as in a humanized mouse model. Correcting this single splicing defect was sufficient to restore spontaneous and evoked synaptic vesicle release, release probability at individual synapses, and broader network synchrony in human neurons. The findings establish UNC13A splice correction as a mechanistically grounded and genetically validated therapeutic strategy potentially applicable to the vast majority of patients with TDP-43 pathology across both diseases.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it identifies a pre-degenerative state in which neurons lose synaptic function before structural breakdown occurs, linking this directly to TDP-43 loss and aberrant *UNC13A* splicing. Crucially, it demonstrates that splice-switching antisense oligonucleotides can correct this cryptic splicing event and rescue synaptic dysfunction, pointing toward a potential therapeutic strategy for related diseases.

NRF2 is a mechanosensitive checkpoint for NLRP3 inflammasome activation

Daniel A. Okin, Joon H. Choi, Chun Wang et al.. NRF2 is a mechanosensitive checkpoint for NLRP3 inflammasome activation. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The paper investigated why NLRP3 inflammasome activation behaves differently in laboratory cell culture compared to living organisms, a longstanding disconnect in the field. The researchers discovered that extracellular matrix stiffness is a key environmental variable governing this difference, finding that culturing human and mouse phagocytes on substrates with physiologically relevant stiffness recapitulates the in vivo condition where a single inflammatory signal (such as lipopolysaccharide alone) is sufficient to activate the NLRP3 inflammasome. They identified the transcription factor NRF2 as a mechanosensitive checkpoint induced by physiological matrix stiffness, which in turn promotes NLRP3 activity through transcriptional regulation of the gene Panx1. Consistent with this mechanism, macrophages residing in naturally stiff tissues showed altered NRF2 levels and corresponding changes in inflammasome activity, and patients with pulmonary fibrosis displayed reduced NRF2 activity in alveolar macrophages. The study further demonstrated that NRF2 is required for effective antitumour immunity when NLRP3-activating adjuvants are used in cancer vaccines, positioning NRF2 as a central transcriptional regulator linking mechanical tissue cues to innate immune responses.

Why it is getting attention: This work draws attention by revealing that physical properties of the cellular environment—specifically extracellular matrix stiffness—can regulate a key inflammatory signaling complex, with NRF2 serving as the mechanosensitive link between mechanical cues and NLRP3 inflammasome activation both in vitro and in vivo.

Non-halogen aqueous electrolytes for four-electron iodine conversion

Guanjie He, Haobo Dong, Yuhang Dai et al.. Non-halogen aqueous electrolytes for four-electron iodine conversion. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigated how to achieve reversible four-electron iodine redox chemistry in aqueous metal-iodine batteries without relying on the halogen-containing electrolytes that have traditionally been required. The researchers developed a electrolyte design framework combining kosmotropic anions, polar anions, and nucleophilic species, where polar anions create a water-deficient, anion-enriched environment at the electrode interface and kosmotropic anions regulate interactions to increase the availability of nucleophilic species for stabilizing reactive hypervalent iodine cations through halogen bonding. This approach stabilizes the hypervalent iodine intermediates through nucleophile–iodine cation–nucleophile halogen bonds rather than the conventional interhalogen complexation mechanism. The framework was validated using diverse nucleophilic species and demonstrated to work in both aqueous zinc-iodine and aluminum-iodine battery systems. The findings establish a mechanistically distinct electrolyte chemistry that enables high-energy aqueous energy storage while eliminating the need for corrosive halogen-containing electrolytes.

Why it is getting attention: This work draws attention because it achieves reversible four-electron iodine conversion—a high-capacity electrochemical process—using halogen-free aqueous electrolytes, overcoming a longstanding chemical compatibility challenge. The approach is validated in functional metal–iodine batteries, suggesting broad practical relevance for energy storage.

Bistable regulation of adipocyte heterogeneity governs thermogenesis

Anying Song, Yifan Wang, Guan Wang et al.. Bistable regulation of adipocyte heterogeneity governs thermogenesis. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigated how brown adipose tissue maintains two distinct subpopulations of brown adipocytes—those with high and low expression of the thermogenic protein UCP1—and how the balance between these states governs heat production. The researchers found that inflammatory cytokine IL-1β, secreted by neutrophils and macrophages, stabilizes a signaling enzyme that generates active glucocorticoids, which in turn activate the glucocorticoid receptor and suppress UCP1 expression, locking adipocytes in a low-thermogenic state. Cold exposure reduces IL-1β levels through sympathetic nervous system signaling, allowing the stabilizing enzyme to degrade and enabling low-UCP1 cells to convert into high-UCP1 thermogenic cells, while warming reverses this process. Genetically deleting the glucocorticoid receptor specifically in brown adipocytes during cold exposure locked cells in the high-thermogenic state and sustained elevated energy expenditure even after returning to normal temperatures. Crucially, aged animals showed chronically elevated IL-1β in brown adipose tissue that skewed the balance toward the low-thermogenic state, causing cold intolerance, and suppressing glucocorticoid receptor signaling in aged mice restored thermogenic capacity and energy expenditure.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it reveals a bistable regulatory mechanism by which immune signals from neutrophils and macrophages control brown adipocyte identity and thermogenic capacity. It shows that cold exposure drives a switch to high heat-producing states by suppressing this immune signaling through the nervous system, fundamentally linking inflammation, neural activity, and fat cell heterogeneity in temperature regulation.

Epigenetic heterogeneity in breast tumours informs diagnosis and prognosis

Noa Furth, Christina Riemenschneider, Yishai Arieli et al.. Epigenetic heterogeneity in breast tumours informs diagnosis and prognosis. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This study investigated epigenetic heterogeneity within breast tumours, focusing on how distinct epigenetic cell populations within luminal-subtype tumours relate to cancer biology, diagnosis, and prognosis. Using a single-cell epigenetic approach based on cytometry by time-of-flight, along with whole-genome DNA methylation deconvolution and single-cell proteomic and genomic profiling of histone modifications, the researchers profiled treatment-naive human luminal tumours obtained after surgical resection. They discovered a recurrent subpopulation of basal-like cells within luminal tumours that carries a conserved epigenetic signature across patients, characterised by aggressive oncogenic traits, downregulated hormone receptor signalling, and alterations in histone methylation marks linked to loss of luminal identity. The study further demonstrated that this basal-like epigenetic signature can be detected in cell-free nucleosomes from blood, establishing a potential liquid biopsy approach for early breast cancer diagnosis. Critically, detection of this basal signature in blood was associated with prognosis, suggesting it could identify patients at heightened risk of disease relapse.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because single-cell epigenetic analyses of breast tumours have uncovered a conserved signature that could improve both diagnosis and the prediction of which patients are at heightened risk of disease relapse.

A plant RNA-binding protein senses 2′,3′-cNMP produced by insect effectors

Xiao‐Xiang Guo, Jia‐Fa Huang, Jinsong Zhao et al.. A plant RNA-binding protein senses 2′,3′-cNMP produced by insect effectors. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This study investigated how rice plants detect and respond to feeding by sucking insects, specifically focusing on the molecular signals generated during insect attack. The researchers discovered that a glycine-rich RNA-binding protein called NIB1 serves as a sensor for small cyclic nucleotide molecules produced when a salivary ribonuclease from green stink bugs digests plant RNA during feeding. When NIB1 binds these nuclease-generated products, it forms condensates in the nucleus that recruit a core immune regulator, thereby activating plant defences. The findings further suggest a mechanistic link between this insect-triggered signalling pathway and the defence signalling mediated by plant TIR proteins, which independently produce similar nuclease products as immune signals. Overall, the study reveals a conserved immune pathway in which glycine-rich RNA-binding proteins act as sensors of nuclease-derived second messengers to coordinate plant defence responses.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it uncovers a previously unrecognized plant immune mechanism in which an RNA-binding protein detects small-molecule signals generated by insect salivary enzymes, triggering a nuclear defense response. The findings suggest this messenger-based surveillance pathway is conserved, potentially reshaping understanding of how plants recognize and respond to herbivorous insects.

Flexible rubber blades for tidal turbines

Zhehong Zhang, Junchen Tan, Eric-Carin Neba et al.. Flexible rubber blades for tidal turbines. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

Tidal power offers predictable renewable energy but exposes turbines to severe and variable hydrodynamic loads, driving up costs through the need for complex active pitch control systems. This study investigated whether passively flexible rubber blades, designed to deform along the chord at the trailing edge, could serve as a simpler alternative for load management. Through experimental testing under simulated tidal cycle conditions, the researchers found that flexible blades matched the total energy yield of rigid blades equipped with active pitch control. Crucially, the flexible blades also reduced high-frequency fatigue-inducing load fluctuations by up to one-third, with this passive regulation arising from aeroelastic deformation that flattens blade camber under loading. The results suggest that chordwise-flexible blades offer a mechanically simpler and potentially more cost-effective route to durable tidal turbine design.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because flexible rubber blades achieve energy output comparable to more complex active-control systems while substantially cutting the load fluctuations that cause wear and mechanical failure. The combination of maintained performance with reduced maintenance complexity represents a meaningful advance for practical tidal energy deployment.

Hadean mantle depletion and 3.95-Gyr subduction implied by North China rocks

Hangqiang Xie, Chunyan Dong, Pengchuan Li et al.. Hadean mantle depletion and 3.95-Gyr subduction implied by North China rocks. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper reports the discovery of an ancient trondhjemitic gneiss from the North China Craton, extending the known record of terrestrial rocks older than roughly four billion years beyond the previously unique Acasta Gneiss Complex in Canada. The researchers analyzed magmatic zircons from this rock, measuring oxygen isotope ratios and hafnium isotope compositions, which together indicate that the source material was low-temperature altered basaltic crust derived from a Hadean depleted mantle. Unlike the Acasta rocks, which formed under low-pressure conditions, this gneiss displays geochemical signatures consistent with a high-pressure origin, and thermodynamic and trace element modelling indicates that its parental magma formed through partial melting of basaltic crust at depths corresponding to pressures exceeding one and a half gigapascals along a cool thermal gradient. The researchers interpret these conditions as requiring the rapid burial of near-surface altered oceanic crust to mantle depths, most plausibly achieved through a transient subduction event approximately three and a half billion years ago. The findings suggest that both Hadean mantle depletion and early subduction-like processes were operating on Earth by this time.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it pushes evidence for subduction — a hallmark of modern-style plate tectonics — back to nearly four billion years ago, among the earliest such signatures yet proposed. The ancient rocks also imply that mantle depletion began even earlier, during the Hadean, offering rare geochemical insight into Earth's formative geological processes.

Human land use drives microplastic abundance in terrestrial wildlife

Marcia de Almeida Monteiro Melo Ferraz, Arnaud Léonard Jean Desbiez, Mário Henrique Alves et al.. Human land use drives microplastic abundance in terrestrial wildlife. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This study investigated how human land-use change influences microplastic accumulation in free-ranging terrestrial mammals in Brazil. The researchers combined direct microplastic measurements from living animals with high-resolution GPS tracking data from lowland tapirs and giant anteaters to link individual habitat use with microplastic burdens. They found that microplastic loads in these mammals increased with greater exposure to human-modified landscapes and closer proximity to development and waste management infrastructure. Notably, microplastics were also detected in the blood of animals inhabiting apparently pristine, remote habitats, indicating that direct contact with developed areas is not the only pathway to systemic microplastic contamination. The findings suggest that managing wildlife proximity to anthropogenic infrastructure could help reduce microplastic burdens in terrestrial animals amid ongoing global land-use change.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it links human-modified land use and proximity to development directly to microplastic accumulation in wild mammals, while also revealing that even animals in remote habitats are not spared, underscoring the pervasive reach of plastic pollution across landscapes.

Transient nuclear softening for zygotic gene activation in mice

Masahito Tanaka, Rin Sakanoue, Yasuki Miyagawa et al.. Transient nuclear softening for zygotic gene activation in mice. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

During early mouse embryogenesis, nuclei undergo extensive epigenetic reprogramming, yet how the physical properties of the nucleus itself change during this process had remained largely unexplored. Using direct organelle capture and biophysical manipulation, researchers examined the mechanical properties of nuclei at the two-cell embryonic stage and discovered that nuclei become remarkably soft and deformable precisely when zygotic gene activation occurs. This transient softness results from autophagy-mediated clearance of lamin B1 from the nuclear envelope, and experimentally preventing this clearance or artificially stiffening the nucleus impairs zygotic gene activation and embryonic development. High-resolution imaging and sequential three-dimensional fluorescence in situ hybridization revealed that nuclear deformability facilitates the accumulation of active RNA polymerase II and the coordinated positioning of key genomic loci near the nuclear envelope. The findings establish that a distinct, transient physical state of the nucleus is essential for initiating the zygotic transcription program at the onset of mammalian life.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it reveals that embryos transiently adopt a physically softer nuclear state at the very start of life, and that this mechanical change in the nucleus plays a functional role in activating the embryo's own gene expression program.

Suppression of epithelial proliferation and tumorigenesis by IgA

Gregory P. Donaldson, Gabriella Lima dos Reis, Marwa Saad et al.. Suppression of epithelial proliferation and tumorigenesis by IgA. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

Abstract unavailable. Open the link to read more.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it identifies a previously unrecognized role for a common gut antibody in controlling the rate at which colon lining cells divide and in protecting against colorectal cancer. The findings, demonstrated in mouse models, suggest that immune factors within the gut lumen itself may directly influence epithelial cell turnover and tumor susceptibility.

Evolutionary dynamics in the years preceding acute myeloid leukaemia

Caroline J. Watson, Gladys Y. P. Poon, Hamish A J MacGregor et al.. Evolutionary dynamics in the years preceding acute myeloid leukaemia. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This study investigated the pre-cancerous somatic evolutionary dynamics that precede acute myeloid leukaemia, with the goal of quantifying how driver mutations accumulate and expand in blood over time. Using serial blood samples collected annually for up to a decade before leukaemia diagnosis, the researchers applied deep duplex sequencing combined with evolutionary theory to reconstruct the timing and fitness effects of key driver events. They found that initial driver mutations are typically acquired early in life, when blood remains highly polyclonal, and that these early clones grow slowly. Subsequently, the acquisition of additional driver mutations confers dramatically larger selective advantages, enabling faster-growing multi-driver clones to completely sweep through the blood compartment decades before diagnosis, a pattern strongly associated with eventual leukaemia development. The findings suggest that clonal haematopoiesis follows simple evolutionary rules that balance stochastic and deterministic processes, providing a quantitative framework for understanding cancer predisposition.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it reveals the somatic evolutionary trajectories of blood cells in the period before acute myeloid leukaemia develops, showing that clonal dynamics follow predictable, simple evolutionary rules. Understanding these pre-diagnostic patterns could shed light on how leukaemia arises and potentially inform early detection strategies.

Scale-rich network-based metamaterials

Csaba Both, Andrew Yen-Jong Chen, Ting-Ting Gao et al.. Scale-rich network-based metamaterials. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper introduces a design framework called scale-rich metamaterials, which embeds the kind of multiscale structural heterogeneity found in real-world and biological networks—such as the brain or the Internet—into the architecture of mechanical metamaterials. Unlike conventional metamaterials that rely on periodic, homogeneous unit cells with discrete length scales, the proposed framework uses network science principles to generate structures with orders-of-magnitude variation in ligament lengths, thicknesses, and connectivity, governed by just two parameters. Through a combination of simulations and physical experiments, the authors demonstrate that these scale-rich metamaterials exhibit mechanical properties unattainable by traditional single-scale systems, including tunable elastic anisotropy, delocalized nonlinear deformation, high energy absorption, enhanced dynamic energy dissipation, and programmable elastic-wave control. The work establishes network heterogeneity as a minimal and universal basis for engineering multifunctional materials whose behavior emerges directly from structural scale diversity, while also suggesting new directions for network science research.

Why it is getting attention: By applying network-science principles to mechanical metamaterial design, this work introduces structural heterogeneity across multiple scales, unlocking mechanical properties that conventional single-scale systems cannot achieve.

The molecular basis of replication fork restart via repriming by PRIMPOL

Jinho Park, Luke D. Lynch, Elena Blanco‐Romero et al.. The molecular basis of replication fork restart via repriming by PRIMPOL. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The paper investigated how stalled DNA replication forks are restarted through a process called repriming carried out by the enzyme PRIMPOL, focusing on the molecular mechanisms governing its recruitment, positioning, and regulation. Using real-time single-molecule imaging in nuclear extracts that recapitulate genome maintenance, the researchers directly visualized repriming events at stalled forks. They found that PRIMPOL is recruited to stalled forks via a direct interaction with the replicative helicase complex CMG, which facilitates handover of newly synthesized primers to the high-fidelity polymerase ε, thereby minimizing mutagenic DNA synthesis. Under near-physiological conditions this primary repriming mode operates close to CMG and does not require DNA damage checkpoint signalling, whereas PRIMPOL overexpression or checkpoint activation triggers a secondary mode in which repriming occurs far from CMG and primers are extended by polymerase δ instead. Together, these findings reveal a targeted, CMG-dependent mechanism that restricts PRIMPOL activity to stalled forks and suggest a potential strategy for inhibiting the repriming pathway.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it reveals the molecular mechanism by which PRIMPOL restarts stalled replication forks, showing how it is recruited to the replicative helicase and hands off primers to a high-fidelity polymerase to limit error-prone DNA synthesis.

Domain-alteration chimeras rewire oncogenic complexes in prostate cancer

Jie Luo, Jianzhang Yang, Jean Ching-Yi Tien et al.. Domain-alteration chimeras rewire oncogenic complexes in prostate cancer. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This study investigated a new therapeutic strategy for metastatic castration-resistant prostate cancer, which depends on androgen receptor signaling maintained through its interactions with the acetyltransferases p300 and CBP. The researchers developed a novel class of molecules called domain-alteration chimeras (DALTACs) that work by enforcing non-native interactions between specific protein domains, thereby rewiring endogenous oncogenic protein complexes rather than simply inhibiting them. Their lead compound forces proximity between the androgen receptor's ligand-binding domain and the bromodomain of p300/CBP, locking the complex in a non-productive configuration that suppresses androgen receptor-driven transcription more effectively than blocking either target alone. Mechanistically, this intervention reprograms how p300/CBP engages its substrates, eliminates specific histone acetylation marks, induces aberrant acetylation of the androgen receptor and its coactivators, and disrupts the assembly of transcriptional machinery at oncogenic enhancers. The compound demonstrated selective activity in androgen receptor-dependent cancer cells while sparing others, and produced sustained tumor regressions in multiple animal models, suggesting broad applicability of the DALTAC strategy for rewiring disease-relevant protein complexes.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it introduces a new class of molecules that force abnormal interactions between a key cancer-driving protein and its regulatory partners, achieving unusually potent and selective suppression of metastatic prostate cancer. Beyond the specific disease application, the approach is framed as a broadly applicable therapeutic strategy for rewiring oncogenic protein complexes through induced proximity.

Glycosylation and importins liquefy the FG barrier in live nuclear pores

Miao Yu, Maziar Heidari, Karen Palacio‐Rodríguez et al.. Glycosylation and importins liquefy the FG barrier in live nuclear pores. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

The nuclear pore complex controls selective molecular traffic between the nucleus and cytoplasm through a permeability barrier made of intrinsically disordered phenylalanine-glycine-rich nucleoporins (FG-NUPs), but the physical state of these proteins inside living cells has remained contentious. Using site-specific labelling combined with time-resolved fluorescence anisotropy and molecular-dynamics simulations, the researchers investigated how FG domains behave in live cells compared to reconstituted systems. They found that FG domains display rapid, liquid-like segmental motion on nanosecond timescales in living cells, whereas reconstituted FG condensates undergo a liquid-to-solid transition in vitro, highlighting the importance of cellular context. The transport receptor importin-β and O-linked glycosylation of FG-NUPs together act to stabilize this dynamic liquid-like network, generating a radial organization in which importin-β concentrates near the pore periphery while extended FG domains occupy the central channel to form the transport barrier. These findings reconcile previously conflicting models of FG-NUP organization and are supported by direct nanoscale imaging of importin-β localization, providing a unified framework for how intrinsically disordered proteins are tuned by their cellular environment.

Why it is getting attention: This work is attracting attention because it reveals the molecular mechanisms by which nuclear pore proteins form a fluid yet selective barrier, offering a resolution to long-standing, competing models of how nuclear pores are structured and how they achieve selective transport.

Low-power 2D CFETs using single-crystal high-κ dielectrics

Ni Yang, Ji Zhang, Yu-Cheng Lu et al.. Low-power 2D CFETs using single-crystal high-κ dielectrics. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper investigated the challenge of reducing power consumption in scaled silicon electronics by exploring two-dimensional semiconductor transistors with improved gate dielectric interfaces. The researchers developed a wafer-scale, transferable single-crystal anatase titanium dioxide dielectric that can be integrated with two-dimensional transition metal dichalcogenides via van der Waals bonding, creating an exceptionally clean interface. This pristine interface yielded subthreshold swing approaching the fundamental thermal limit, record-low hysteresis, and stable performance for both n- and p-type enhancement-mode transistors. Using these components, the team fabricated complementary field-effect transistors whose inverters demonstrated high voltage gain, large noise margins, and subpicowatt static power consumption. The results establish single-crystal high-κ dielectrics as a compelling route to ultra-low-power operation in two-dimensional semiconductor devices.

Why it is getting attention: This work draws attention because using a transferable single-crystal high-κ dielectric achieves high-quality interfaces with two-dimensional semiconductors, resulting in near-ideal switching behavior, low hysteresis, and ultra-low-power complementary transistors—addressing longstanding challenges in scaling next-generation low-power electronics.

Independent interferometric observable confirms black hole shadow in M87*

Samuel Lai, Nithyanandan Thyagarajan, O. Ivy Wong et al.. Independent interferometric observable confirms black hole shadow in M87*. Nature. 2026 commentary, not primary research

This paper addresses ongoing debates about the reliability of the first black hole shadow image of M87*, where different imaging methods and data analysis choices had produced inconsistent representations of the source's fine-scale structure, with some analyses even questioning whether the fundamental ring-like morphology was real. The challenge stems from the sparse interferometric measurements of very long baseline interferometry, which make image reconstruction an underdetermined problem highly sensitive to calibration, algorithms, and tuning parameters. The researchers developed and applied an independent imaging algorithm that avoids prescriptive morphological assumptions and relies on observables that are immune to instrument calibration errors. Their method independently reconstructed a ring-like morphology from the same dataset, unequivocally confirming the black hole shadow, with the inferred angular diameter consistent with a black hole mass in agreement with previous estimates and an inclination broadly consistent with the known jet orientation. This work strengthens confidence in the original black hole shadow detection by demonstrating that the ring morphology is robust across fundamentally different imaging approaches.

Why it is getting attention: This work is drawing attention because it independently confirms the black hole shadow and mass using a novel approach that sidesteps the calibration and imaging assumptions that have constrained previous analyses. The use of closure-invariant formalism combined with diffusion-based reconstruction provides a more robust, assumption-free validation of one of astronomy's landmark observations.

PCR-Based Molecular Diagnostics for Strengthening Environmental Pathogen Surveillance and Public Health Preparedness Across United States Communities

Iyanuoluwa Oluwafemi. PCR-Based Molecular Diagnostics for Strengthening Environmental Pathogen Surveillance and Public Health Preparedness Across United States Communities. International Journal of Research Publication and Reviews. 2026

This review paper examines the role of PCR-based molecular diagnostic technologies in strengthening environmental pathogen surveillance and public health preparedness across the United States. The authors critically assess advances across multiple PCR formats, including conventional, quantitative, digital, and multiplex approaches, as well as point-of-need platforms, evaluating their application to complex environmental matrices such as wastewater, surface water, soil, and air. The review addresses key methodological considerations including assay design, sample processing, quality assurance, and computational data interpretation, alongside integration with national surveillance infrastructures such as wastewater-based epidemiology programs. The authors find that PCR technologies offer substantial advantages over conventional culture-based methods in terms of speed, sensitivity, and throughput for detecting bacterial, viral, fungal, and protozoan pathogens, with demonstrated utility for early outbreak detection and antimicrobial resistance monitoring. The review concludes by identifying current implementation challenges and highlighting opportunities to combine artificial intelligence with molecular diagnostics to further enhance surveillance capacity and public health resilience.

Policy and Governance of Disease Surveillance Systems in Nigeria: Addressing Emerging Microbial Threats

Nnamdi Michael Nwadiogbu, James Kodilichukwu Anekwe, Obioma Davison Mbanefo et al.. Policy and Governance of Disease Surveillance Systems in Nigeria: Addressing Emerging Microbial Threats. 2026

This review examines the policies, governance structures, and operational capacity of disease surveillance systems in Nigeria as they relate to managing infectious disease threats and antimicrobial resistance. The authors critically assessed the country's Integrated Disease Surveillance and Response framework, coordinated by the national disease control agency, alongside the broader public health infrastructure responsible for outbreak detection and response. Nigeria's experience managing major outbreaks has yielded meaningful advances in workforce capacity and laboratory networks, yet the review identifies persistent weaknesses including fragmented data systems, inadequate diagnostic infrastructure at sub-national levels, insufficient funding, and poor integration of One Health principles. Promising developments noted include the launch of a national antimicrobial resistance survey and early exploration of wastewater-based epidemiology as a surveillance tool. The review concludes that although national policy frameworks are broadly sound, achieving genuine health security will require sustained investment in laboratory capacity, digital surveillance infrastructure, antibiotic stewardship, and equitable implementation of surveillance systems down to the local level.

Comprehensive sequence analysis reveals the vast β-lactam resistome

Sevan Gholipour, John Z. Chen, Dong‐Kyu Lee et al.. Comprehensive sequence analysis reveals the vast β-lactam resistome. Microbiome. 2026

This study investigated the environmental reservoir of β-lactamase resistance genes, which are enzymes that confer resistance to β-lactam antibiotics and are thought to have ancient origins in environmental microorganisms. Using an integrated bioinformatics and experimental workflow applied to large-scale sequence databases, the researchers systematically identified and classified putative β-lactamase sequences across all four enzyme classes. They discovered a reservoir of putative β-lactamases vastly exceeding what is currently catalogued in existing resistance gene databases, distributed across diverse bacterial phyla and environmental ecosystems. Notably, putative β-lactamases were detected at frequencies approaching those of an essential housekeeping gene, with relative enrichment found in wastewater and plant-associated environments, though terrestrial and aquatic settings also harbored close homologs of clinically relevant variants. The findings reveal a largely unexplored and taxonomically broad β-lactamase resistome with the potential for mobilization into clinical pathogens, and the authors provide a reusable mapping tool to support future surveillance efforts.

Resistance indicators in sewage microbiota mirror clinical antibiotic use with temporal lag linked to host

Connor Brown, MA. Rumi, Lauren F. McDaniel et al.. Resistance indicators in sewage microbiota mirror clinical antibiotic use with temporal lag linked to host. Nature Communications. 2026

This study investigated whether antibiotic resistance genes detected in wastewater can serve as meaningful indicators of community-level antibiotic use, a question complicated by the complex microbial ecology of sewer systems. The researchers combined a multi-year dataset of outpatient antibiotic prescription records with intensive metagenomic sampling of a single wastewater treatment plant over the course of a year to disentangle human-derived resistance signals from in-sewer microbial dynamics. They found that resistance genes associated with Enterobacteriaceae tracked closely and contemporaneously with antibiotic usage patterns in the community, while resistance genes linked to Pseudomonadaceae showed delayed correlations of one to three months, reflecting the distinct ecological behavior of these organisms within the sewer environment. Statistical modeling further confirmed that the dynamics of Pseudomonas populations in sewage were associated with lagged use of specific antibiotic classes after controlling for sewage characteristics and seasonality. The findings demonstrate that wastewater-based surveillance can capture epidemiologically relevant antibiotic resistance signals, but only when the host bacterial ecology shaping those signals is explicitly taken into account.

Aircraft and Airport Wastewater Surveillance for Public Health Preparedness Demands an Integrated and Sustainable Approach.

Vaidya SR, Pandey PG, Giri S et al.. Aircraft and Airport Wastewater Surveillance for Public Health Preparedness Demands an Integrated and Sustainable Approach.. Viruses. 2026

This review examines the use of wastewater collected from aircraft lavatories and airport facilities as a public health surveillance tool for detecting and monitoring infectious pathogens among traveling populations. The authors conducted a structured narrative review synthesizing current evidence on the scientific, methodological, and interpretive dimensions of aviation-based wastewater surveillance, with particular focus on lessons drawn from monitoring for the virus responsible for the pandemic. They find that while detection of viral RNA in aviation wastewater can provide timely, aggregate signals about pathogen circulation, the reliability of these signals depends on a complex array of factors including pathogen shedding dynamics, flight duration, sampling design, assay sensitivity, and the availability of epidemiological context. The review cautions that wastewater signals cannot be interpreted as individual-level diagnoses, evidence of transmission, or definitive attribution to specific flights or origin countries. The authors conclude that extending surveillance beyond the best-documented pathogen to cover a broader range of viruses, vaccine-preventable infections, and antimicrobial resistance markers will require pathogen-specific validation, and that the approach's public health value ultimately rests on converting technical detections into rigorously interpretable evidence within an integrated, sustainable surveillance framework.

From Sewers to Surveillance: Monitoring Salmonella Through Wastewater.

Kuhn KG, Shelton K, Rimal B et al.. From Sewers to Surveillance: Monitoring Salmonella Through Wastewater.. Methods in molecular biology (Clifton, N.J.). 2027

This paper examined wastewater surveillance as a complementary tool for monitoring *Salmonella* at the population level, addressing the limitations of traditional clinical surveillance in capturing asymptomatic or unreported infections. The researchers developed and validated a workflow involving polyethylene glycol precipitation for sample concentration, magnetic bead-based nucleic acid extraction, and quantitative PCR for pathogen detection. When deployed across a statewide surveillance network, the approach revealed seasonal transmission patterns and demonstrated the ability to identify outbreak activity that passive clinical surveillance missed. Notably, the wastewater signal preceded clinical case reporting by approximately one week, highlighting its potential for early warning. The authors conclude that despite challenges such as low pathogen concentrations and limited resolution at the serovar level, integrating wastewater surveillance with conventional public health systems meaningfully improves the detection and understanding of *Salmonella* dynamics.

Multi-virus Wastewater Surveillance in Heterogeneous Urban-Sewage Systems: Comparative Insights From Sewage Treatment Plants and Open Drains.

Shaikh A, Nikam S, Mistry N. Multi-virus Wastewater Surveillance in Heterogeneous Urban-Sewage Systems: Comparative Insights From Sewage Treatment Plants and Open Drains.. Food and environmental virology. 2026

This study examined how the structure of urban wastewater infrastructure affects the detection and interpretation of multiple respiratory viruses through wastewater-based epidemiology, focusing on the heterogeneous sewage systems of Mumbai. Researchers collected wastewater samples across two monitoring periods from both centralised sewage treatment plants and decentralised open drains, analysing them for SARS-CoV-2, influenza viruses, and respiratory syncytial virus using RT-qPCR. Centralised treatment plants produced more consistent viral signals with less short-term variability, reflecting their integration of signals across larger populations, while open drains showed greater temporal fluctuation consistent with more localised transmission dynamics. SARS-CoV-2 was reliably detected across both node types, whereas influenza detection was more sporadic and suggested seasonal patterns, with co-detection of influenza A and B subtypes observed in one monitoring period. The authors conclude that viral signal profiles reflect both pathogen-specific characteristics and the structural configuration of the sampling network, and advocate for integrated surveillance frameworks combining centralised and decentralised nodes alongside clinical validation to improve epidemiological inference.

Wastewater pathogen composition in Atlanta, GA prior to and during the 2026 FIFA World Cup

Raymo N, Kraft CS, Gulick D et al.. Wastewater pathogen composition in Atlanta, GA prior to and during the 2026 FIFA World Cup. 2026

This study examined how a major international mass gathering event affected the infectious disease landscape in a host city by employing wastewater-based surveillance as a complement to traditional clinical monitoring. Researchers collected wastewater samples from three water reclamation facilities before, during, and after the FIFA World Cup matches held in Atlanta, testing each sample against a broad panel of respiratory and gastrointestinal pathogen targets using multiplexed qualitative PCR assays. The primary analysis found no statistically significant differences in pathogen detection between the pre-tournament and tournament periods across the facilities. A sensitivity analysis that incorporated post-tournament samples did reveal a statistically significant increase in SARS-CoV-2 detection, but this increase mirrored regional and national trends, suggesting it reflected broader community transmission rather than a tournament-specific effect. The authors conclude that qualitative multiplexed PCR panels can serve as a practical tool for population-level pathogen surveillance during mass gathering events, while acknowledging that wide confidence intervals, the absence of quantitative data, and limited post-event sampling constrain definitive conclusions.

Machine learning-enabled wastewater-based surveillance for emerging pathogen detection and monitoring: current applications, challenges, and future prospects.

Song A, Jia P, Luo J et al.. Machine learning-enabled wastewater-based surveillance for emerging pathogen detection and monitoring: current applications, challenges, and future prospects.. Emerging microbes & infections. 2026

This review examined the application of machine learning to wastewater-based surveillance for detecting and monitoring emerging pathogens at the community level. The authors conducted a narrative review of published literature, classifying studies by their analytical role, task type, and strength of evidence, organizing methods into four layers spanning classical statistical models through deep learning and hybrid mechanistic approaches across five analytical tasks. They found that the evidence base is heavily concentrated on SARS-CoV-2 and that demonstrated early-warning performance came predominantly from simpler classical statistical methods rather than more complex machine learning approaches, with most other pathogen-task combinations remaining prospective rather than validated. Reported performance was consistently context-dependent, and broader application is constrained by data-quality problems including sampling variability, environmental noise, and population-dependent biases alongside methodological and governance barriers. The authors call for standardized collaborative frameworks, multimodal data fusion, mechanistic and digital-twin modelling with explicit uncertainty quantification, and transparent analytics to advance the field beyond its current narrow evidence base.

Developing an AI-Powered Occupational Health Surveillance System for Real-Time Detection and Management of Workplace Health Hazards

Cynthia Obianuju Ozobu. Developing an AI-Powered Occupational Health Surveillance System for Real-Time Detection and Management of Workplace Health Hazards. WORLD JOURNAL OF INNOVATION AND MODERN TECHNOLOGY. 2026

This paper addresses the limitations of traditional occupational health surveillance systems, which suffer from delayed hazard detection and poor adaptability to changing workplace conditions. The authors propose an AI-powered surveillance framework that integrates wearable devices and Internet of Things sensors to continuously collect data on both environmental factors and individual employee health metrics. Machine learning algorithms and predictive analytics are applied to these data streams to identify anomalies, recognize risk patterns, and trigger proactive interventions before hazards escalate. The system also includes a dashboard for visualizing health and safety insights, generating automated compliance documentation, and delivering recommendations to managers and workers. The authors claim the framework is scalable across industries and capable of improving workplace safety culture, reducing injuries, and enhancing employee well-being by bridging emerging technologies with occupational health practice.

Inferring variant-specific effective reproduction numbers from combined case and sequencing data

Marlin D Figgins, Trevor Bedford. Inferring variant-specific effective reproduction numbers from combined case and sequencing data. eLife. 2026

This paper addresses the challenge of accurately characterizing the transmission dynamics of co-circulating SARS-CoV-2 variants. The authors extended existing methods for estimating effective reproduction numbers from case count data to jointly incorporate genetic sequencing data, enabling simultaneous inference of variant-specific effective reproduction numbers and variant frequencies. Applying this framework to confirmed case and sequencing data across United States states over roughly fourteen months, they modeled structured relationships between reproduction numbers to estimate fixed variant-specific growth advantages that remain consistent across locations. The approach moves beyond analyses based solely on sequence frequency dynamics, which cannot fully capture epidemiological behavior, by linking variant prevalence directly to transmission intensity. Using this method, they estimated effective reproduction numbers and location-consistent growth advantages for multiple SARS-CoV-2 variants of concern and interest circulating in the United States during the study period.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the challenge of monitoring maritime activities by fusing two complementary data sources: the Automatic Identification System, which identifies vessels but has known vulnerabilities, and space-based radio frequency observations, which detect vessel activity independently but cannot identify ships on their own. Following a structured data science methodology, the researchers developed a Pair Hidden Markov Model to align individual radio frequency detections with AIS vessel trajectories by incorporating temporal and spatial information while accounting for noise and timing discrepancies. The model was evaluated on real AIS data combined with synthetic radio frequency detections, with performance benchmarked against nearest-neighbor baselines and analyzed for systematic biases related to trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and spatial separation. A log-odds scoring variant that compares the full model against a null model without match transitions substantially improved both association precision and the detection of vessels behaving suspiciously by suppressing their AIS signals. Despite these improvements, a time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline without any parameter fitting still outperformed the probabilistic model, indicating that the proposed single-detection Pair Hidden Markov Model formulation approaches but does not surpass simpler heuristic methods.

Polynomial-Time Solvability of the Multi-Dimensional Slope Change Problem

Apoorva Narula, Santanu S. Dey. Polynomial-Time Solvability of the Multi-Dimensional Slope Change Problem. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies the problem of detecting slope changes in multidimensional time series by fitting continuous piecewise-linear functions that share a single breakpoint, examining both a dense setting where all dimensions may change slope and a sparse setting where at most a fixed number of dimensions may do so. Despite the resulting optimization problems being nonconvex mixed-integer nonlinear programs that can yield irrational solutions even for rational inputs, the authors prove that all six problem variants across three common loss functions are exactly solvable in polynomial time. Efficient algorithms are derived for the least-squares case, with the dense variant requiring operations linear in the product of dimensions and time points and the sparse variant scaling with the square of the number of dimensions. These algorithms are implemented and benchmarked on both simulated and real-world datasets spanning climate, motion tracking, and engineering sensor domains, demonstrating that they solve large instances in well under a second on a laptop. Compared to a general-purpose mixed-integer nonlinear programming solver, the proposed algorithms are several orders of magnitude faster while consistently finding exact optimal solutions on instances where the solver struggles or fails to converge within time limits.

Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction

Abhilekha Dalal, Avishek Das, Samatha Ereshi Akkamahadevi. Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated how convolutional neural networks exploit contextual background information when classifying agricultural images, and whether the internal representations of hidden neurons can be made interpretable using a technique called Concept Induction. The researchers applied this approach across multiple agricultural datasets with varying degrees of contextual bias, examining several CNN architectures at different network depths and across different spatial patch locations within images. Quantitative visual descriptors were encoded in a knowledge graph and used as background knowledge to derive logical expressions characterizing when individual neurons activate, with these expressions then statistically validated on held-out images. The study found that neuron-feature relationships were most frequently validated at intermediate network depths, and that texture-related features such as edge, Gabor, and Tamura descriptors were most commonly linked to neuron activations. Notably, the degree of contextual bias in a dataset did not align with the rate of interpretable neuron-feature validation, suggesting that a network's behavioral reliance on background context and the semantic interpretability of its hidden neurons reflect distinct and separable aspects of CNN behavior.

Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection

Donghoon Lee, Jusung Park, Shinjin Kang. Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the problem of detecting out-of-distribution objects in real-world object detection systems, framing each detected bounding box decision as a hypothesis test where the null hypothesis assumes the box contains an in-distribution object. The authors identify that the null distribution of box features varies systematically with box size and border contact, motivating a conditioning strategy that compares each box only against in-distribution boxes sharing the same geometric properties, which yields distribution-free false-alarm rate bounds and reveals separations obscured by pooling across all box types. Because classification-oriented layers discard information about unknown objects, the method reads features from earlier in the network, specifically from the last backbone stage before class-specific processing. Building on these insights, the authors propose PLUM, a training-free scoring method that pools backbone features within a box and ranks their distance to in-distribution classes using the box-conditional reference set. PLUM achieves state-of-the-art out-of-distribution detection performance on standard benchmarks without requiring external models or additional training.

Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space

Chanasorn Kongprachaya, Nicola Bartolo, Daniele Bertacca et al.. Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether Redshift Space Distortions (RSD) can mimic parity violation signals in galaxy clustering statistics, specifically in the four-point correlation function (4PCF). The authors analyzed the perturbative relativistic galaxy number density using Standard Eulerian Perturbation Theory to third order, incorporating wide-angle effects, tidal bias, and an arbitrary cosmological background. By expanding the 4PCF in spherical harmonics, they demonstrated that angular momentum selection rules encoded in Wigner 3-j symbols force all parity-odd contributions from RSD to vanish when averaged over all line-of-sight orientations. This result holds both in the plane-parallel approximation and in the full wide-angle case, up to leading-order general relativistic corrections. The finding is significant for ongoing observational searches for cosmological parity violation, as it confirms that any detected parity-odd signal in isotropic 4PCF estimators cannot be attributed to RSD as a systematic artefact.

An All-Digital Coherent AFSK Demodulator for CubeSat Applications

Rubem Vasconcelos Pacelli, Rodrigo de Lima Florindo, Felix Antreich et al.. An All-Digital Coherent AFSK Demodulator for CubeSat Applications. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of coherent detection for audio frequency-shift keying (AFSK) modulation in CubeSat communications, where the severe line-of-sight dynamics of low Earth orbit typically make synchronization too difficult for practical use. The authors developed an all-digital coherent AFSK demodulator that combines a Kalman filter for carrier phase and timing synchronization with the Viterbi algorithm for maximum likelihood sequence detection, with detected bit statistics fed back to the Kalman filter to track phase shift, Doppler frequency shift, and Doppler drift. Original mathematical analyses were derived to characterize synchronization accuracy under dynamic low Earth orbit conditions, and the system was evaluated using simulations with real CubeSat orbital parameters over an additive white Gaussian noise channel. The proposed coherent demodulator achieved a substantial improvement in bit error rate performance compared to the conventional noncoherent AFSK approach, demonstrating that coherent detection is viable even under challenging orbital dynamics.

Rapid and Sensitive Detection of &lt;i&gt;Schistosoma mansoni&lt;/i&gt; in the Intermediate Snail Hosts Using Loop-Mediated Isothermal Amplification (LAMP) Diagnostics.

Li HM, Qin ZQ, Lv S et al.. Rapid and Sensitive Detection of &lt;i&gt;Schistosoma mansoni&lt;/i&gt; in the Intermediate Snail Hosts Using Loop-Mediated Isothermal Amplification (LAMP) Diagnostics.. Tropical medicine and infectious disease. 2026

The paper studied the detection of *Schistosoma mansoni* infections in its intermediate snail hosts, which is critical for controlling and eliminating schistosomiasis, a neglected tropical disease. The researchers developed a novel loop-mediated isothermal amplification assay targeting the mitochondrial NADH dehydrogenase subunit 1 gene of the parasite, optimizing it for a simple, single-temperature reaction, and evaluated its performance against conventional parasitological methods, standard PCR, and real-time PCR using both laboratory-infected snails and field-collected samples from two endemic countries. The assay achieved complete specificity and high sensitivity in laboratory conditions, outperforming microscopy and matching the performance of real-time PCR, while in field settings it detected a higher proportion of infected snails than conventional PCR and identified infections entirely missed by parasitological examination. These results demonstrate that the assay is a sensitive, specific, and practically deployable diagnostic tool suitable for routine surveillance of schistosomiasis transmission in snail populations.

Towards point-of-care diagnostics of neglected tropical diseases using nucleic acid amplification tests.

Priem N, Rodriguez-Mateos P, Waweru H et al.. Towards point-of-care diagnostics of neglected tropical diseases using nucleic acid amplification tests.. Analytical and bioanalytical chemistry. 2026

This paper reviewed the state of point-of-care nucleic acid amplification tests for neglected tropical diseases, a group of conditions that have historically received less diagnostic innovation than other major infectious diseases despite their considerable global burden. The authors conducted a systematic critical analysis of published studies, examining the full nucleic acid amplification workflow with an emphasis on operational feasibility and implementation barriers rather than purely analytical performance metrics. Common themes emerging from the literature included simplified extraction methods, integrated device platforms, smartphone-based result interpretation, and workflows designed to reduce dependence on cold chains and electricity. Despite these advances, the review identified persistent technical, analytical, and implementation obstacles that prevent most current developments from transitioning from proof-of-concept prototypes to genuinely field-deployable tools. The central finding is that the majority of nucleic acid amplification test developments for neglected tropical diseases do not yet meet the World Health Organization's criteria for point-of-care application in resource-limited settings, and that achieving elimination goals will require careful attention to every step from sample collection through to real-world deployment.

A LAMP-CRISPR/Cas12a Assay for Specific Detection of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; in Whole Blood: Analytical Performance and Clinical Evaluation in Suspected Bloodstream Infection.

Quyen DT, Thau NS, Trung PQ et al.. A LAMP-CRISPR/Cas12a Assay for Specific Detection of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; in Whole Blood: Analytical Performance and Clinical Evaluation in Suspected Bloodstream Infection.. Diagnostics (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This paper developed and evaluated a diagnostic assay combining loop-mediated isothermal amplification with CRISPR/Cas12a detection to identify *Escherichia coli* directly in whole blood, targeting the *dxs* gene. The assay was optimised and benchmarked against quantitative PCR and blood culture using both laboratory-spiked samples and clinical specimens from patients with suspected bloodstream infection. The method demonstrated complete inclusivity across tested *E. coli* isolates and full exclusivity against non-target organisms, with results readable within minutes of initiating the CRISPR detection step. Its limit of detection was modestly higher than that of quantitative PCR, and in the clinical evaluation it identified all patient samples at or above the quantitative PCR detection threshold while producing no false positives. Because bacteraemia typically involves extremely low bacterial loads, the assay's clinical sensitivity was insufficient for use as a rule-out test, but its specificity and speed make it appropriate as a rapid confirmatory tool used alongside blood culture.

Development and Application of a Dual-Readout RPA-PfAgo System for Rapid Detection of Streptococcus agalactiae in Bovine Milk

Xujie Zhao, Yuying Du, Qianlei Zhu et al.. Development and Application of a Dual-Readout RPA-PfAgo System for Rapid Detection of Streptococcus agalactiae in Bovine Milk. Veterinary Sciences. 2026

This study developed a rapid molecular detection platform for *Streptococcus agalactiae*, a major cause of bovine mastitis, by combining recombinase polymerase amplification (RPA) with the prokaryotic Argonaute protein PfAgo, targeting the conserved *cfb* gene. The researchers screened multiple primer pairs and guide DNAs to optimize the system, which was configured in two readout formats: a real-time fluorescence assay and a lateral flow dipstick assay. The fluorescence-based format achieved a lower detection limit than the dipstick format, and both demonstrated high analytical specificity with no cross-reactivity against non-target bacteria. Field validation using bovine milk samples from multiple dairy-farming regions in China showed that both RPA-PfAgo formats detected substantially more positive samples than bacteriological culture, while achieving complete concordance with a standardized quantitative PCR assay across all samples tested. The authors conclude that this dual-readout platform offers a rapid and reliable alternative to conventional methods for on-site detection of *S. agalactiae* in dairy settings.

Hantavirus Emergence in a Changing World: Virology, Pathogenesis, Surveillance, and One Health Preparedness
