Molecular engineering of CRISPR/Cas12a: from activity enhancement to exponential signal amplification

Qing-Nan Li, Qi-Fan Yang, Wei-Liang Jin et al.. Molecular engineering of CRISPR/Cas12a: from activity enhancement to exponential signal amplification. Chemical Science. 2026

This review examines strategies to improve the detection sensitivity of CRISPR/Cas12a-based molecular diagnostics by moving beyond its inherent linear signal output limitations. Rather than coupling Cas12a with external pre-amplification techniques, which add complexity and uncertainty to clinical workflows, the authors focus on molecular engineering approaches that intrinsically enhance the system's performance. The review systematically covers methods including crRNA reprogramming, activator strand engineering, reporter probe design, and reaction environment optimization as means to improve catalytic kinetics, specificity, and signal output. Together, these strategies are argued to shift the paradigm from the conventional "one-target-one-enzyme" model toward exponential signal amplification without pre-amplification steps. The authors propose that such integrated engineering approaches enable ultrasensitive detection of both nucleic acid and non-nucleic acid targets, supporting the development of field-deployable point-of-care diagnostics.

Evolving Approaches to Bacterial Identification: A Review of Classical and Modern Techniques

Ina Gajić, Milos Jovicevic, Dušan Kekić et al.. Evolving Approaches to Bacterial Identification: A Review of Classical and Modern Techniques. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review examines the landscape of bacterial identification methods in the context of rising antimicrobial resistance and the limitations of conventional diagnostic approaches. The authors surveyed techniques spanning classical methods such as microscopy, culture, and biochemical testing through to advanced proteomic, immunological, and molecular tools including various forms of polymerase chain reaction, whole-genome sequencing, and metagenomics. Emerging technologies such as CRISPR-based diagnostics and phage- and nanomaterial-based detection systems are also considered, alongside the integration of artificial intelligence to enhance analytical performance. The review finds that while the combination of these approaches promises improvements in accuracy, speed, and applicability across clinical, veterinary, agricultural, and environmental settings, variability in bioinformatics frameworks and algorithm-defined thresholds continues to hinder standardized implementation, particularly for whole-genome sequencing in routine laboratories. The authors conclude that technological modernization must be pursued cautiously, with standardization treated as a central priority.

Scan-specific k-space interpolation using deep Koopman autoencoders

Wassim Ben Salah, Jon Cleary, Sarah McElroy et al.. Scan-specific k-space interpolation using deep Koopman autoencoders. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper developed a scan-specific deep learning method for reconstructing accelerated MRI data through nonlinear k-space interpolation. The proposed approach uses a deep Koopman autoencoder architecture comprising an encoder, a linear interpolation layer, and a decoder, trained on autocalibration signals with a combined prediction and autoencoding loss. The method was evaluated against established techniques on a large set of retrospectively undersampled brain datasets using quantitative metrics and a clinical reader study assessing image quality attributes such as sharpness, noise, and aliasing. The proposed technique outperformed both GRAPPA and RAKI on quantitative measures, demonstrated greater resilience to noise amplification, and was preferred by clinical readers over RAKI in a forced-choice evaluation. The method also showed robustness to variations in key parameters such as autocalibration signal count, kernel size, and latent space dimensionality.

Extending an X-ray & Mid-Infrared diagnostic for absorption in Active Galactic Nuclei to higher redshifts using NuSTAR and XMM-Newton

Ryan J. Turney, Claire L. Greenwell, David M. Alexander. Extending an X-ray & Mid-Infrared diagnostic for absorption in Active Galactic Nuclei to higher redshifts using NuSTAR and XMM-Newton. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated methods for measuring the fraction of Compton-thick active galactic nuclei, which is key to understanding supermassive black hole growth and evolution. The researchers extended an existing column density estimation technique — based on the ratio of observed X-ray to mid-infrared luminosity — from the local universe to higher redshifts, using a sample of bright AGN drawn from a hard X-ray survey catalog with a median redshift around a third. They applied X-ray spectral fitting with physically motivated models to constrain column densities and calibrate the luminosity ratio diagnostic at these greater distances. The high-redshift relationship was found to be broadly consistent with the low-redshift version, with modest parameter differences attributable to higher X-ray luminosities in the higher-redshift sample rather than genuine evolutionary change. Applying the calibrated method across the full catalog, they derived an observed Compton-thick fraction consistent with recent independent measurements from other hard X-ray surveys of the local universe.

MamaReason: Unfinished Reasoning and Answer Recovery in Clinical Language Models

Thanni Adewuyi, Adepoju Jeremiah Moyondafoluwa, Dorcas Amapu et al.. MamaReason: Unfinished Reasoning and Answer Recovery in Clinical Language Models. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates a specific failure mode in clinical language models where incomplete reasoning traces already contain the correct diagnosis before an answer is explicitly produced. The authors developed MamaReason, a model adapted from a large dense language model, and evaluated it on a maternal and child health benchmark consisting of paired clinical presentations. They employed an explicit-commitment extractor to identify diagnoses within failed reasoning passes, finding that the extracted commitments frequently matched the final answer produced by continuation across both reinforcement learning and supervised fine-tuning conditions. Crucially, simply reading the committed diagnosis from within the unfinished trace — without generating additional tokens — recovered a substantial portion of the cases restored by full continuation, suggesting that diagnostic content is often present in the trace before completion occurs. The paper argues that clinical language model evaluation must distinguish between diagnostic content already present in a reasoning trace, completion of an accepted answer, and overall paired diagnostic correctness, as these represent meaningfully different outcomes that only become separable when the same trace is examined through multiple readout strategies.

Compressible gas flow through porous media under forced pressure and fluxes: compressibility number and pressure curvature

A. F. Visentini, J. J. Hidalgo, M. Dentz. Compressible gas flow through porous media under forced pressure and fluxes: compressibility number and pressure curvature. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how gas compressibility shapes pressure evolution and transient behavior during flow through porous media under different types of boundary forcing. The authors analyze isothermal gas flow using the porous medium equation, nondimensionalized with a Péclet-type compressibility number that characterizes the degree of steady-state compression and allows direct comparison across inlet conditions controlling pressure, volumetric flux, or mass flux. They derive a semi-analytical perturbation series valid across all three forcing conditions and confirm it with numerical solutions spanning several orders of magnitude in the compressibility number, finding that the effective pressure-squared diffusivity transitions from boundary-condition-dependent plateau values at low compression to a power-law scaling at high compression, explained by the slowest eigenmode of the linearized system. To capture localized flow behavior, the authors introduce the pressure curvature and describe its dynamics through a nonlinear advection-diffusion-reaction equation, revealing that compression pulses migrate downstream and dissipate during relaxation, with curvature moments exposing a compressibility-dependent crossover between compression- and expansion-dominated transients. The framework is illustrated with applications to gas chromatography and underground hydrogen storage.

Positive Weights Do Not Certify TWFE Inference: A Design Diagnostic under Treatment-Effect Heterogeneity

Stanisław M. S. Halkiewicz. Positive Weights Do Not Certify TWFE Inference: A Design Diagnostic under Treatment-Effect Heterogeneity. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper examines whether positive treatment weights in two-way fixed-effects (TWFE) regressions are sufficient to guarantee valid inference under treatment-effect heterogeneity, arguing they are not. The authors develop a design-only diagnostic that quantifies the worst-case mean shift in the TWFE test statistic arising from local treatment-effect drift, expressed as a function of the discrepancy between actual and uniform treatment weights. To perform inference on this diagnostic, they propose two regular one-sided procedures—a reverse-triangle multiplier lower bound and a covariance-aware noncentral chi-squared inversion—that can either detect unsafe configurations or certify adequacy, since a debiased quadratic estimator of the shift is nonregular at zero. Applying the framework to several well-known empirical panels, they find that conventional inference remains uncertified even in cases where all treated-cell weights are positive, with the minimum-wage and property-tax applications failing certification while the castle-doctrine application passes under a restricted heterogeneity class. The diagnostic thus provides a formal basis for separating point correction, exposure uniformity, and statistically warranted certification of TWFE inference.

KnowVis: Dual-View Evaluation and Diagnosis of World-Knowledge Grounding in Text-to-Image Models

Yonghang Yu, Ruoyu Wang, Haoyu Zhang et al.. KnowVis: Dual-View Evaluation and Diagnosis of World-Knowledge Grounding in Text-to-Image Models. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the limitation that text-to-image models, despite advances in visual realism and instruction following, struggle to ground implicit world knowledge, structured facts, and domain-specific commonsense in generated images. The authors developed KnowVis, a dual-view evaluation and diagnosis framework built on a hierarchical knowledge taxonomy with case-level annotations, combining two complementary benchmarks with a multimodal large language model-based visual question answering judge protocol. One benchmark supports fine-grained atomic knowledge evaluation through structured question answering, while the other examines open-ended knowledge composition using a combination of required, optional, and dynamically discovered knowledge expressions alongside human-judge collaboration. Experiments using KnowVis revealed substantial gaps in world-knowledge grounding across existing text-to-image models and demonstrated that the framework provides structured diagnostic signals capable of attributing errors to specific knowledge categories.

Proteomics Reveals Novel Biomarkers in the Blood for Sepsis Diagnosis.

Fan Z, Chen M, Sun Y et al.. Proteomics Reveals Novel Biomarkers in the Blood for Sepsis Diagnosis.. Journal of proteome research. 2026

This study addressed the critical need for early and accurate sepsis diagnosis in critically ill patients by using a multi-phase proteomics approach to discover and validate blood-based biomarkers. The researchers combined data-independent acquisition proteomics, targeted mass spectrometry verification, ELISA-based clinical confirmation, and machine learning across discovery, verification, and prospective cohorts. Circulating calprotectin and S100A8/A9-related biomarkers for diagnosis and prognostic stratification in adult acute infection and sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.

Liao YS, Deng YP, Song JL et al.. Circulating calprotectin and S100A8/A9-related biomarkers for diagnosis and prognostic stratification in adult acute infection and sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.. Frontiers in cellular and infection microbiology. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis examined whether circulating calprotectin and related S100A8/A9 biomarkers can accurately diagnose acute infection and sepsis and stratify prognosis in adults. The researchers searched multiple biomedical databases and trial registries, pooling data from independent cohorts using random-effects models to estimate diagnostic and prognostic areas under the receiver operating characteristic curve, alongside bivariate analyses of sensitivity and specificity, with evidence quality assessed using established appraisal tools. Across the included cohorts, calprotectin and S100A8/A9 markers demonstrated only moderate overall diagnostic performance for both sepsis and infection-related outcomes, with considerable variability in sensitivity and specificity depending on the clinical context. Prognostic accuracy for mortality and survival was similarly moderate, and paired comparisons with other biomarkers did not establish clear superiority for calprotectin-based measures. The authors conclude that these biomarkers may serve as adjunctive indicators of host immune response in selected clinical contexts but that current evidence, rated as very low certainty, does not support their use as stand-alone diagnostic tools, universal decision thresholds, or replacements for established biomarkers and clinical evaluation.

Procalcitonin as a Stewardship Checkpoint, Not an Antibiotic Gatekeeper: From Antibiotic Initiation to Discontinuation in Resource-Limited Hospitals.

Abdi DA, Yusuf AA, Hirsi AM et al.. Procalcitonin as a Stewardship Checkpoint, Not an Antibiotic Gatekeeper: From Antibiotic Initiation to Discontinuation in Resource-Limited Hospitals.. Infection and drug resistance. 2026

This paper examines the clinical role of procalcitonin as a biomarker within antimicrobial stewardship programs, with particular attention to how its utility varies depending on where it is applied along the antibiotic treatment pathway. The authors reviewed evidence on procalcitonin's performance both at the point of antibiotic initiation and during ongoing treatment, finding that its value for safely withholding antibiotics at the outset is limited, especially in patients with suspected sepsis or physiological instability. By contrast, serial procalcitonin measurements show more consistent benefit after empirical therapy has begun, supporting de-escalation, shorter treatment courses, and earlier discontinuation. The paper further argues that in resource-limited hospitals, introducing procalcitonin without the infrastructure for timely serial testing and structured antibiotic review may increase costs without meaningfully improving antibiotic use. The authors therefore propose that procalcitonin be used selectively as a reassessment checkpoint to guide discontinuation decisions in patients already receiving empirical antibiotics, rather than as a standalone gatekeeper for initiating treatment.

Citrullinated Histone H3 and Myeloperoxidase are Associated with Sepsis-Induced Coagulopathy (SIC) in Sepsis Patients but Fail to Predict 28-Day Mortality Among SIC Patients.

Lai H, Zhong A, Liao W et al.. Citrullinated Histone H3 and Myeloperoxidase are Associated with Sepsis-Induced Coagulopathy (SIC) in Sepsis Patients but Fail to Predict 28-Day Mortality Among SIC Patients.. International journal of general medicine. 2026

This study investigated whether two neutrophil extracellular trap (NET)-related biomarkers, citrullinated histone H3 and myeloperoxidase, could serve as clinically useful markers for sepsis-induced coagulopathy and subsequent mortality in sepsis patients. Using a single-center retrospective design, the researchers measured plasma levels of both biomarkers at hospital admission in adult sepsis patients, then assessed their association with coagulopathy occurrence and twenty-eight-day all-cause mortality. Both biomarkers were significantly elevated in patients who developed sepsis-induced coagulopathy compared to those who did not, and each independently predicted coagulopathy occurrence after adjusting for confounders, with moderate diagnostic accuracy. However, among patients who had already developed sepsis-induced coagulopathy, neither biomarker differed meaningfully between survivors and non-survivors, and neither independently predicted twenty-eight-day mortality. The findings suggest that circulating citrullinated histone H3 and myeloperoxidase reflect the inflammatory-coagulation crosstalk underlying sepsis-induced coagulopathy but lack prognostic utility for mortality once coagulopathy is established.

Fifth Universal Definition of Myocardial Infarction (2026)

Nicholas L. Mills, Laura Kristin Newby, Sarah J. Zaman et al.. Fifth Universal Definition of Myocardial Infarction (2026). Journal of the American College of Cardiology. 2026

The Fifth Universal Definition of Myocardial Infarction is a consensus document that updates the clinical and pathophysiological framework for defining, classifying, and diagnosing myocardial infarction. The document introduces a revised three-part clinical classification that distinguishes primary myocardial infarction arising from acute coronary pathology, secondary myocardial infarction resulting from myocardial supply-demand imbalance, and procedure-related myocardial infarction occurring as a complication of cardiac intervention or surgery. Updated diagnostic criteria incorporate cardiac troponin testing, electrocardiographic findings, and coronary or cardiac imaging, with refined thresholds for defining acute and chronic myocardial injury based on the ninety-ninth percentile upper reference limit. The classification is aligned with International Classification of Diseases coding and includes adapted diagnostic pathways for low-resource settings where biomarker testing and imaging may not be available. The document also addresses special clinical situations, implications for public health and research, and provides guidance on silent or unrecognized myocardial infarction, unstable angina, and myocardial injury with non-obstructive coronary arteries.

Quantum–Photonic Topological Intelligence for Multiscale Biomedicine: Brain Networks, Biosignals, Spatial Multi-Omics and Autonomous Therapeutics

Murali Krishna Pasupuleti. Quantum–Photonic Topological Intelligence for Multiscale Biomedicine: Brain Networks, Biosignals, Spatial Multi-Omics and Autonomous Therapeutics. International Journal of Academic and Industrial Research Innovations(IJAIRI). 2026

This paper proposes a theoretical framework called Quantum–Photonic Topological Intelligence for Multiscale Biomedicine, designed to address the challenge of integrating heterogeneous biomedical data spanning multiple biological scales, including brain network dynamics, physiological signals, spatially resolved molecular measurements, and treatment response data. The authors developed a unified mathematical and conceptual architecture combining graph and simplicial representations, persistent topological descriptors, multimodal learning, hybrid quantum–photonic feature maps, and constrained therapeutic optimization across four coupled layers. The framework is entirely model-based and uses an illustrative numerical scoring scheme rather than real patient data, and explicitly does not claim empirical clinical superiority. The analysis suggests that the framework's value lies in disciplined multiscale coupling, whereby persistent topological representations, cross-modal fusion connecting electrophysiology with spatial molecular states, and governed optimization can together support constrained therapeutic decision-making. The authors call for future empirical work benchmarking the framework's components against matched datasets, prospective validation, fairness constraints, and clinically meaningful endpoints before any deployment claims are made.

Detection of Chagas disease from the ECG: the George B. Moody PhysioNet Challenge 2025

Matthew A. Reyna, Soheil Saghafi, Zuzana Koscova et al.. Detection of Chagas disease from the ECG: the George B. Moody PhysioNet Challenge 2025. Physiological Measurement. 2026

The paper describes a machine learning challenge focused on detecting Chagas disease, a parasitic infection causing cardiomyopathy, using electrocardiogram data. Teams were invited to develop algorithms that could identify likely Chagas disease cases from a large collection of ECGs drawn from multiple Brazilian databases, framing the problem as a triage task aligned with regional serological testing capacity. The evaluation metric measured the true positive rate among the patients ranked most likely to have the disease by each algorithm, reflecting realistic clinical constraints. Key innovations included combining datasets with both weak and strong labels, data augmentation for generalizability, and development of an ensemble model that aggregated top-performing submissions. The challenge attracted a large international field of participants, and results showed that both individual top models and the ensemble approach achieved meaningful sensitivity at clinically relevant operating points, though performance varied across datasets.

Objective Bayes Factor Based Model Selection for Zero-Inflated Microbiome Count Data Under Jeffreys Prior

Paramahansa Pramanik, Arnab K. Maity. Objective Bayes Factor Based Model Selection for Zero-Inflated Microbiome Count Data Under Jeffreys Prior. Statistics in Biopharmaceutical Research. 2026

This paper addresses the challenge of selecting appropriate statistical models for microbiome count data, which is characterized by excess zeros and overdispersion arising from the complex relationships between microbial communities and disease, diet, genetics, and environmental factors. The authors developed a Bayes factor approach for model selection among negative binomial, Poisson, zero-inflated negative binomial, and zero-inflated Poisson distributions, using non-informative Jeffreys prior to maintain objectivity. The proposed method was evaluated through simulation studies and real data analysis, where it was compared against traditional model selection criteria including the Akaike information criterion and Vuong's test. The Bayes factor approach was found to outperform these conventional methods in both settings, suggesting it provides more reliable discrimination between competing count data models for microbiome applications. The authors also released a dedicated R package to make the methodology accessible for broader use in Bayesian model selection for zero-inflated microbiome data.

Nanopore direct RNA sequencing and the epitranscriptome: Advances in mapping native RNA landscapes

Tianyuan Zhang, Jia Li, Chao Tang et al.. Nanopore direct RNA sequencing and the epitranscriptome: Advances in mapping native RNA landscapes. iMeta. 2026

This review examines nanopore direct RNA sequencing as a tool for studying the transcriptome and epitranscriptome, focusing on its capacity to analyze native RNA molecules without reverse transcription or amplification. The authors outline the biophysical principles of the technology and trace its development, comparing it against short-read RNA sequencing, long-read cDNA sequencing, and conventional RNA modification mapping approaches. A key emphasis is placed on the technology's ability to simultaneously capture RNA modifications, full-length transcript structures, alternative splicing, and poly-adenylation tail features within single molecules, offering an integrated regulatory view unavailable to competing methods. The review also surveys optimized experimental workflows for diverse RNA biotypes, state-of-the-art computational algorithms for modification detection and isoform quantification, and analytical pipelines for integrating multiple regulatory layers, while stressing the importance of benchmarking and orthogonal validation. Emerging applications discussed include resolving complex transcriptomes, characterizing coordinated epitranscriptomic regulation, identifying disease-associated RNA signatures, and future deployment in precision medicine and broader biological research.

Benchmarking AI Agents for Addressing Scientific Challenges Across Scales

Tianyu Liu, A F Wang, Antonia Panescu et al.. Benchmarking AI Agents for Addressing Scientific Challenges Across Scales. arXiv (Cornell University). 2026

This paper addresses the gap between the growing use of AI agents for scientific discovery and the lack of realistic benchmarks for evaluating their capabilities. The authors developed SciAgentArena, a benchmark comprising approximately two hundred tasks drawn from real-world scientific research scenarios across multiple domains, featuring stepwise verification and an interactive environment compatible with diverse AI agents. Evaluating current agents with this framework revealed that they perform reasonably well on structured data-analysis workflows with clear task definitions and evaluation criteria, but struggle with generating novel insights, sustaining self-directed exploration, and tackling open-ended research questions. The study also characterizes recurring failure modes and identifies directions for improving agent reliability, autonomy, and scientific reasoning. Overall, SciAgentArena is proposed as a practical framework for tracking progress in AI agents for science and guiding the development of more capable future systems.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of monitoring maritime activity by fusing two complementary data sources: the Automatic Identification System, which provides vessel identification but has known vulnerabilities including intentional manipulation, and space-based radio frequency observations, which detect vessel activity independently but cannot identify ships on their own. Following a structured data science methodology, the authors developed a Pair Hidden Markov Model to align individual radio frequency detections with AIS vessel trajectories by jointly modeling temporal and spatial information along with noise and timing discrepancies. The approach was evaluated on real AIS data combined with synthetic radio frequency detections, benchmarked against nearest-neighbor baselines, and analyzed for systematic biases related to trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and the spatial distance between the two data sources. A log-odds formulation that contrasts the full matching model against a null model substantially improved precision and the ability to detect vessels behaving covertly, though a simple time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline without any model fitting still outperformed the probabilistic approach. The study concludes that the single-detection Pair Hidden Markov Model formulation approaches but does not surpass the hand-designed baseline, while identifying remaining biases that future work would need to address.

Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data

Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri. Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether recent hints of non-standard cosmological expansion are accompanied by modifications to how gravity behaves, going beyond a simple dark energy equation-of-state analysis. The authors perform a joint, non-parametric reconstruction of the effective dark energy density and two gravitational response functions governing matter clustering and lensing, using a combination of CMB, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation, supernova, and large-scale structure data. A persistent feature emerges in the dark energy density at intermediate redshifts, corresponding to a crossing of the phantom divide, and this feature is robust across different data combinations and whether or not gravitational modifications are allowed. The reconstructed gravitational response functions show suggestive but less significant enhancements over a similar redshift range, though including large-scale structure data shifts the lensing response toward values below the standard expectation. The authors conclude that current departures from the standard cosmological model are most clearly manifested in the expansion history, while correlated modifications to gravitational dynamics remain possible but await confirmation from next-generation surveys.

Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction

Abhilekha Dalal, Avishek Das, Samatha Ereshi Akkamahadevi. Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how convolutional neural networks exploit background contextual information when classifying agricultural images, and whether a technique called Concept Induction can reveal what hidden neurons represent when processing such context-dominated patches. The researchers applied this approach across five agricultural datasets exhibiting varying degrees of contextual bias, examining four CNN architectures at multiple network depths and patch locations, with image descriptors encoded in a knowledge graph serving as background knowledge for deriving logical characterizations of neuron activations. They found that roughly a quarter of tested neuron-feature pairs were statistically validated, with validation occurring most frequently at intermediate network depths and being most strongly associated with edge, Gabor, and Tamura texture features. Notably, the degree of contextual bias in a dataset did not predict how interpretable its hidden-neuron activations were, as a moderately biased dataset yielded the highest validation rate while the most biased dataset yielded the lowest. The authors conclude that behavioral context dependence and the semantic interpretability of hidden neurons reflect distinct aspects of CNN behavior, and that Concept Induction offers a complementary lens for understanding bias beyond classification accuracy alone.

KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids

Ivan Kuznetsov, Alexey Androsov, Dmitrii Pantiukhin et al.. KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces KELP, a Lagrangian particle tracking tool designed to follow virtual particles through ocean model velocity fields in order to study transport pathways, connectivity, and dispersion. The software supports unstructured ocean meshes with triangular, quadrilateral, and mixed elements, as well as HEALPix equal-area grids used by kilometre-scale digital twin ocean models, and computes both two- and three-dimensional trajectories. A central design goal is compatibility with coding agents driven by language models, and the paper demonstrates that structured task instructions called skills substantially improved the completeness of study setups generated by multiple language models. KELP's results were validated against two independent trackers and trajectories computed on HEALPix grids were shown to converge toward those on the native model mesh as pixel resolution increased, supporting the use of HEALPix output for ensemble Lagrangian statistics when the original mesh is unavailable. The tool demonstrated practical scalability through a multi-decade simulation tracking millions of particles in the Beaufort Gyre and a stress test involving tens of billions of particles on a high-resolution HEALPix mesh.

Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection

Donghoon Lee, Jusung Park, Shinjin Kang. Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the problem of detecting out-of-distribution objects in real-world object detection scenarios, framing each detected bounding box decision as a hypothesis test where the null hypothesis assumes the box contains an in-distribution object. The authors identify that the null distribution over box scores varies meaningfully with box size and contact with image borders, and that conventional approaches obscure this by pooling all boxes together. To exploit this structure, they propose PLUM, a training-free scoring method that extracts features from the last backbone stage before class-specific layers, then ranks a box's distance to in-distribution classes only against boxes sharing the same size and border-contact properties. This conditioning provides distribution-free false-alarm rate bounds per box stratum and preserves feature information that class-specific layers would otherwise discard. The method achieves leading out-of-distribution detection performance on standard benchmarks without requiring external models or additional training.

On numerical issues of divergences between nearly equal distributions

Brandon Amos. On numerical issues of divergences between nearly equal distributions. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates numerical instability problems that arise when computing divergences between categorical distributions that are nearly identical. Building on prior work identifying similar issues in softmax and soft cross-entropy operations, the authors demonstrate that forward and reverse Kullback-Leibler divergence, generalized Jensen-Shannon divergence, Monte Carlo KL estimates, and entropy all suffer from rounding and cancellation errors under these conditions, affecting both forward computations and gradient calculations. These instabilities have practical consequences in machine learning workflows that rely on divergences as optimization objectives, including knowledge distillation, reinforcement learning, and proximal optimization methods. The authors derive numerically stable reformulations for each case and provide concrete implementations validated through property-based testing.

Off-panel resistance determinants in a high-resistance setting: comparative evaluation of syndromic multiplex PCR, metagenomic sequencing, and phenotypic AST in bloodstream infections.

Nitayanon P, Phoonthong S, Peizner MT et al.. Off-panel resistance determinants in a high-resistance setting: comparative evaluation of syndromic multiplex PCR, metagenomic sequencing, and phenotypic AST in bloodstream infections.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

This study examined the diagnostic performance of three approaches for evaluating bloodstream infections in a setting with high antimicrobial resistance burden: a commercial syndromic multiplex PCR panel, metagenomic next-generation sequencing performed directly from positive blood cultures, and conventional phenotypic antimicrobial susceptibility testing. The researchers retrospectively compared these methods across a large series of positive blood-culture episodes from patients at a tertiary-care center. Metagenomic sequencing recovered the full set of organisms identified by routine culture more completely than the multiplex panel and detected off-panel carbapenem resistance genes, particularly an oxacillinase variant in *Acinetobacter baumannii*, that accounted for false-susceptible results on the targeted panel. Neither platform demonstrated statistically significant superiority for predicting meropenem or ceftriaxone resistance, and broader genomic detection did not consistently translate into improved phenotypic resistance prediction. The authors conclude that syndromic panels and metagenomic sequencing have complementary strengths in organism and resistance-mechanism coverage, while phenotypic susceptibility testing remains indispensable for definitive clinical guidance.

Pathogen identification and resistance gene expression tests using ASTar remnant bacterial suspension in gram-negative contrived positive blood cultures.

Gupta V, Myers M, Niklasson I et al.. Pathogen identification and resistance gene expression tests using ASTar remnant bacterial suspension in gram-negative contrived positive blood cultures.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

This study evaluated whether remnant bacterial suspension produced during routine antimicrobial susceptibility testing on the ASTar System could be repurposed for simultaneous pathogen identification and resistance gene detection, thereby streamlining diagnostic workflows for bloodstream infections. Gram-negative bacterial strains from multiple curated reference collections were contrived into blood culture bottles, processed through the ASTar System, and the remnant suspensions were analyzed using mass spectrometry for identification and lateral flow assays for detection of clinically important resistance mechanisms including extended-spectrum beta-lactamases and carbapenemases. All samples yielded high-confidence mass spectrometry identification scores, and lateral flow assay results showed complete or near-complete agreement with reference isolate characterization for all targeted resistance markers. Reflex testing workflows triggered by ASTar phenotypic results did not compromise lateral flow assay performance, and the approach could deliver identification and resistance detection results within approximately one to two hours of instrument initiation. The authors conclude that this integrated approach reduces duplicate sample handling, may enable earlier susceptibility reporting compared to current fragmented workflows, and offers potential cost savings relative to commercial multiplex polymerase chain reaction platforms.

From empirical to targeted therapy: A comprehensive review of clinical microbiological diagnostics for urinary tract infections.

Kim JA, Kim JH. From empirical to targeted therapy: A comprehensive review of clinical microbiological diagnostics for urinary tract infections.. Investigative and clinical urology. 2026

This review examines the full clinical microbiological diagnostic workflow for urinary tract infections, a globally prevalent bacterial infection increasingly complicated by antimicrobial resistance and high recurrence. The authors systematically describe the sequential steps from specimen collection and traditional culture-based methods through modern identification technologies such as mass spectrometry and multilocus sequence typing. They also survey the range of antimicrobial susceptibility testing approaches, from broth microdilution and automated systems to molecular methods including multiplex polymerase chain reaction and next-generation sequencing for genotypic resistance detection. The review argues that combining conventional phenotypic testing with rapid molecular diagnostics creates a practical clinical framework for accelerating the shift from empirical to targeted antibiotic therapy. The authors conclude that integrating genotypic and phenotypic data, alongside systematic diagnostic pipelines, strengthens antimicrobial stewardship programs and supports precision medicine in managing urinary tract infections amid rising multidrug resistance.

Endolysins for precision antimicrobial therapy and rapid bacterial detection: translational challenges and future directions.

Khan FM, Zourob M. Endolysins for precision antimicrobial therapy and rapid bacterial detection: translational challenges and future directions.. Frontiers in pharmacology. 2026

This review examines bacteriophage-derived endolysins as dual-purpose tools to address the global challenge of antimicrobial resistance, covering both their therapeutic potential and their utility in rapid bacterial detection. The authors synthesize current knowledge on how these peptidoglycan-degrading enzymes achieve bacteriolytic activity with high target specificity, and how derived structural domains such as cell wall-binding domains and receptor-binding proteins can serve as precise recognition molecules in clinical and food safety diagnostics. The review identifies key translational obstacles, including difficulties in protein expression, stability, formulation, and large-scale production, with particular emphasis on how the outer membrane of Gram-negative bacteria restricts endolysin access to its peptidoglycan target. Recent progress in protein engineering, membrane-permeabilization strategies, and novel delivery systems is discussed as promising means of overcoming these barriers. The authors conclude that engineered endolysins hold considerable future promise as precision antimicrobials and diagnostic agents against resistant bacterial pathogens, provided remaining translational challenges can be resolved.

Integrated evaluation of rapid diagnostic testing, genotypic-phenotypic resistance profiling, and AI-Assisted prediction models for antimicrobial stewardship and clinical outcomes in a resource-limited setting.

Hammad M, Arif R, Fardoos S et al.. Integrated evaluation of rapid diagnostic testing, genotypic-phenotypic resistance profiling, and AI-Assisted prediction models for antimicrobial stewardship and clinical outcomes in a resource-limited setting.. PloS one. 2026

This prospective observational study examined whether combining rapid diagnostic testing, molecular resistance gene profiling, and an artificial intelligence prediction model could improve antimicrobial stewardship and patient outcomes compared to conventional antibiotic susceptibility testing alone. Conducted at a hospital in Pakistan, the study enrolled adult patients with suspected bacterial infections and measured outcomes including time to effective therapy, antibiotic appropriateness, hospital length of stay, and mortality. The integrated approach dramatically shortened the time to pathogen identification and to initiation of effective therapy, and substantially increased the proportion of patients receiving appropriate antibiotics while reducing broad-spectrum antibiotic use. These improvements were accompanied by shorter hospital stays and lower mortality rates in patients managed with the integrated strategy. The AI model showed strong discriminative performance, and genotypic and phenotypic resistance profiles showed substantial concordance, supporting the potential of this combined approach as a scalable, cost-effective strategy for combating antimicrobial resistance in resource-limited settings.

Distributional Policy Evaluation for Heterogeneous Treatment Effects, Social Equity and Climate-Transition Governance

Murali Krishna Pasupuleti. Distributional Policy Evaluation for Heterogeneous Treatment Effects, Social Equity and Climate-Transition Governance. International Journal of Academic and Industrial Research Innovations(IJAIRI). 2026

This paper addresses a fundamental limitation in climate policy evaluation: relying on average treatment effects can obscure how the burdens and benefits of climate-transition policies are distributed across vulnerable populations, carbon-dependent workers, and low-income households. The authors develop a comprehensive framework called D-PECT that integrates heterogeneous treatment effects, quantile and distributional methods, doubly robust causal learning, and equity-sensitive welfare optimization alongside emissions effectiveness and governance quality indicators. The framework is formalized through potential-outcome equations, dynamic transition-state models, and a constrained social-welfare optimization with a risk-adjusted master objective. Using a pedagogical numerical demonstration, the authors show that an adaptive, targeted policy package scores notably higher on an integrated resilience metric than an untargeted benchmark, and that policy rankings can reverse entirely when the evaluation objective shifts from average welfare to lower-tail protection. The paper concludes that compensation schemes gain credibility when tied to estimated exposure and treatment response rather than income alone, and offers the framework as a tool for governments and institutions seeking climate action that is causally rigorous, distributionally transparent, and institutionally governable.

MedicalAgentsBench for complex medical reasoning: Comparing internalized reasoning models versus externalized agent-based frameworks

Yanjun Shao, Xiangru Tang, Jiwoong Sohn et al.. MedicalAgentsBench for complex medical reasoning: Comparing internalized reasoning models versus externalized agent-based frameworks. Patterns. 2026

This paper investigated whether internalized reasoning models and externalized agent-based frameworks represent competing or complementary approaches to complex medical reasoning tasks. The researchers developed MedicalAgentsBench, a curated benchmark of challenging clinical questions drawn from multiple medical datasets using difficulty-aware selection and contamination screening. They systematically evaluated a range of internalized reasoning models, standard base models, and externalized multi-agent scaffolding methods across this benchmark. The key finding is that both approaches independently improve performance and their benefits are additive, with the best results achieved by combining agent workflows on top of an internalized reasoning model. Additionally, Pareto analysis revealed that this combined approach dominates on the cost-performance frontier, while lightweight optimization on less expensive models provides a viable path for resource-limited settings.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

This review examines how the integration of multiomics technologies and artificial intelligence is reshaping precision drug discovery and clinical translation. Generative AI-enabled clinical decision support system in primary care: a pragmatic, cluster-randomized trial

Ambrose Agweyu, Paul Mwaniki, Vaishnavi Menon et al.. Generative AI-enabled clinical decision support system in primary care: a pragmatic, cluster-randomized trial. Nature Medicine. 2026

This study investigated whether a large language model integrated into an electronic medical record system could improve clinical outcomes in real-world primary care settings in Kenya. Using a pragmatic cluster-randomized trial design, clinical officers at primary care facilities were assigned to either use the LLM-assisted decision support tool or standard electronic medical records, with the primary outcome being expert-adjudicated treatment failure within fourteen days of patient enrollment. Nearly ten thousand patients were enrolled across the two arms, making this one of the larger real-world trials of generative AI clinical decision support in a low-resource context. Treatment failure rates were similar between the intervention and control groups, with the difference not reaching statistical significance, and no safety signals were identified. The investigators concluded that while LLM assistance appeared safe, it did not significantly reduce treatment failures, and any clinical benefit, if it exists, is likely to be modest.

Antimicrobial-laden PerOssal beads display in vitro inhibition and killing of extensively drug-resistant Gram-negative bacteria associated with conflict wounds in Ukraine

Henry Adam Claireaux, Edward J. A. Douglas, Scott J C Pallett et al.. Antimicrobial-laden PerOssal beads display in vitro inhibition and killing of extensively drug-resistant Gram-negative bacteria associated with conflict wounds in Ukraine. Bone and Joint Research. 2026

This study investigated the potential of locally delivered antimicrobials to combat extensively drug-resistant Gram-negative bacteria commonly found in conflict-related wounds, particularly open fractures from the war in Ukraine. The researchers tested antimicrobial-laden PerOssal hydroxyapatite and calcium sulphate beads against clinical bacterial isolates using zone-of-inhibition testing and time-kill analysis. The bacterial isolates showed resistance to multiple standard antimicrobials including gentamicin, tobramycin, and ciprofloxacin at concentrations exceeding established susceptibility breakpoints, and beads loaded with single agents such as gentamicin or cefazolin showed only weak inhibitory activity. However, beads combining gentamicin with rifampicin achieved consistent broad-spectrum bacterial inhibition and killing, and co-amoxiclav-loaded beads also inhibited all tested strains. The findings suggest that local antimicrobial delivery via these beads may help reduce infection risk in heavily contaminated open fractures when surgical wound excision is delayed, with potential applications in both conflict and civilian disaster settings, though in vivo validation is still needed.

Quantum Efficiency Measurements of a Low-Cost, Commercial CMOS Sensor in the Soft X-ray Bandpass

Casey T. DeRoo, Mallory Weber, Carson Bantz et al.. Quantum Efficiency Measurements of a Low-Cost, Commercial CMOS Sensor in the Soft X-ray Bandpass. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated the suitability of a commercially available, back-illuminated CMOS image sensor for use in space-based soft X-ray observatories. The researchers measured the quantum efficiency of the sensor across the soft X-ray bandpass using a synchrotron beamline facility, supplementing these measurements with microscopy of the sensor's physical layer structure to constrain a quantum efficiency model. The sensor was found to achieve quantum efficiency in the range of roughly ten to forty percent across the tested energy band. The results affirm this low-cost sensor's potential for deployment in affordable X-ray observatories, while also identifying design improvements — specifically the removal of microlenses and the use of deeper silicon depletion layers — that would enhance performance in future commercial X-ray CMOS sensors.

Deterministic ECDSA on secp256k1: the RFC 6979 assumption, a counterexample, and the state of deployed implementations

Ilia Prosianoi. Deterministic ECDSA on secp256k1: the RFC 6979 assumption, a counterexample, and the state of deployed implementations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examines the security foundations of RFC 6979, the standard for deterministic ECDSA nonce generation, which derives the signing nonce by keying HMAC-DRBG with the private signing key. The authors isolate the precise security assumption required, which they call A*, showing it is distinct from and not equivalent to standard pseudorandom function security or auxiliary-input PRF security, and they construct explicit counterexamples demonstrating these separations in both directions. They prove that A* follows tightly from the discrete logarithm problem when an ideal compression function is assumed and a randomized signing oracle is available, and they show that using a nonce key independent of the private key would eliminate the assumption entirely. An empirical audit of secp256k1 implementations reveals that the vast majority sign deterministically without added entropy, and that a deviation from the specification present in an early version of libsecp256k1 persists in several actively used cryptocurrency libraries. Additional findings include quantitative bounds on how close the HMAC input block is to uniformly distributed across several standard curves, unconditional bounds on the Fourier coefficients of the low-R grinding nonce set, and test vectors for an undertested branch of the nonce generation procedure.

Convergence rate of observables in the Marcus-Lushnikov process toward Smoluchowski with singular homogeneous kernels

R{é}mi Catellier, Sebastian Baudelet-Aponte Garcia. Convergence rate of observables in the Marcus-Lushnikov process toward Smoluchowski with singular homogeneous kernels. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies how well the Marcus-Lushnikov stochastic particle system approximates the deterministic Smoluchowski coagulation equation when the coagulation kernel is singular at the origin, specifically for the family of kernels that blow up as particle size approaches zero. For regular mean-field systems, weak errors for smooth observables of the empirical measure are generically expected to be of order one over the number of particles, and the central question is whether this favorable scaling survives when the kernel singularity creates analytical difficulties near zero cluster size. The authors prove that the weak error of order one over particle number is indeed preserved on finite time intervals for a suitable class of nonlinear functionals of the empirical measure. The key insight is that the singularity is self-regularizing: small clusters coagulate at very high rates, causing rapid escape from the dangerous region near zero and generating exponential moments in the singular variable. To make this precise, the authors develop a Wasserstein-type distance adapted to the singular variable together with weighted total variation estimates that allow them to control derivatives of the Smoluchowski flow.

Quantized resistance limitations for graphene p-n junction devices

Tamar M. Padwa, DaVonne Henry, Dominick S. Scaletta et al.. Quantized resistance limitations for graphene p-n junction devices. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated the feasibility of using graphene p-n junction devices to achieve quantized Hall resistance values beyond those readily available from standard epitaxial graphene. The researchers examined multiple device geometries — rectangular, Corbino, and triangular — by developing an automated Landauer-Büttiker solver in Python to map the relationship between device topology and effective resistance, since this mapping is mathematically degenerate and not straightforwardly invertible. The computational approach was validated against cryogenic multi-terminal transport measurements and circuit simulations. By applying Shannon entropy analysis to quantify the diversity of accessible resistance states, the study found that rectangular checkerboard configurations offer the greatest topological complexity and range of tunable outputs, while Corbino geometries are severely limited in the resistance values they can produce.

Zeeman-Doppler imaging of V889 Her: Surface spots and magnetic field topology 2017 - 2023

Thomas Hackman, Jyri J. Lehtinen, Oleg Kochukhov et al.. Zeeman-Doppler imaging of V889 Her: Surface spots and magnetic field topology 2017 - 2023. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigates the evolving surface magnetic field and starspot configuration of the young solar analogue V889 Her using Zeeman-Doppler imaging over a multi-year observational campaign. Spectropolarimetric observations were obtained with the SOFIN instrument at the Nordic Optical Telescope, and differential rotation parameters previously reported for this star were tested within the imaging framework. The analysis reveals significant temporal changes in the radial magnetic field near the rotational pole, including a full polarity reversal around 2021, alongside a persistently strong azimuthal field component at high latitudes. Abrupt reconfigurations of the spot distribution were detected on timescales of approximately one week in 2018, confirming earlier reports of rapid surface evolution that complicate differential rotation measurements. The observations favor solar-type differential rotation, and no clear link could be established between the long-term spot activity and the observed changes in large-scale magnetic field topology.

Kronecker Approximations of Covariance Matrices for Solving Inverse Problems

Srijon Sarkar, Julianne Chung. Kronecker Approximations of Covariance Matrices for Solving Inverse Problems. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the computational challenge of handling large prior covariance matrices in Bayesian inverse problems, which arise when recovering unknown parameters from indirect or noisy observations. The authors developed approximations of large covariance matrices using structured Kronecker-Toeplitz products, exploiting the Block-Toeplitz-Toeplitz-Block structure of covariance kernels to create compact representations that are substantially cheaper to store and invert than the full matrices. These approximations were incorporated as prior covariance surrogates within generalized hybrid iterative methods for solving Bayesian inverse problems. The proposed Kronecker-Toeplitz approximation was shown to achieve lower relative approximation error than existing matrix approximation approaches, with rapid error decay as rank increases, and enables efficient matrix-vector products. The practical utility of the method was demonstrated through applications to two-dimensional seismic tomography reconstruction.

Why Sensors Fail in Biological Samples: Fouling, Blocking, Matrix Effects and Prevention Solutions

Nikola Lenar, Beata Paczosa‐Bator. Why Sensors Fail in Biological Samples: Fouling, Blocking, Matrix Effects and Prevention Solutions. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review paper examines why sensors and biosensors fail when deployed in complex biological fluids such as blood, serum, plasma, and sweat, focusing on the interconnected problems of surface fouling, interface blocking, and matrix interference. The authors provide a mechanistic analysis of how phenomena including protein adsorption, nonspecific binding, ionic strength variations, pH fluctuations, and electroactive interferents degrade sensor performance, leading to signal drift, reduced sensitivity, false-positive responses, and shortened operational lifetimes. The review systematically compares how these failure modes affect different sensing platforms, including electrochemical, optical, capacitive, potentiometric, and wearable biosensors, as well as field-effect transistors. A substantial portion of the work catalogues practical mitigation strategies already applied in real sensing systems, spanning antifouling surface coatings, zwitterionic and hydrogel interfaces, blocking agents, membrane engineering, microfluidic sample pre-treatment, and artificial intelligence-assisted drift correction. The paper ultimately synthesizes advances across materials science, surface chemistry, and signal processing to offer guidance for developing more stable, reliable biosensors suitable for point-of-care biomarker analysis.

Transforming Diagnostic Precision in Periodontology Through Lab-on-a-Chip Technology: A Scoping Review.

Manoj M, Jaison J, Varghese J. Transforming Diagnostic Precision in Periodontology Through Lab-on-a-Chip Technology: A Scoping Review.. International journal of dentistry. 2026

This scoping review examined the state of evidence for lab-on-a-chip and microfluidic technologies as point-of-care diagnostic tools for periodontal and peri-implant disease, addressing the limitation that conventional periodontal diagnostics reflects past damage rather than active disease. Following established scoping review methodology, the authors searched multiple electronic databases and screened records for studies involving biosensor platforms, microfluidic systems, or lab-on-a-chip devices targeting periodontal tissues or relevant biomarkers. A small subset of identified records met inclusion criteria, revealing that saliva was the predominant sample matrix and that platforms ranged from paper-based microfluidics to electrochemical biosensors and PCR chips, most targeting key periodontal pathogens or inflammatory markers such as matrix metalloproteinase-8 and interleukin-1 beta. The review found that clinical diagnostic accuracy was rarely reported, validation was predominantly conducted under controlled laboratory rather than clinical conditions, and the majority of devices remained at the prototype stage. The authors conclude that while these technologies demonstrate analytical promise, widespread clinical adoption requires multicentric clinical validation, standardized fabrication protocols, and clearer regulatory frameworks.

Microfluidic Biosensors for the Detection of Foodborne Pathogens: A Comprehensive Review.

Mao W, Zhang L. Microfluidic Biosensors for the Detection of Foodborne Pathogens: A Comprehensive Review.. Food science & nutrition. 2026

This review examines the current state of microfluidic biosensors designed to detect foodborne pathogens in complex food matrices. The authors systematically compare these platforms across multiple dimensions, including substrate materials, fabrication approaches, sample handling strategies, and detection modalities spanning optical, electrochemical, and nucleic-acid-based readouts. While published studies show that rapid pathogen detection is achievable in controlled settings, the review highlights that reported performance metrics are rarely comparable across studies due to inconsistent reporting of matrices, enrichment steps, and assay conditions. The authors identify key translational barriers including reproducible manufacturing, reagent stability, and the lack of integrated sample-to-answer workflows validated in authentic food environments. They conclude that emerging approaches such as CRISPR-based assays and AI-assisted interpretation are promising but require rigorous direct comparison and independent validation, and that future work must address matrix-specific performance, multi-site robustness, usability, cost, and regulatory compliance before commercial deployment can be justified.

Translational Barriers and AI-Driven Challenges of Microfluidics-Enabled Wearables and Implantable Systems in Personalized Medicine.

Huang K, Fong CY, Huang X et al.. Translational Barriers and AI-Driven Challenges of Microfluidics-Enabled Wearables and Implantable Systems in Personalized Medicine.. Advanced science (Weinheim, Baden-Wurttemberg, Germany). 2026

This review paper examines the state of wearable and implantable microfluidic systems and their pathway toward clinical use in personalized medicine. The authors argue that existing literature treats materials chemistry and device fabrication in isolation, and so they construct a unified, translation-focused framework spanning foundational biomaterials, fabrication techniques, and diagnostic performance benchmarking through to closed-loop theranostic platforms that combine sensing with autonomous therapy. A central focus is the integration of artificial intelligence across sensor self-calibration, multiplexed molecular signal decoding, and on-device decision-making, which together enable a shift from passive health monitoring toward adaptive therapeutic intervention. The review critically identifies socio-technical barriers to clinical translation, particularly how heterogeneity in biofluid data and disparities in population baselines can propagate algorithmic bias into deployed systems. The authors conclude that robust hardware interfaces, standardized validation benchmarks, and alignment with regulatory frameworks are necessary prerequisites for equitable and responsible deployment of these technologies in digital health.

Toward self-driving laboratory 2.0 for chemistry and materials discovery

Heeseung Lee, Hyuk Jun Yoo, Hye Su Jang et al.. Toward self-driving laboratory 2.0 for chemistry and materials discovery. Materials Horizons. 2026

This review examines the evolution of self-driving laboratories (SDLs) — autonomous platforms that design, execute, and analyze scientific experiments with minimal human input — and proposes a framework for a next-generation architecture termed SDL 2.0. The authors identify key limitations of earlier SDL systems, including narrow scope, poor interoperability, and dependence on human-curated heuristics, and use these shortcomings to motivate a more ambitious vision. The review surveys recent advances in modular hardware, AI-driven decision-making approaches such as Bayesian optimization and large language models, and orchestration software that integrates scheduling, data management, and safety protocols. From these developments, the authors derive six defining characteristics for SDL 2.0 — interoperable, collaborative, generalizable, orchestrated, safe, and creative — envisioning globally networked platforms that enable reproducible and accelerated discovery in chemistry and materials science. The paper ultimately argues that embedding modularity, AI reasoning, and community-driven standards into SDL infrastructure will not only transform how experiments are conducted but also broaden participation in and access to advanced scientific research.

ANO6-mediated trogocytosis of cancer-associated fibroblasts promotes pancreatic cancer cell growth and immune suppression

Charline Ogier, Charles Solomon Akino Mercy, Elena Bondarenko et al.. ANO6-mediated trogocytosis of cancer-associated fibroblasts promotes pancreatic cancer cell growth and immune suppression. Nature Cancer. 2026

Pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma presents a paradox in which cancer cells thrive despite a dense, poorly vascularized stroma that should limit nutrient delivery. This study investigated how cancer-associated fibroblasts (CAFs) supply essential lipids, including cholesterol, to pancreatic cancer cells within this challenging microenvironment. The researchers discovered that cancer cells form synapse-like contacts with CAFs, triggering calcium influx through Orai channels in the fibroblasts, which activates the phospholipid scramblase ANO6 and causes phosphatidylserine exposure on CAF membranes, thereby initiating trogocytosis—a process by which cancer cells bite off and internalize fragments of CAF plasma membranes loaded with lipids acquired from blood-borne particles. This ANO6-dependent phosphatidylserine externalization additionally enables CAFs to suppress cytotoxic T cell activity through trogocytosis, linking the same molecular mechanism to both metabolic support and immune evasion. Blocking ANO6 impaired tumor growth by disrupting cholesterol delivery to cancer cells and reduced immunosuppression, identifying ANO6 as a potential therapeutic target in pancreatic cancer.

Recent Advances in Ferrite‐Based Materials for Biomedical Applications: A Comprehensive Review

Pramod D. Mhase, Varsha C. Pujari, Akash V. Fulari et al.. Recent Advances in Ferrite‐Based Materials for Biomedical Applications: A Comprehensive Review. Advanced Materials. 2026

This review examines the development of ferrite-based nanomaterials for biomedical applications, tracing their evolution from basic magnetic contrast agents into multifunctional therapeutic platforms. The authors critically analyze how atomic-level engineering approaches, including cation distribution, defect modulation, and morphological anisotropy, can be used to tune magnetic, catalytic, and heating properties in spinel and hexagonal ferrite systems. Beyond established uses in magnetic resonance imaging and drug delivery, the review highlights emerging applications such as viscosity-independent magnetic hyperthermia, reactive oxygen species-driven antimicrobial therapy, and magnetic tissue engineering. A central focus is the translational gap between laboratory performance and clinical use, with the authors identifying key barriers including discrepancies between colloidal and intracellular heating efficiency, protein corona complexity, and challenges in scalable, regulatory-compliant manufacturing. The review concludes by proposing a roadmap that integrates artificial intelligence-driven material discovery and green chemistry to advance next-generation magnetotheranostic systems for precision medicine.

Regulatory Challenges and Opportunities for Cell‐Derived Extracellular Vesicles in Pharmaceutical Development: A European and Global Perspective

Tania Limongi, Paola Milla, Barbara Stella et al.. Regulatory Challenges and Opportunities for Cell‐Derived Extracellular Vesicles in Pharmaceutical Development: A European and Global Perspective. Journal of Extracellular Vesicles. 2026

This paper examines the regulatory landscape governing cell-derived extracellular vesicles as therapeutic and diagnostic products, with attention to both European and global frameworks. The authors analyze how the acellular nature of extracellular vesicles causes them to fit imperfectly into existing regulatory categories, including a recent European guideline clarifying that minimally modified extracellular vesicles fall outside advanced therapy medicinal product definitions, while other jurisdictions such as the United States regulate them as drugs and biological products under distinct criteria. By critically reviewing regulatory, technical, safety, and ethical considerations across multiple regions, the paper identifies key operational challenges including inconsistent classification criteria, undefined critical quality attributes, and unresolved comparability standards that complicate cross-regional clinical development. The authors argue that extracellular vesicle products can be advanced within existing medicinal product frameworks provided regulatory tools are applied consistently and current ambiguities are systematically addressed. The review is intended to support dialogue among international regulatory agencies and scientific societies to promote methodological convergence and move extracellular vesicle therapies toward safe and internationally credible clinical application.

Polyendocrine metabolic ovarian syndrome (PMOS)/polycystic ovary syndrome (PCOS): current and future trends

Jessica L. Chan, Irene Masini, Margareta Danuta Pisarska. Polyendocrine metabolic ovarian syndrome (PMOS)/polycystic ovary syndrome (PCOS): current and future trends. Journal of Clinical Investigation. 2026

This review examines polycystic ovary syndrome, described as the most common endocrinologic disorder affecting women, with particular focus on the gaps in understanding that have complicated diagnosis and management. The authors synthesize current knowledge of the disease's pathophysiology from a translational perspective, covering proposed mechanisms that remain incompletely understood. The paper evaluates existing diagnostic criteria and current treatment strategies, while also addressing the long-term health consequences associated with the condition. The authors conclude by identifying future research directions and highlighting areas where scientific understanding remains insufficient for this frequently overlooked disorder.

FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices

Jonathan D. McCart, Pranav I. Deevi, Mehdi Azabou et al.. FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of deploying brain-to-text speech neuroprostheses in real-world settings, where current systems rely on computationally intensive language models that produce high and variable inference latencies. The authors developed FETTUCCINE, an end-to-end decoding framework that repurposes automatic speech recognition model architectures to translate intracortical neural activity directly into text with greater efficiency. When evaluated on publicly available data from a neuroprosthesis user, the framework achieved low word error rates and, crucially, ran successfully on a commercially available mobile device at speeds far exceeding real-time demands. The system also demonstrated the ability to adapt to neural signal drift across recording sessions through fine-tuning, and gradient-based saliency maps revealed that the neural features driving decoding align with established physiological knowledge. These results collectively demonstrate that portable, low-latency brain-to-text communication is achievable outside of laboratory infrastructure.

Impact of Grid-Forming Inverter Placement and Penetration on Local and Aggregate Frequency

Fatma Alfailkawi, Marena Trujillo, Bri-Mathias Hodge. Impact of Grid-Forming Inverter Placement and Penetration on Local and Aggregate Frequency. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how the placement and penetration level of grid-forming inverters within a power network affect frequency stability, distinguishing between local bus-level and aggregate system-wide frequency metrics. Using an exhaustive search across all possible assignments of synchronous generators and droop-controlled grid-forming inverters in the IEEE 39-bus test system, the authors evaluated frequency nadir and rate of change of frequency under load disturbances applied at every eligible bus. The results show that at a fixed grid-forming inverter penetration level, placement choices alone produce substantial variation in worst-case local frequency nadir and maximum local rate of change of frequency. Critically, aggregate frequency assessment dramatically underrepresents this variation, masking nearly all of the placement-dependent differences visible in local frequency metrics. The authors conclude that relying solely on aggregate frequency measures may conceal both beneficial and harmful effects of inverter placement on local frequency performance.

Complex fluid flow dynamics by bright scattershot boson sampling

Robert Schade, Shailendra K. Rathor, Tim J. Bartley et al.. Complex fluid flow dynamics by bright scattershot boson sampling. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether boson sampling machines can be used to model the time evolution of complex nonlinear dynamical systems relevant to fluid dynamics. The researchers extended the existing scattershot boson sampling framework by incorporating higher photon number components — a scheme they call bright scattershot boson sampling — which overcomes scaling limitations by making use of all randomly generated input photon number distributions rather than discarding them. Using a digital twin of the proposed architecture, they demonstrated that the photonic approach accurately reproduces the temporal dynamics of both a Lorenz-type model of two-dimensional thermally driven convection and a higher-dimensional model of global atmospheric circulation. The scheme proved resilient to substantial photon losses and outperformed both coherent light implementations and a classical reservoir-computing model of equivalent query complexity in terms of prediction horizon. These findings suggest that near-term, noisy intermediate-scale photonic quantum devices hold practical promise for simulating real-world complex systems.

Solution of the Qubit Marginal Problem from Fermionic Representability

David A. Mazziotti. Solution of the Qubit Marginal Problem from Fermionic Representability. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the quantum marginal problem—determining whether a set of local reduced states is consistent with some global quantum state—specifically for qubit systems. The authors establish a mathematical connection between qubit representability and the well-developed representability theory of fermionic systems by encoding each qubit as a single fermion distributed across two orbitals. Through this mapping, they prove that valid qubit marginals correspond to an exposed face of the set of representable fermionic reduced density matrices, characterized by the vanishing of site-occupation variance, allowing the full hierarchy of fermionic representability conditions to transfer directly to qubits. Testing these conditions on frustrated Heisenberg and transverse-field Ising rings demonstrates that low-level positivity conditions are either exact at a known analytical point or accurate to within a fraction of a percent near criticality. Beyond ground-state physics, the authors argue that their framework can also be applied to mitigate measurement errors on qubit-based quantum hardware.

Tessa: Encoding Classical Data Across an Entire Quantum Processor

Daniel Bultrini. Tessa: Encoding Classical Data Across an Entire Quantum Processor. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces Tessa, a hardware-aware encoding scheme designed to store classical data across an entire quantum processor by distributing address and data qubits in a checkerboard pattern across the device's coupling graph. The approach extends prior methods by ensuring that neighboring data qubits share only a subset of address qubits, which eliminates the need for SWAP gates or routing when the native coupling graph is used, though the scheme generalizes to any bipartite coupling graph. The number of measurement shots required for readout scales with the local coupling degree of the device rather than the full system size. The scheme was experimentally validated on real quantum hardware, successfully encoding and decoding large numbers of real values representing diverse data types including images, audio, genetic sequences, and protein sequences.

Toward On-Device Markerless 6DoF Tracking of a Physical Manipulative on a Mixed-Reality Headset: A Preliminary Study

Guangxi Feng, Philip Yasskin, Wei Yan. Toward On-Device Markerless 6DoF Tracking of a Physical Manipulative on a Mixed-Reality Headset: A Preliminary Study. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates markerless six-degree-of-freedom tracking of a physical LEGO airplane manipulative for use in a mixed-reality educational application running on a standalone headset. The researchers developed a pipeline using a compact YOLO model trained on synthetically rendered images to predict keypoints from the physical object, which are then passed to a perspective-n-point solver to estimate the object's pose. Evaluations on synthetic validation images showed that cropping the input around the object of interest substantially improved detection rates and keypoint performance compared to using uncropped images. On-device testing demonstrated that the system could maintain a high display frame rate at a tracking frequency of two Hz, and a qualitative demonstration confirmed that virtual overlays aligned with the physical airplane following manual rotation. The authors acknowledge that quantitative pose accuracy on real headset footage remains to be assessed in future work.
