Detection of Salmonella Typhimurium using a nanoflower-mediated horseradish peroxidase-linked antibody signal system combined with microfluidic sensors.

Yu Y, Zhou H, Bu S et al.. Detection of Salmonella Typhimurium using a nanoflower-mediated horseradish peroxidase-linked antibody signal system combined with microfluidic sensors.. Mikrochimica acta. 2026

This paper developed a colorimetric biosensor for detecting the foodborne pathogen Salmonella typhimurium by combining aptamer-based recognition with enzyme-mediated signal amplification. The researchers synthesized three-dimensional nanoflower-shaped organic-inorganic nanocomposites using a one-step method, incorporating horseradish peroxidase-labeled secondary antibodies and Salmonella-specific aptamers into the material as signal labels within a sandwich-type sensing architecture. In the presence of hydrogen peroxide, the horseradish peroxidase oxidizes a colorless substrate into a colored product, enabling visual colorimetric detection, and the system was further integrated with a microfluidic device to simplify operation. The biosensor achieved a broad linear detection range and an exceptionally low detection limit, while also demonstrating good selectivity, reproducibility, and stability. The authors conclude that this platform has strong potential for pathogen detection and on-site rapid screening applications.

Rapid visual and direct detection of Acinetobacter baumannii and carbapenem resistance by oligoDNA-gold nanoparticles biosensor in clinical samples.

Chalana A, Gupta V, Sharma P et al.. Rapid visual and direct detection of Acinetobacter baumannii and carbapenem resistance by oligoDNA-gold nanoparticles biosensor in clinical samples.. Diagnostic microbiology and infectious disease. 2026

The paper investigated the rapid detection of *Acinetobacter baumannii* and its carbapenem resistance genes directly in clinical respiratory samples, addressing the limitations of conventional diagnostic methods that are slow and infrastructure-dependent. The researchers developed oligonucleotide-functionalized gold nanoparticle biosensors targeting a conserved region of the *nucAb* gene for species identification and two key carbapenem resistance genes, *bla*OXA-23 and *bla*NDM, without requiring DNA purification or amplification. The biosensors produced visible colorimetric results within thirty minutes and demonstrated a detection limit sufficient for clinically relevant bacterial loads. Notably, the *bla*NDM biosensor also detected resistance gene variants across multiple other clinically important Gram-negative pathogens, including *Klebsiella pneumoniae*, *Escherichia coli*, and *Pseudomonas aeruginosa*. The authors propose these biosensors as rapid, economical tools to support early clinical decision-making and antimicrobial resistance surveillance.

SHIELD: An Argonaute-powered immobilization-free homogeneous electrochemical biosensor for ultrasensitive viable pathogen detection.

Qiao J, Bai B, Zhang Y et al.. SHIELD: An Argonaute-powered immobilization-free homogeneous electrochemical biosensor for ultrasensitive viable pathogen detection.. Biosensors & bioelectronics. 2026

The paper describes the development of an electrochemical biosensor platform called SHIELD, designed to detect viable foodborne pathogens with high sensitivity and specificity. The system addresses a key challenge in food safety diagnostics: distinguishing living bacteria from dead ones, which can otherwise generate false-positive signals. To achieve this, the platform combines Propidium Monoazide pretreatment to chemically block DNA from dead cells, isothermal amplification to generate abundant target sequences, and Argonaute-guided cleavage of dual-labeled electrochemical probes, with unreacted probes removed via magnetic separation to produce a clean signal-on readout. By operating in solution rather than on a surface-immobilized format, the design avoids reproducibility problems common to traditional biosensors. The resulting platform demonstrated the ability to detect viable Salmonella typhimurium at extremely low concentrations within a rapid timeframe, suggesting broad applicability for sensitive screening of foodborne pathogens.

Photoelectrochemical biosensor using CoPP to quench PTCA for the detection of mip gene fragment.

Zhang F, Lu L, Li S et al.. Photoelectrochemical biosensor using CoPP to quench PTCA for the detection of mip gene fragment.. Analytical sciences : the international journal of the Japan Society for Analytical Chemistry. 2026

The paper describes the development of a photoelectrochemical biosensor designed to detect a specific gene fragment from *Legionella pneumophila*, the pathogen responsible for Legionnaires' disease. The biosensor was constructed using a perylene-based compound as a photoactive material to generate a strong initial photocurrent, with gold nanoparticles electrodeposited on the electrode surface to anchor a DNA probe strand. Upon hybridization with the target gene sequence, a cobalt porphyrin compound intercalated into the resulting DNA duplex and quenched the photocurrent signal, enabling quantitative detection. The sensor achieved a wide linear detection range and an exceptionally low detection limit in the femtomolar range, demonstrating high sensitivity. The authors propose this platform as a promising tool for practical screening of *Legionella pneumophila* in respiratory specimens with high bacterial loads.

Cross-validated SPR/SERS dual-mode fiber biosensor for rapid and accurate detection of Salmonella Typhimurium in foods.

Zhu S, Su Y, Wang Y et al.. Cross-validated SPR/SERS dual-mode fiber biosensor for rapid and accurate detection of Salmonella Typhimurium in foods.. Talanta. 2026

This paper developed a portable fiber-optic biosensor that simultaneously uses surface plasmon resonance and surface-enhanced Raman scattering to detect the foodborne pathogen Salmonella Typhimurium in food samples. The sensing platform employs sea-urchin-shaped gold/silver nanoparticles conjugated with bacteriophage-based recognition probes, which form sandwich complexes on tapered optical fibers coated with a complementary phage capture layer, allowing both detection modalities to be read from a single binding event. The SPR channel provided a wide linear detection range extending from low to high bacterial concentrations with a very low detection limit, while the SERS channel offered a similarly broad range, and the entire workflow was completed within about half an hour. The biosensor was validated against fish, vegetable, and milk matrices, demonstrating good accuracy and resistance to matrix interference. Critically, the two independent signal outputs serve as mutual cross-verification, substantially reducing false-positive results and improving overall detection reliability for field-deployable food safety applications.

Replaying germinal center evolution on a quantified affinity landscape

William S. DeWitt, Ashni A. Vora, Tatsuya Araki et al.. Replaying germinal center evolution on a quantified affinity landscape. Cell. 2026

The paper investigated the evolutionary dynamics of affinity maturation in germinal centers, where B cells undergo Darwinian selection to produce increasingly potent antibodies. The researchers developed an experimental evolution model by replaying over one hundred monoclonal germinal center reactions and used deep mutational scanning to assign affinity values to individual cells, constructing a quantified affinity landscape. They found that germinal centers achieve predictable evolutionary outcomes through noisy but persistent selection, with the exploration of sequence space heavily constrained by somatic hypermutation biases. By inferring a fitness landscape that quantitatively reproduces the affinity maturation trajectory, they demonstrated that previously observed features such as tolerance for low-affinity lineages and rapid affinity plateauing are likely artifacts of survivorship bias rather than true properties of the selection process. These findings clarify how germinal center evolution is shaped by both selective pressures and mutational constraints, while cautioning that observational biases can fundamentally distort interpretations of B cell affinity progression over time.

Recent Advances in Ferrite‐Based Materials for Biomedical Applications: A Comprehensive Review

Pramod D. Mhase, Varsha C. Pujari, Akash V. Fulari et al.. Recent Advances in Ferrite‐Based Materials for Biomedical Applications: A Comprehensive Review. Advanced Materials. 2026

This review examines the development of ferrite-based nanomaterials as multifunctional biomedical platforms, tracing their evolution from basic magnetic contrast agents to sophisticated systems capable of actively modulating biological microenvironments. The authors survey how atomic-level engineering strategies—including cation distribution, defect modulation, and morphological anisotropy—can be used to tune magnetic, catalytic, and thermal properties in both spinel and hexagonal ferrite structures. Applications discussed span established uses in MRI and drug delivery as well as emerging areas such as viscosity-independent magnetic hyperthermia, reactive oxygen species-mediated antimicrobial therapy, and magnetic tissue engineering. The review critically identifies key barriers preventing clinical translation, including discrepancies between colloidal and intracellular heating efficiency, protein corona complexity, and the difficulty of achieving scalable, GMP-compliant synthesis. The authors conclude by proposing a development roadmap that integrates artificial intelligence-driven material discovery and green chemistry to advance next-generation magnetotheranostic systems for precision medicine.

Point of care molecular cancer diagnostics

Seemesh Bhaskar, Saurabh Umrao, Hankeun Lee et al.. Point of care molecular cancer diagnostics. Lab on a Chip. 2026

This review examines the current state and future trajectory of point-of-care molecular diagnostics for cancer, addressing the need for rapid, accessible testing outside centralized laboratory settings. The authors survey a broad range of enabling technologies, including microfluidics, lab-on-a-chip platforms, nanomaterial-enhanced biosensors, and smartphone-integrated devices, alongside advances in sample preparation and detection of nucleic acid and protein biomarkers. Clinical requirements are illustrated through case studies drawn from diverse oncology settings, grounding the technological discussion in real-world diagnostic scenarios. The review also analyzes funding landscapes, intellectual property considerations, and regulatory frameworks that govern the translation of these technologies from research to commercial use. The authors conclude that interdisciplinary innovation, equitable access, and integration with precision medicine will be central to establishing point-of-care molecular diagnostics as transformative tools for cancer detection, therapy selection, and treatment monitoring.

Aminopeptidase N is a receptor for hedgehog merbecoviruses

Min Jin, Victoria A. Jefferson, Zhe Zhao et al.. Aminopeptidase N is a receptor for hedgehog merbecoviruses. Nature Microbiology. 2026

This paper investigated how hedgehog merbecoviruses — relatives of Middle East respiratory syndrome coronavirus circulating in European and Asian hedgehogs — gain entry into host cells. Using cell-based assays, pseudotype experiments with a vesicular stomatitis virus reporter system, and protein binding assays, the researchers identified aminopeptidase N (APN) as the functional entry receptor for these viruses. They found that spike protein-mediated infectivity was enhanced at temperatures matching the hedgehog's physiological body temperature rather than the standard mammalian temperature, and a broad screen of mammalian APN orthologues revealed restricted cross-species receptor usage, with human APN unable to support infection. Electron cryomicroscopy showed that the glycoprotein–receptor interface is structurally distinct from previously characterized coronavirus spike–APN interactions, providing a molecular explanation for the observed species barrier. These findings clarify the receptor biology of hedgehog merbecoviruses and contribute to assessments of their potential for zoonotic emergence.

A multimodal adaptive optical microscope for in vivo imaging from molecules to organisms

Tian-ming Fu B, Gaoxiang Liu, Daniel Edward Milkie et al.. A multimodal adaptive optical microscope for in vivo imaging from molecules to organisms. Nature Methods. 2026

This paper presents MOSAIC, a reconfigurable multimodal microscope designed to address the challenge of studying biological systems across vastly different spatial and temporal scales within complex native environments. The instrument integrates multiple advanced imaging modalities — including light-sheet, label-free, super-resolution, and multiphoton microscopy — all augmented with adaptive optics to correct for sample-induced aberrations. The authors demonstrate that MOSAIC enables noninvasive imaging of subcellular dynamics in cultured cells and live multicellular organisms, nanoscale mapping of molecular architectures across large expanded tissue samples, and structural and functional neural imaging in live mice. By allowing correlative studies across biological scales within the same specimen, MOSAIC overcomes the typical limitation of single-purpose microscopes that sacrifice versatility for performance. The platform is presented as an integrated solution for broad biological investigation spanning molecules to whole organisms.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the challenge of monitoring maritime vessels by fusing two complementary data sources: the Automatic Identification System, which provides vessel identification but has known vulnerabilities, and space-based radio frequency observations, which detect vessel activity independently but cannot identify ships on their own. Following a structured data science methodology, the researchers developed and applied a Pair Hidden Markov Model to align individual radio frequency detections with AIS vessel trajectories by jointly modeling temporal and spatial information along with noise and timing discrepancies. The approach was evaluated on real AIS data combined with synthetic radio frequency detections, benchmarked against nearest-neighbor baselines, and analyzed for systematic biases related to trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and spatial separation. A log-odds scoring variant that compares the full matching model against a null model substantially improved both pairing precision and the ability to detect vessels behaving suspiciously, though both metrics remained below those of a time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline that requires no parameter fitting. The study concludes that this single-detection Pair Hidden Markov Model formulation closely approaches but does not surpass the hand-designed baseline, highlighting remaining biases and directions for further refinement.

Polynomial-Time Solvability of the Multi-Dimensional Slope Change Problem

Apoorva Narula, Santanu S. Dey. Polynomial-Time Solvability of the Multi-Dimensional Slope Change Problem. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies the problem of detecting slope changes in multidimensional time series by fitting continuous piecewise-linear functions that share a single breakpoint, examining both a dense setting where all dimensions may change slope and a sparse setting where at most a fixed number of dimensions may do so. Despite the resulting optimization problems being nonconvex mixed-integer nonlinear programs that can even yield irrational solutions for rational inputs, the authors prove that all six problem variants across three common loss functions are exactly solvable in polynomial time. They derive efficient algorithms for the least-squares case with explicit operation counts that scale jointly in the number of dimensions and time points, and implement these for empirical evaluation. Testing on simulated data and three real-world datasets covering climate, motion tracking, and industrial sensor domains, the algorithms handle very large instances in well under a second on standard hardware. Compared to a general-purpose mixed-integer nonlinear programming solver, the proposed algorithms are faster by several orders of magnitude, while the solver frequently fails to find near-optimal solutions within a generous time limit on larger instances.

Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction

Abhilekha Dalal, Avishek Das, Samatha Ereshi Akkamahadevi. Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated whether a technique called Concept Induction can reveal what convolutional neural network hidden layers actually represent when networks exploit contextual background information rather than target objects to classify agricultural images. The researchers applied this approach across five agricultural datasets with varying degrees of contextual bias, examining four CNN architectures at different network depths and multiple image patch locations, encoding quantitative image descriptors into a knowledge graph to derive logical expressions characterizing neuron activations. They found that a substantial minority of tested neuron-feature pairs were validated, with intermediate network depths showing the highest validation rates and texture-related features such as edge, Gabor, and Tamura descriptors being most frequently validated. Importantly, the degree of contextual bias in a dataset did not predict how interpretable the corresponding hidden neurons were, suggesting that a network's behavioral reliance on background context and the semantic interpretability of its internal representations are distinct phenomena. The authors conclude that Concept Induction can usefully complement existing patch-based methods for detecting contextual bias by providing insight into what hidden neurons encode.

Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection

Donghoon Lee, Jusung Park, Shinjin Kang. Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the problem of detecting out-of-distribution objects in real-world deployment of object detectors by framing each detected bounding box decision as a hypothesis test. The authors identify that the null distribution of in-distribution boxes varies systematically with box size and border contact, factors the detector already observes, and argue that boxes should only be compared against in-distribution boxes sharing these same characteristics. Building on this insight, they propose reading features from before the classification layers, since later layers optimized for known classes discard information that distinguishes unknown objects. The resulting method, PLUM, is a training-free scoring approach that pools features from the last backbone stage within a box and measures their distance to in-distribution classes conditioned on box size and border contact, achieving leading out-of-distribution detection performance on standard benchmarks without requiring external models.

Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space

Chanasorn Kongprachaya, Nicola Bartolo, Daniele Bertacca et al.. Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether Redshift Space Distortions (RSD), including leading-order general relativistic corrections, can mimic parity violation in measurements of the galaxy four-point correlation function (4PCF). The authors analyzed the perturbative relativistic galaxy number density to third order using Standard Eulerian Perturbation Theory, incorporating tidal bias, wide-angle effects through a local line-of-sight definition, and an arbitrary cosmological background. By expanding the 4PCF in spherical harmonics, they demonstrated that angular momentum selection rules encoded in Wigner 3-j symbols force all parity-odd contributions from RSD to vanish upon averaging over all line-of-sight orientations, in both the plane-parallel and full wide-angle cases. This finding holds for the isotropic 4PCF estimator and extends prior trispectrum results to a more general setting. The result is significant for large-scale structure analyses because it supports the interpretation that any detected parity-violation signal in the isotropic 4PCF cannot be attributed to RSD as a systematic artifact.

An All-Digital Coherent AFSK Demodulator for CubeSat Applications

Rubem Vasconcelos Pacelli, Rodrigo de Lima Florindo, Felix Antreich et al.. An All-Digital Coherent AFSK Demodulator for CubeSat Applications. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of coherent demodulation for audio frequency-shift keying signals used in CubeSat communications, where the severe line-of-sight dynamics of low Earth orbit typically make coherent detection impractical due to synchronization degradation. The authors developed an all-digital coherent demodulator that combines a Kalman filter for carrier phase and timing synchronization with the Viterbi algorithm for maximum likelihood sequence detection, with detected bit statistics fed back to estimate phase shift, Doppler frequency shift, and Doppler drift. Original mathematical analyses were derived to characterize synchronization accuracy under realistic low Earth orbit conditions, and the system was evaluated using simulated real CubeSat orbits over an additive white Gaussian noise channel. The proposed coherent demodulator achieves a substantial gain in bit error rate performance compared to conventional noncoherent AFSK demodulation, demonstrating that coherent detection can be made viable even under intense orbital dynamics.

Development and clinical validation of a multiplex PCR-based targeted next-generation sequencing panel for simultaneous DNA/RNA pathogen detection in respiratory tract infections: a comprehensive performance evaluation.

Long J, Tao X, Wang J et al.. Development and clinical validation of a multiplex PCR-based targeted next-generation sequencing panel for simultaneous DNA/RNA pathogen detection in respiratory tract infections: a comprehensive performance evaluation.. Clinical chemistry and laboratory medicine. 2026

This study developed and validated a multiplex PCR-based targeted next-generation sequencing panel designed to detect a broad range of clinically relevant pathogens simultaneously from respiratory samples. The panel was evaluated against both conventional microbiological tests and metagenomic next-generation sequencing using bronchoalveolar lavage fluid samples from patients with suspected lower respiratory tract infections in a combined retrospective and prospective design. A novel single-nucleotide polymorphism-based bioinformatics pipeline incorporating machine learning was integrated to distinguish true infections from laboratory contamination, achieving near-perfect discriminative performance. The panel demonstrated high sensitivity, particularly for viral pathogens, reliably detected fastidious organisms, and showed strong concordance with phenotypic antimicrobial resistance testing. The authors conclude that the panel offers diagnostic performance comparable to metagenomic sequencing while providing advantages in turnaround time, contamination control, and resistance prediction, positioning it as a practical molecular diagnostic tool for lower respiratory tract infections.

Development and validation of a multiplex TaqMan real-time quantitative PCR assay for the simultaneous detection of six major bacterial respiratory pathogens in goats.

Fu M, Liu Y, Zhang Z et al.. Development and validation of a multiplex TaqMan real-time quantitative PCR assay for the simultaneous detection of six major bacterial respiratory pathogens in goats.. Frontiers in veterinary science. 2026

This study developed and validated a multiplex real-time quantitative PCR assay for the simultaneous detection of six major bacterial and mycoplasmal respiratory pathogens in goats. The researchers designed two triplex TaqMan panels, one targeting three Mycoplasma species and another targeting two bacterial pathogens and a Streptococcus subspecies, then evaluated the assays for analytical specificity, sensitivity, and reproducibility using plasmid standards and nucleic acids from both target and non-target organisms. The assay demonstrated high specificity with no cross-reactivity against non-target organisms, low limits of detection across all six targets, and excellent reproducibility as indicated by low intra- and inter-assay coefficients of variation. When applied to field-collected goat nasal swab samples, the assay detected at least one pathogen in the majority of samples and identified mixed infections in a substantial proportion of those positives, highlighting the prevalence of co-infections in clinical settings. The authors conclude that this multiplex qPCR platform offers a rapid, specific, and practical tool for clinical diagnosis and epidemiological surveillance of caprine respiratory disease.

Syndromic Testing in Clinical Microbiology and Infectious Diseases: An Updated Review of Diagnostic Performance, Clinical and Healthcare Impact, and Emerging Developments.

Wang G, Chen F. Syndromic Testing in Clinical Microbiology and Infectious Diseases: An Updated Review of Diagnostic Performance, Clinical and Healthcare Impact, and Emerging Developments.. Microorganisms. 2026

This review examines syndromic testing, a diagnostic approach that simultaneously detects multiple pathogens and antimicrobial resistance determinants from clinical specimens using nucleic acid amplification, across seven major infectious disease syndromes including respiratory, bloodstream, gastrointestinal, and central nervous system infections. The authors surveyed the current landscape of regulatory-cleared multiplex panels available in the United States, evaluating their diagnostic performance, clinical utility, and limitations for each syndrome. Evidence reviewed indicates that syndromic panels provide comprehensive pathogen identification within hours compared to the day-to-multiday turnaround of traditional culture-based methods, enabling faster targeted therapy and reductions in unnecessary antimicrobial use. When integrated with antimicrobial stewardship programs, these panels can shorten hospital length of stay and have shown mortality benefits particularly in patients with bloodstream infections, though rapid results do not automatically translate into improved clinical action or outcomes. The review also highlights ongoing challenges including optimizing panel utilization, accounting for geographically variable pathogen distributions, and keeping pace with evolving antimicrobial resistance epidemiology.

Replaying germinal center evolution on a quantified affinity landscape

William S. DeWitt, Ashni A. Vora, Tatsuya Araki et al.. Replaying germinal center evolution on a quantified affinity landscape. Cell. 2026

The paper investigated the evolutionary dynamics of affinity maturation in germinal centers, where B cells compete to produce progressively higher-affinity antibodies through mutation and selection. The researchers developed an experimental evolution model by replaying over one hundred monoclonal germinal center reactions and used deep mutational scanning to assign affinity values to individual cells, thereby constructing a quantified affinity landscape. They found that germinal centers achieve predictable evolutionary outcomes despite noisy selection, with the exploration of the affinity landscape being heavily constrained by biases in somatic hypermutation. The study also inferred a fitness landscape that accurately recapitulates the observed affinity maturation trajectory. Notably, features previously attributed to germinal center biology—such as tolerance for low-affinity lineages and rapid affinity plateauing—appear to be artifacts of survivorship bias rather than true properties of the selection process.

Recent Advances in Ferrite‐Based Materials for Biomedical Applications: A Comprehensive Review

Pramod D. Mhase, Varsha C. Pujari, Akash V. Fulari et al.. Recent Advances in Ferrite‐Based Materials for Biomedical Applications: A Comprehensive Review. Advanced Materials. 2026

This review examines the design and biomedical potential of ferrite-based nanomaterials, tracing their evolution from basic magnetic contrast agents into multifunctional therapeutic platforms. The authors analyze how atomic-level engineering strategies, including cation distribution, defect modulation, and morphological control, can be used to tune magnetic, catalytic, and heating properties relevant to medical applications. The review surveys both established uses such as MRI and drug delivery and emerging applications including nanozyme-based catalytic therapy, antimicrobial treatment via reactive oxygen species, magnetic hyperthermia, and magnetic tissue engineering. A central focus is the translational gap between laboratory performance and clinical use, with critical attention given to challenges such as reduced intracellular heating efficiency, protein corona formation, and difficulties in scalable manufacturing under regulatory standards. The authors conclude by proposing a development roadmap that combines artificial intelligence-driven materials discovery with green synthesis approaches to advance next-generation ferrite-based theranostic systems for precision medicine.

mRNA-based influenza vaccine expands the B cell response breadth in humans

Hanover C. Matz, Tae-Geun Yu, Kritika Dixit et al.. mRNA-based influenza vaccine expands the B cell response breadth in humans. Nature Immunology. 2026

The paper investigated whether mRNA-based influenza vaccines could induce broader and more robust B cell responses in humans compared to conventional split-virion vaccines. Using an observational study design, researchers tracked B cell responses in healthy young adults receiving either a licensed inactivated or an investigational mRNA-based quadrivalent seasonal influenza vaccine across two consecutive seasons, examining draining lymph nodes, serum antibody repertoires, and memory B cell populations. The mRNA vaccine consistently produced higher antibody titers, greater memory B cell frequencies, and sustained germinal center reactions in draining lymph nodes lasting at least six months after vaccination in a subset of participants. Proteomic analysis of serum immunoglobulin repertoires revealed that mRNA vaccination expanded the number of vaccine-elicited antibody clonotypes and promoted intraclonal diversification linked to ongoing germinal center activity. This repertoire remodeling translated into broader binding against antigenically divergent influenza viruses, highlighting the importance of persistent germinal center responses in widening vaccine-induced antibody coverage.

ECCO Guidelines on the Prevention, Diagnosis, and Management of Infections in Inflammatory Bowel Disease

Henit A. Yanai, Pierre Ellul, Bram Verstockt et al.. ECCO Guidelines on the Prevention, Diagnosis, and Management of Infections in Inflammatory Bowel Disease. Journal of Crohn s and Colitis. 2026

These guidelines address the growing challenge of infection risk in inflammatory bowel disease patients who are treated with increasingly potent immunosuppressive and biologic therapies. The document was developed by a European professional organisation to update previous consensus guidance in light of the rapid expansion of available treatments, including newer small molecules and advanced biologics, each carrying distinct infection risk profiles. The guidelines provide structured, evidence-based recommendations covering risk stratification and the prevention, diagnosis, and management of viral, bacterial, mycobacterial, and vaccine-preventable infections in this immunocompromised patient population. A central emphasis is placed on preventative strategies tailored to the contemporary treatment landscape, recognising that opportunistic infections can cause significant morbidity and mortality in patients whose immune systems are pharmacologically modulated.

Hantavirus Emergence in a Changing World: Virology, Pathogenesis, Surveillance, and One Health Preparedness

Maria E. Ramos‐Niño, Nicolette Chiem, Prakash V. A. K. Ramdass. Hantavirus Emergence in a Changing World: Virology, Pathogenesis, Surveillance, and One Health Preparedness. Microorganisms. 2026

This review examines hantaviruses as emerging rodent-borne pathogens responsible for hemorrhagic fever with renal syndrome and hantavirus cardiopulmonary syndrome, synthesizing current knowledge across virology, epidemiology, pathogenesis, clinical presentation, and public health response. The authors highlight how environmental change, climate variability, urbanization, and land-use transformation are driving shifts in rodent reservoir dynamics and increasing the risk of virus spillover to humans. Particular attention is given to surveillance and diagnostic gaps in tropical and Caribbean regions where hantavirus circulation is likely underrecognized. The review also covers advances in molecular diagnostics, genomic surveillance, vaccine development, monoclonal antibody therapies, and climate-based early warning systems as tools for improving outbreak detection and response. The authors argue that integrated One Health surveillance combining human, animal, and environmental monitoring, alongside strengthened laboratory capacity and regional collaboration, is essential for reducing the global burden of hantavirus disease and preparing for future zoonotic threats.

Amplification-free dual-blocking autocatalytic CRISPR-Cascade for attomolar DNA detection with low nonspecific signal

Jongwon Lim, An Bao Van, Matthew Wester et al.. Amplification-free dual-blocking autocatalytic CRISPR-Cascade for attomolar DNA detection with low nonspecific signal. Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences. 2026

This paper investigated how to suppress nonspecific background signal (leakage) in autocatalytic CRISPR-based nucleic acid detection systems, which can achieve amplification-free sensitivity by coupling target recognition to self-reinforcing ribonucleoprotein generation. The researchers developed a dual-blocking CRISPR-Cascade design that simultaneously cages both the guide RNA and trigger DNA, creating an AND-gate logic requirement so that both blocking elements must be released before productive complex formation can proceed. This dual approach reduced leakage substantially compared to single-blocking configurations while maintaining rapid detection, single-copy sensitivity, and quantitative capability. The addition of a competitive guide RNA decoy further reduced background without compromising true target signal. The system was validated on clinically relevant samples by detecting Methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus directly from whole blood within a short total workflow time, demonstrating its potential as a generalizable, leakage-resistant framework for decentralized diagnostic applications.

Who Should Be on the Panel? Task-Conditioned Persona Selection for LLM Trading Agents

Boming Yang, Yuquan Wang, Jiaxian Guo et al.. Who Should Be on the Panel? Task-Conditioned Persona Selection for LLM Trading Agents. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how the composition of a multi-agent large language model system should be tailored to the specific task at hand, rather than fixed uniformly across all tasks. The authors introduce FinPanel, a financial trading system in which a recruiter module selects a panel of trader personas for each individual stock based on statistical properties of how that stock trades, with each persona independently generating daily trading calls and a manager aggregating their arguments alongside hierarchical track records. The key claim is that task-conditioned persona selection — choosing *who* is in the group based on task characteristics — is more important than the coordination mechanism or the size of the panel. Evaluated on US stocks over an out-of-sample period, FinPanel outperforms fixed-panel configurations of equal size, prior LLM-based trading agents, and rule-based and reinforcement-learning baselines, while also exhibiting shallower drawdowns than a buy-and-hold strategy and maintaining performance without any warm-up history. The findings suggest that group composition should be treated as a per-task design decision made prior to determining how agents coordinate.

STAMP: Manufacturing-Informed Generative Design and Shape Optimization of Sheet Panels

Jihoon Kim, Jun Hyeok Song, Wonho Ha et al.. STAMP: Manufacturing-Informed Generative Design and Shape Optimization of Sheet Panels. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces STAMP, a generative design framework for formed sheet metal panels that embeds manufacturing constraints directly into the shape representation rather than enforcing them through penalty terms during optimization. Each panel is described by a material layout and a depth map defining a single height per planar position, which inherently prevents undercuts and fixes nominal thickness, ensuring every generated or optimized panel is manufacturable by construction. The system uses boundary-conditioned diffusion to generate novel panel layouts and employs predictor gradients to optimize surface geometry while preserving outlines, openings, and fixed regions. Demonstrated on automotive hood inner panels, STAMP achieves accurate surface reconstruction and can produce varied novel layouts from very few training examples, with data efficiency improving rapidly as training set size grows. Predictor-guided selection and surface optimization yield substantial reductions in structural deformation with only modest mass increases, outperforming random design selection across all tested design families.

Positive Weights Do Not Certify TWFE Inference: A Design Diagnostic under Treatment-Effect Heterogeneity

Stanisław M. S. Halkiewicz. Positive Weights Do Not Certify TWFE Inference: A Design Diagnostic under Treatment-Effect Heterogeneity. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether positive treatment weights in two-way fixed-effects regression—often considered a sufficient condition for valid causal inference—actually guarantee adequate inference under treatment-effect heterogeneity. The authors derive a design-based diagnostic quantity that measures the worst-case mean shift in the test statistic when local treatment-effect drift is present, showing this quantity depends entirely on the study design rather than on the data-generating process. To certify or reject the adequacy of conventional inference, they develop two regular one-sided procedures: a multiplier lower bound to detect unsafe designs and a covariance-aware noncentral chi-squared inversion to certify adequacy, establishing a numerical threshold for a given nominal size and tolerance. Applying the diagnostic to several empirical panels—including a minimum-wage study where all treated-cell weights are positive—they find that positive weights alone do not certify uniform inferential adequacy, with estimated lower bounds on the shift exceeding the safety threshold in multiple cases. The paper concludes that the diagnostic usefully separates the distinct problems of point correction, uniform exposure, and statistically warranted certification of conventional two-way fixed-effects inference.

Does Increasing Government Spending on Tertiary Education Provide a Return on Investment? A Bayesian Causal Inference Analysis

Andrew C. Parnell. Does Increasing Government Spending on Tertiary Education Provide a Return on Investment? A Bayesian Causal Inference Analysis. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examined whether government increases in public spending on tertiary education generate a positive fiscal return on investment, disentangled from general economic growth. The researchers assembled a large multi-decade cross-country panel and applied continuous-treatment Multivariate Bayesian Causal Forests to jointly model changes in four tax outcomes while controlling for starting conditions, predicted funding levels, and subsequent economic growth. They found that increasing per-student university spending shifts the tax base toward direct income and profit taxation, with effects strengthening over time as graduating cohorts enter the workforce, suggesting the fiscal response takes roughly a decade to fully materialize. When a cohort's educational costs were compared against its projected career-length tax contributions, high-income economies showed a several-fold total tax return on the initial investment. Critically, the analysis also found that expanding overall university budgets without protecting spending per student yields no measurable fiscal return, underscoring the importance of maintaining per-student funding levels rather than simply increasing aggregate expenditure.

Constant-Weight Sparse Vector Coded Augmented-AFDM

Zhou Lu, Mohammed El-Hajjar, Lie-liang Yang. Constant-Weight Sparse Vector Coded Augmented-AFDM. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses two related problems in wireless communications: the growing complexity of sparse vector coding schemes at higher data rates, and the susceptibility of conventional multicarrier waveforms to Doppler-induced interference and high peak-to-average power ratio in doubly selective channels. The authors propose a constant-weight sparse vector coded augmented affine frequency division multiplexing scheme that represents information through constant-weight codewords with fixed Hamming weight and large Hamming distance, keeping the sparse vector set manageable while maintaining reliability. A joint support and symbol detection algorithm is developed alongside the scheme to recover transmitted information by solving sparse recovery problems. Simulations comparing the proposed approach against conventional and enhanced sparse vector coding schemes, as well as multiple channel coding options for QAM symbol protection, show reliability gains of up to fourteen decibels over existing methods. The results also reveal that the weight and minimum Hamming distance of the constant-weight codewords must be carefully balanced against modulation order and sparse recovery feasibility to optimize overall system performance.

More Genes, Faster Inference: Efficient Large-Panel Spatial Transcriptomics Prediction from Histology

Songming Zhang, Yuxiao Luo, Shifu Luo et al.. More Genes, Faster Inference: Efficient Large-Panel Spatial Transcriptomics Prediction from Histology. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the problem of predicting spatially resolved gene expression profiles from routine tissue histology images, a task typically constrained by complex model architectures and small gene panels. The authors introduce GeneFlow, a framework that combines flow-matching generative modeling with structured multimodal fusion, using tokenizers to encode both histological morphology and cross-gene interactions at spatial spots. Rather than predicting an intermediate velocity field, the model directly predicts gene expression while still being trained with a velocity-based flow-matching objective. GeneFlow achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple spatial transcriptomics benchmarks, with substantial gains in large-panel gene prediction, while dramatically reducing computational cost compared to existing approaches. The authors argue that this combination of efficiency, scalability, and biological fidelity makes GeneFlow particularly suitable for clinical applications and downstream biological discovery from widely available histology images.

Rapid carbapenemase detection in enterobacterales using a lateral flow immunoassay: diagnostic performance and association with antimicrobial treatment decisions.

The study evaluated a lateral flow immunochromatographic assay (LFA) for rapid detection of carbapenemase-producing Enterobacterales, comparing its diagnostic performance against commercial quantitative PCR, the Carba NP-direct test, and the modified carbapenem inactivation method, while also examining whether same-day LFA results influenced clinical treatment decisions. Both retrospective and prospective cohorts of Enterobacterales isolates were tested, with agreement metrics calculated and multivariable logistic regression used to assess the association between LFA positivity and antimicrobial treatment escalation. The LFA achieved perfect agreement with qPCR for carbapenemase detection and type identification across both cohorts, outperforming the phenotypic comparator methods, which showed notably lower sensitivity. The findings support the LFA as a highly accurate, rapid diagnostic tool whose results are meaningfully linked to same-day clinical antimicrobial management decisions.

Sharp nonuniqueness for the Vlasov--Navier--Stokes system

Xingyu Li. Sharp nonuniqueness for the Vlasov--Navier--Stokes system. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper studies the Vlasov–Navier–Stokes system, which couples a kinetic equation for a particle distribution function with the fluid equations governing the surrounding velocity field. The authors construct infinitely many weak solutions for smooth initial data, achieving sharpness at the endpoint of the classical Ladyzhenskaya–Prodi–Serrin uniqueness scale, since uniqueness holds for velocities in the borderline space but fails just below it. The nonuniqueness is driven entirely by the fluid component via a convex integration scheme, while the kinetic equation is solved exactly; the central analytical challenge is that the particle distribution retains memory of the entire past velocity history, requiring a novel causal gluing procedure that reconstructs the system sequentially on successive time intervals. This approach produces wild solutions that are nonetheless classically strong outside a closed, exceptionally thin set of times, and a causal mollification argument extends the construction to rough, finite-energy initial data in low dimensions. Additionally, in three dimensions the authors construct Leray solutions satisfying the strong energy inequality whose singular time sets have vanishing Hausdorff measure at a precise threshold.

Elliptic Sampling: Optimal Interpolant Schedules for Gaussians and Beyond

Cole Becker, Taylan Kargin, Kimia Hassibi et al.. Elliptic Sampling: Optimal Interpolant Schedules for Gaussians and Beyond. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the problem of designing optimal interpolant schedules and numerical discretizations for stochastic interpolant-based generative models, which require bridging an initial distribution to a target distribution via a probability-flow ODE. The authors introduce a framework grounded in the analyticity properties of complexified ODE trajectories, solving the joint schedule-discretization design problem exactly using conformal mapping, which yields a family of variance-preserving schedules parametrized by Jacobi elliptic functions. Pairing these elliptic schedules with a tailored discretization produces a new algorithm called Zoloflow, which is closely related to Zolotarev's classical optimal rational function approximants and explicitly depends on the condition number of the target distribution. For Gaussian targets, Zoloflow is shown to achieve minimax optimality among oblivious score-span algorithms in terms of score query complexity, and for log-concave targets the elliptic schedule enables sampling with controlled Wasserstein error using a number of score evaluations that scales only polylogarithmically in the condition number and error tolerance. The theoretical guarantees are complemented by empirical evaluations on both synthetic benchmarks and image generation datasets, demonstrating the practical advantages of the elliptic schedule over alternative proposals.

No free lattice contains an infinite ascending chain of copies of FL(3)

William DeMeo, Ralph Freese. No free lattice contains an infinite ascending chain of copies of FL(3). arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates whether a free lattice can contain an infinite strictly ascending chain of sublattices each isomorphic to the free lattice on three generators, a question posed decades ago and previously left open. The authors analyze the structure of proper self-embeddings of the free lattice on three generators, showing that any such chain would require a coherent sequence of self-embeddings with specific compatibility and fixed-point properties. Through careful case analysis of how self-embeddings interact with free generating triples and dual operations, they demonstrate that the required configuration is impossible beyond a certain length, thereby proving no such infinite ascending chain exists in any free lattice. They also establish a contrast showing that without the freeness condition, an infinite strictly ascending chain of three-generated sublattices does exist, meaning the lattice analogue of a classical chain condition for free groups fails. The entire theorem is formally verified using the Agda proof assistant under safe flags, with the proof avoiding classical principles by replacing infinite pigeonhole arguments with finite searches and induction.

Valid Inequalities for Bilevel 0-1 Shortest-Path Problems

Bruno M. Pacheco, Margarida Carvalho, Kim Yu. Valid Inequalities for Bilevel 0-1 Shortest-Path Problems. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper studies bilevel optimization problems where a leader makes binary decisions and a follower solves a shortest-path problem, aiming to characterize the valid inequalities needed to tighten the continuous relaxation of such programs. The authors employ a dualize-and-combine reformulation and demonstrate that even with ideal formulations for both the leader's decisions and the follower's primal solution set, fractional solutions can persist in the relaxation. They identify that the critical structure requiring convexification lies in the interaction between leader variables and follower dual variables, which they formalize as a leader-potential set, and they characterize valid inequalities for this set using network flow theory, introducing a family called root cuts that are sufficient to close the objective gap. Despite these structural insights, the authors prove that the associated cut generation problem is computationally hard even in very simple network settings. To address this complexity, they propose a constructive method that exploits the network structure to fix coefficients in the valid inequalities while still preserving separation guarantees.

OpenLayout: An Open and Agentic-Driven Platform for Holistic VLSI Physical Design and Benchmarking

Qiufeng Li, Sizhe Tang, Quan Cheng et al.. OpenLayout: An Open and Agentic-Driven Platform for Holistic VLSI Physical Design and Benchmarking. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the challenge of automating design space optimization in VLSI physical design, where the enormous combinatorial space of technology nodes, circuit configurations, and constraints—combined with long tool runtimes—forces heavy reliance on experienced engineers to meet power, performance, and area targets. The authors propose that large language models, enriched with distilled human design expertise, can interpret complex design contexts and reason through the optimization process in ways that conventional algorithms cannot. To realize this, they developed OpenLayout, an agentic platform employing multi-agent collaboration with embedded design knowledge to enable self-iterative design space optimization across the full physical design flow. They also introduced OpenLayout-Bench, a benchmark targeting advanced technology nodes using commercial EDA tools to systematically evaluate LLM-driven physical design automation. Experimental results show that OpenLayout achieves state-of-the-art performance with a substantial accuracy improvement over baseline methods.

Complex fluid flow dynamics by bright scattershot boson sampling

Robert Schade, Shailendra K. Rathor, Tim J. Bartley et al.. Complex fluid flow dynamics by bright scattershot boson sampling. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether photonic quantum computing based on boson sampling can be used to model complex nonlinear dynamical systems relevant to fluid dynamics. The researchers extended the existing scattershot boson sampling framework by incorporating higher photon number components — termed bright scattershot boson sampling — to overcome scaling limitations by exploiting all randomly generated input photon number distributions. Using a digital twin of the proposed architecture grounded in multiphoton statistics, they applied the scheme to two fluid-flow benchmarks: a Lorenz-type model of thermally driven convection and a model of global atmospheric circulation. The photonic approach accurately reproduced the temporal dynamics of both systems, demonstrated resilience to photon losses up to fifty percent, outperformed coherent light under comparable conditions, and exceeded the prediction horizon of a classical reservoir-computing model with equivalent query complexity. These findings suggest that near-term, noisy intermediate-scale photonic quantum devices hold practical promise for simulating real-world complex systems.

HIV point-of-care viral load

Topic mode · generated November 16, 2026 at 21:59 UTC · 18 items

Validation and performance of a rapid HIV-1 viral load assay.

Uhteg K, Bayan MH, Boudreau A et al.. Validation and performance of a rapid HIV-1 viral load assay.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

The paper evaluated whether a rapid quantitative nucleic acid test, the Xpert HIV-1 Viral Load XC assay, could serve as a primary diagnostic tool within HIV-1 testing workflows, potentially enabling both diagnosis and initial viral load measurement in a single clinical encounter. The assay was assessed for analytical range, precision, and agreement against standard-of-care methods, including comparison with four other HIV assays using plasma samples from HIV-positive and HIV-negative individuals. The assay demonstrated strong analytical performance, complete agreement with a reference quantitative assay, and a substantially faster turnaround time compared to conventional batch testing. However, positive agreement with comparator assays was imperfect, with most discrepancies occurring in samples near or below the lower limit of quantification, and the assay produced a small proportion of invalid results. The findings underscore both the promise and the remaining limitations of deploying rapid molecular HIV diagnostics at the point of care, particularly regarding sensitivity at low viral concentrations.

Patient perspectives on point-of-care HIV nucleic acid tests for viral load monitoring in Seattle, WA.

Niemann LA, Murphy M, Violette LR et al.. Patient perspectives on point-of-care HIV nucleic acid tests for viral load monitoring in Seattle, WA.. AIDS care. 2026

This study examined patient perspectives on point-of-care nucleic acid testing for HIV viral load monitoring as an alternative to standard laboratory tests, which can take days to return results. Participants receiving a semi-quantitative point-of-care test through a dedicated study were invited to complete acceptability surveys and in-depth interviews about their experiences with the technology. Participants generally found the point-of-care approach acceptable and preferred its faster turnaround time and the convenience of using a fingerstick blood sample collected directly in the clinic compared to conventional viral load testing. However, participants expressed a preference for an even shorter turnaround time than the approximately two hours the test required, as well as a lower limit of detection than the current threshold. The findings suggest that point-of-care nucleic acid testing could be valuable for people with HIV who are incompletely engaged in care, though improvements to speed and sensitivity would enhance its feasibility for broader implementation.

Can exercise affect immune function to increase susceptibility to infection?

Richard J. Simpson, Richard J. Simpson, Maree Gleeson et al.. Can exercise affect immune function to increase susceptibility to infection?. PubMed. 2026

This debate article addresses a fundamental question in exercise immunology: whether exercise itself can suppress immune function and increase susceptibility to infection. The paper contrasts the generally accepted view that moderate, short-duration exercise benefits immune defense with the contested claim that arduous exercise, as practiced by high-performance athletes and military personnel, suppresses immunity and raises infection risk. Opposing groups debated whether athletes are genuinely more susceptible to infection than the general population, whether exercise independently drives increased infection risk or whether confounding factors such as sleep disruption, psychological stress, travel, and nutritional deficits are responsible, and whether the biomarkers and study designs used in the field provide reliable evidence. Both sides agreed that infection susceptibility is multifactorial in origin, but could not resolve whether exercise itself is a direct causative factor in elevated infection risk among athletes. The authors conclude that the question remains open and call for more rigorous empirical research to disentangle the specific contribution of exercise from the many interrelated variables common to high-performance sport and military populations.

MedicalAgentsBench for complex medical reasoning: Comparing internalized reasoning models versus externalized agent-based frameworks

Yanjun Shao, Xiangru Tang, Jiwoong Sohn et al.. MedicalAgentsBench for complex medical reasoning: Comparing internalized reasoning models versus externalized agent-based frameworks. Patterns. 2026

This paper investigated whether internalized reasoning in large language models and externalized agent-based scaffolding represent competing or complementary approaches to complex medical question answering. The authors introduced a curated benchmark of challenging clinical questions drawn from multiple medical datasets, applying difficulty-aware filtering and contamination screening to ensure meaningful evaluation. They tested a range of internalized reasoning models, base models, and agent-based frameworks across this benchmark. The key finding is that both approaches independently improve performance and their benefits are additive, with the best results achieved by combining an internalized reasoning model with an agent workflow. A cost-performance analysis further showed that this combination dominates other configurations, while lightweight optimization on cheaper models provides a viable path for resource-constrained deployments.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

Yuqing Liu, Kun Zhu, Weijun Peng et al.. Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines how the integration of multiomics technologies with artificial intelligence is reshaping precision drug discovery and translational medicine. The authors survey a broad range of multiomics approaches — including genomic, transcriptomic, proteomic, and metabolomic profiling — alongside deep learning methodologies capable of extracting meaningful patterns from complex biological datasets. The review analyzes how this combined framework can accelerate target identification, lead optimization, drug repositioning, de novo compound discovery, virtual screening, pharmacokinetic modeling, and clinical trial design. Applications across oncology, neurodegenerative diseases, and cardiovascular conditions are examined to illustrate the practical therapeutic potential of these converging technologies. The authors also acknowledge persistent challenges, including harmonization of disparate data streams, reproducibility concerns, and algorithmic bias, while arguing that the fusion of multiomics and artificial intelligence marks the beginning of a new era in bench-to-clinic medicine.

APASL clinical practice guidelines on the management of chronic hepatitis B infection: a 2026 update

Hong You, Rakhi Maiwall, Jing Chen et al.. APASL clinical practice guidelines on the management of chronic hepatitis B infection: a 2026 update. Hepatology International. 2026

These guidelines address the ongoing burden of chronic hepatitis B infection in the Asia Pacific region, where the disease continues to drive significant rates of liver failure, cirrhosis, and hepatocellular carcinoma despite decades of scientific progress. The Asia Pacific Association for the Study of the Liver convened a Viral Elimination Taskforce comprising key opinion leaders from member countries to synthesize current evidence and respond to shifting epidemiological, socioeconomic, and technological landscapes. The taskforce developed updated clinical practice recommendations spanning thirteen major domains, including screening, vaccination, antiviral treatment, and hepatocellular carcinoma surveillance. The guidelines aim to support healthcare professionals, policymakers, and patients in making practical and cost-effective management decisions. Their implementation is framed as a coordinated regional effort toward reducing and ultimately eliminating the disease burden of chronic hepatitis B in the Asia Pacific.

MEmilio: a high performance Modular EpideMIcs simuLatIOn software for multi-scale and comparative simulations of infectious disease dynamics

Julia Bicker, Carlotta Gerstein, David Kerkmann et al.. MEmilio: a high performance Modular EpideMIcs simuLatIOn software for multi-scale and comparative simulations of infectious disease dynamics. Scientific Reports. 2026

MEmilio is a modular, high-performance software framework designed to unify the development, execution, and comparison of diverse epidemiological models for infectious disease simulation. The framework addresses the fragmented software ecosystem in epidemic modeling by harmonizing compartmental, metapopulation, and agent-based approaches within a single architecture built on an efficient C++ core with a Python interface. Standardized representations of space, demography, and mobility allow models to be scaled across spatial resolutions and population sizes, while integration with uncertainty quantification and parameter inference tools supports applications ranging from scenario exploration to model calibration. The framework is designed to run on both personal computers and high-performance computing systems, and strict software-engineering practices including continuous integration testing are employed to ensure robustness. By unifying disparate modeling paradigms, MEmilio aims to reduce barriers to model reuse and comparison, facilitate principled examination of model assumptions, and accelerate modeling-based outbreak preparedness.

Impact of Grid-Forming Inverter Placement and Penetration on Local and Aggregate Frequency

Fatma Alfailkawi, Marena Trujillo, Bri-Mathias Hodge. Impact of Grid-Forming Inverter Placement and Penetration on Local and Aggregate Frequency. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how the placement and penetration level of grid-forming inverters affect frequency stability in power systems transitioning away from synchronous generators. Using an exhaustive search across all possible assignments of synchronous generators and droop-controlled grid-forming inverters in the IEEE 39-bus system, the researchers evaluated frequency performance under load disturbances applied at every eligible bus. They assessed both local bus frequency metrics and aggregate frequency metrics, specifically nadir and Rate of Change of Frequency, to understand how well system-wide measures capture location-specific behavior. The results show that at a fixed inverter penetration level, placement choices alone produce substantial variation in worst-case local frequency performance. Critically, aggregate frequency assessment was found to mask the vast majority of this placement-induced variation, suggesting that relying solely on aggregate metrics could obscure whether a given inverter placement is beneficial or harmful for local frequency stability.

Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space

Chanasorn Kongprachaya, Nicola Bartolo, Daniele Bertacca et al.. Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper theoretically investigates whether Redshift Space Distortions (RSD) could mimic parity violation signals in the galaxy four-point correlation function, a question motivated by recent observational claims of cosmological parity violation. The authors analyze the perturbative relativistic galaxy number density using Standard Eulerian Perturbation Theory to third order, incorporating tidal bias, wide-angle effects through a local line-of-sight definition for each galaxy, and generalization to an arbitrary cosmological background. By expanding the four-point correlation function in spherical harmonics, they show that angular momentum selection rules encoded in Wigner symbols force all parity-odd contributions from RSD to vanish upon averaging over all line-of-sight orientations, in both the plane-parallel and wide-angle regimes. This result holds up to the leading order of general relativistic corrections and applies to isotropic estimators of the four-point correlation function. The finding is significant because it supports interpreting any detected parity violation signal as physically meaningful rather than as a systematic artefact introduced by RSD.

EPIGRIDS: Documentation and Validation of Seven Open Synthetic Transmission Grid Cases

Jonathan M. Snodgrass, Scott Greene, Thomas J. Overbye et al.. EPIGRIDS: Documentation and Validation of Seven Open Synthetic Transmission Grid Cases. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper documents and validates a collection of seven open synthetic transmission grid test cases called EPIGRIDS, constructed using an optimization-based method rather than the graph-statistics approach underlying most existing large public synthetic grids. The cases span a wide range of scales and include geolocated substations, multiple voltage levels, and physically derived line parameters, with the larger cases accompanied by a year of hourly bus-level load data. Validation testing confirms that every case solves an alternating-current power flow from its stored operating point, and full single-outage contingency analyses complete successfully across all cases. Optimal power flow benchmarks also largely succeed, with only one base case and a small number of its operating points failing to solve in polar form. Comparison against real interconnection data reveals that the Eastern and Western cases are somewhat denser than their real-world counterparts, and graph-statistics synthetic grids built on the same geographic footprints more closely match published realism criteria, suggesting the optimization-based construction method trades some topological realism for other design objectives.

Verifying the High-Energy Tail in Bi-Kappa Particle Loading: Three-Dimensional Tests, Velocity Bounds, and Finite Precision

Keheng Zhu, Yuri A. Omelchenko. Verifying the High-Energy Tail in Bi-Kappa Particle Loading: Three-Dimensional Tests, Velocity Bounds, and Finite Precision. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the accurate numerical generation of particle velocities following the bi-Kappa distribution, which describes anisotropic plasma populations with suprathermal power-law tails used in kinetic simulations of space plasmas. The authors identify two key numerical problems: floating-point underflow that eliminates the largest velocity draws when the kappa parameter approaches one-half, and velocity bounds that, even when rejecting only a small fraction of draws, can substantially alter the high-energy tail of the loaded distribution. To address underflow, they evaluate a Gamma-ratio construction on a logarithmic scale, which preserves draws in both single and double precision down to very near the theoretical minimum kappa value. They demonstrate quantitatively that a velocity bound set at twenty thermal speeds can reject a seemingly negligible fraction of draws while reducing a high-percentile speed by over ten percent, cautioning against naive application of such bounds. Using chi-squared tests spanning a wide range of kappa values, including regimes where the distribution's second moments do not exist, the authors verify that their loader correctly reproduces the target distribution in terms of speed, direction, and their joint statistics out to very high percentiles.

VellSSLH: Sound Type-Guided Selective Speculative Load Hardening for LLVM IR

Swarn Priya. VellSSLH: Sound Type-Guided Selective Speculative Load Hardening for LLVM IR. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the problem of secret leakage in constant-time cryptographic programs during speculative execution, where mispredicted branches can expose secret data through observable side channels. The authors developed VellSSLH, a selective speculative load hardening tool that operates at the LLVM IR level, using programmer annotations to distinguish public from secret inputs and inferring through type analysis which values require protection during speculation. The approach inserts misspeculation-flag operations only where necessary, avoiding the overhead of blanket protection, and is accompanied by VellSCT, a checker that validates whether the resulting IR satisfies speculative constant-time security. The security guarantees are formally mechanized in Rocq by extending Vellvm's LLVM semantics with speculative execution, attacker directives, and leakage observations, with proofs that accepted programs satisfy speculative constant-time security and that a reference transformation preserves architectural behavior. Evaluation on cryptographic benchmarks shows that VellSSLH achieves meaningfully lower runtime overhead than both standard LLVM speculative load hardening and an alternative called Ultimate SLH, with particularly large improvements on certain primitives like X25519.

Callo: Serving Cache-Miss Requests with Speculative Decoding on Invalidated KV under Prefill-Decode Disaggregation

Jianjun Ou, Matthew Lentz. Callo: Serving Cache-Miss Requests with Speculative Decoding on Invalidated KV under Prefill-Decode Disaggregation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses a latency problem in large language model serving systems where agent sessions with frequent context edits invalidate cached key-value states, forcing requests to stall while re-prefill is performed before decoding can begin. The authors developed Callo, a disaggregated serving system that repurposes these invalidated KV caches as speculative drafts during the waiting period, then verifies and corrects the speculative tokens against the exact recomputed KV after prefill completes. The system improves resource efficiency by scheduling speculative work during decode-resource underutilization and preempting speculations when drafts are unlikely to be accepted. Evaluation on a two-GPU testbed using agent coding sessions across multiple model families showed modest but consistent end-to-end speedups over a standard serving baseline, with cluster-level simulations on real-world traces indicating these gains extend to larger deployments.

Viral suppression among adolescents and adults living with human immunodeficiency virus in Ethiopia during the test-and-treat era.

Jarso H, Asu R, Negawo S et al.. Viral suppression among adolescents and adults living with human immunodeficiency virus in Ethiopia during the test-and-treat era.. PloS one. 2026

This retrospective cohort study examined the rate of viral suppression and its predictors among adolescents and adults living with HIV in Ethiopia during the test-and-treat era, spanning from early antiretroviral therapy initiation through two years of follow-up. Using proportional stratified random sampling and Cox proportional hazards regression, the researchers analyzed patients aged fifteen and older who initiated antiretroviral therapy at public health facilities in Shashemene City. Viral suppression was achieved by roughly seventy percent of patients, falling short of the global target, with a median time to suppression of approximately nine months. Rapid antiretroviral therapy initiation was strongly associated with earlier viral suppression, while undernutrition, poor medication adherence, and ambulatory or bedridden functional status at baseline were each independently associated with lower likelihood of achieving suppression. The authors conclude that improving timely treatment initiation, supporting adherence, and addressing nutritional status are critical strategies for closing the gap between observed outcomes and international suppression targets in this population.

Evaluating long-term programmatic outcomes of patients with advanced HIV disease: Insights from 12 years of antiretroviral therapy scale-up in rural KwaZulu-Natal.

Kitenge MK, Fatti G, Laxmeshwar C et al.. Evaluating long-term programmatic outcomes of patients with advanced HIV disease: Insights from 12 years of antiretroviral therapy scale-up in rural KwaZulu-Natal.. PloS one. 2026

This study examined long-term treatment outcomes among patients with advanced HIV disease who initiated antiretroviral therapy at rural public health facilities in KwaZulu-Natal, South Africa, over a twelve-year period spanning multiple policy eras. Using a retrospective cohort design with routine clinical data, researchers assessed retention in care and virological outcomes at multiple time points, applying competing risk and Poisson regression models to identify factors associated with loss to follow-up and viral suppression. Retention in care was high, remaining above eighty percent at four years, and viral load suppression exceeded eighty percent among those with available test results, with outcomes improving markedly during the Universal Test and Treat era compared with earlier periods. Men and those with very low CD4 counts faced greater risk of loss to follow-up, while women and patients who initiated treatment rapidly were more likely to achieve virological suppression. The authors conclude that despite encouraging retention and suppression rates, viral load testing uptake was suboptimal and a substantial gap remained in achieving undetectable viral loads, highlighting the need for male-targeted interventions, earlier treatment initiation, and broader implementation of the World Health Organization advanced HIV disease care package.

malaria rapid diagnostic test

Topic mode · generated November 16, 2026 at 21:57 UTC · 14 items

A qualitative study exploring factors associated with the accuracy of recording malaria rapid diagnostic test results in two high-burden regions of Côte d'Ivoire.

Kouakou OMA, Bédia-Tanoh VA, Arnzen A et al.. A qualitative study exploring factors associated with the accuracy of recording malaria rapid diagnostic test results in two high-burden regions of Côte d'Ivoire.. Malaria journal. 2026

This study investigated why healthcare workers in two high-burden regions of Côte d'Ivoire sometimes fail to accurately record malaria rapid diagnostic test results, a problem that undermines malaria surveillance. Using a qualitative approach embedded in a broader mixed-methods evaluation, researchers photographed RDTs performed by healthcare workers and had an external panel independently interpret the results, then conducted semi-structured interviews with workers showing high versus low agreement between their recorded and independently verified readings. The key differentiator between groups was not knowledge or resource constraints but rather differing levels of confidence in negative RDT results, particularly fear that a negative test might miss true malaria in symptomatic patients. Workers with low recording accuracy more often reported deliberate falsification—changing negative results to positive or omitting antimalarial prescriptions from registers—to reconcile documentation with their clinical decisions to treat despite a negative result. The authors conclude that interventions should focus on building clinical decision-making confidence for RDT-negative fevers, expanding non-malarial diagnostic options, and creating documentation systems that allow transparent recording of non-adherence without incentivizing misreporting.

Comparative evaluation of a multi-antigen malaria rapid diagnostic test against qPCR and microscopy in a co-endemic area of Southern Ethiopia.

Bogale A, Mitiku A, Tefera S et al.. Comparative evaluation of a multi-antigen malaria rapid diagnostic test against qPCR and microscopy in a co-endemic area of Southern Ethiopia.. Scientific reports. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of a multi-antigen rapid diagnostic test for malaria in a region of southern Ethiopia where both *Plasmodium falciparum* and *P. vivax* co-circulate, and where conventional HRP2-based tests are compromised by low parasite densities and gene deletions. Researchers enrolled febrile patients from health facilities during peak transmission season and tested blood samples using the new Bioline multi-antigen RDT, conventional RDT, expert microscopy, and quantitative PCR. The multi-antigen RDT showed high specificity and near-perfect agreement with microscopy, but its sensitivity dropped notably when qPCR served as the reference standard, revealing that submicroscopic, low-density infections were frequently missed. Molecular testing detected the highest overall malaria prevalence, identifying more cases of each species and mixed infections than either microscopy or the RDT. The findings support the multi-antigen RDT for routine clinical diagnosis but underscore the need for more sensitive tools in surveillance and control efforts within endemic settings.
