The anthropogenic fingerprint on emerging infectious diseases

Rory Gibb, Sadie J. Ryan, David M. Pigott et al.. The anthropogenic fingerprint on emerging infectious diseases. Nature. 2026

This paper investigated how human-driven environmental and social changes shape the geographic distribution of outbreak risk across a broad range of infectious diseases. The researchers compiled a large database of outbreak event records covering dozens of diseases and systematically evaluated multiple hypothesized drivers—including land use change, climate variables, agricultural intensification, and healthcare access—while accounting for biases in disease detection and reporting. They found that outbreak risk tends to be highest in mosaic landscapes where people and livestock coexist near forests and fragmented ecosystems, and that these factors along with long-term precipitation declines are shared drivers across several vector-borne diseases, while directly transmitted zoonoses such as Ebola and mpox share few common environmental drivers. Healthcare access emerged as a consistently important factor across diseases, with reporting rates declining substantially for each additional hour of travel time to a health facility. The authors conclude that infectious disease emergence is fundamentally multi-causal, that no universal prevention strategy is adequate, and that ecosystem-based public health interventions must be tailored to specific disease systems and paired with strengthened health infrastructure and One Health surveillance.

Global trends in the burden of malaria: mapping the prevalence, incidence and mortality of Plasmodium falciparum and Plasmodium vivax in the era of climate change and emerging drug resistance

Ebrahim Abbasi. Global trends in the burden of malaria: mapping the prevalence, incidence and mortality of Plasmodium falciparum and Plasmodium vivax in the era of climate change and emerging drug resistance. BMJ Global Health. 2026

This paper examined the global burden of malaria caused by the two principal parasite species, focusing on how climate change, antimalarial drug resistance, insecticide resistance, and vector adaptation are reshaping transmission and control challenges. The authors conducted a systematic review of published evidence on prevalence, incidence, mortality, geographical distribution, and environmental and resistance-related determinants, drawing on major bibliographic databases alongside surveillance and modelling sources. They found substantial geographical heterogeneity in disease burden, with one species dominating in sub-Saharan Africa and the other contributing considerably elsewhere, while temperature, precipitation, and humidity were identified as key drivers of transmission suitability. Resistance to artemisinin and partner drugs, challenges in achieving radical cure for one species, and insecticide resistance combined with behavioural changes in mosquito vectors were highlighted as major emerging threats. The authors conclude that eliminating malaria will require integrated, species-specific strategies that are simultaneously sensitive to climate variability and informed by resistance patterns, underpinned by robust surveillance and resilient health systems.

The spatial proteome of the Plasmodium falciparum schizont illuminates the composition and evolutionary trajectories of its organelles

Scott A. Chisholm, Victor Martin Quintana Flores, Alison C. Kemp et al.. The spatial proteome of the Plasmodium falciparum schizont illuminates the composition and evolutionary trajectories of its organelles. Nature Communications. 2026

This paper investigated the subcellular organization of proteins in the malaria parasite *Plasmodium falciparum* during its invasive erythrocytic stage, addressing the longstanding problem that a large fraction of the parasite's proteins lack known locations or functions. The researchers applied a spatial proteomics approach called hyperLOPIT, combined with supervised machine learning, to generate high-resolution protein localization maps for thousands of proteins expressed in late schizont stages. The resulting dataset resolved dozens of distinct cellular compartments, including specialized invasion-related organelles and exported sites within the host red blood cell, enabling the classification of a large proportion of previously unlocalized proteins. By integrating these localization data with comparative genomic analyses, the study also illuminated how the spatial organization of proteins reflects evolutionary pressures that have shaped *Plasmodium* biology and its adaptation to parasitic life. The work provides a comprehensive spatial proteome resource that advances understanding of parasite cell biology and the evolutionary trajectories of its organelles.

Evolving Machine Learning in Non-Stationary Environments: A Unified Survey of Drift, Forgetting, and Adaptation

Ignacio Cabrera Martin, Subhaditya Mukherjee, Almas Baimagambetov et al.. Evolving Machine Learning in Non-Stationary Environments: A Unified Survey of Drift, Forgetting, and Adaptation. Applied Artificial Intelligence. 2026

This survey examines the challenge of deploying machine learning models in non-stationary environments where underlying data distributions change over time. The authors propose evolving machine learning as a unifying conceptual framework that integrates four interrelated challenges: data drift, concept drift, catastrophic forgetting, and skewed learning. They systematically reviewed a large body of recent literature, categorizing methods across supervised, unsupervised, and semi-supervised learning settings. A central contribution is the synthesis of these challenges into a coherent analytical perspective that highlights their structural interdependencies and the fundamental tension between stability and adaptability in continuously updating systems. The survey also reviews evaluation protocols, benchmark datasets, and real-world applications, identifying methodological limitations and open research directions for building robust, scalable adaptive learning systems.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the problem of associating space-based radio frequency vessel detections with Automatic Identification System trajectories to monitor maritime activity and detect vessels operating covertly. Following a structured data science methodology, the researchers developed a Pair Hidden Markov Model that uses temporal and spatial information to align individual RF detections with AIS tracks while accounting for noise and timing discrepancies. The model was evaluated on real AIS data augmented with synthetic RF detections, and its performance was benchmarked against nearest-neighbor baseline methods through bias analyses examining trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and spatial separation. A log-odds scoring variant that contrasts the full matching model against a null model substantially improved both precision and dark vessel detection capability, though it retained some directional biases. Despite these improvements, the probabilistic model approaches but does not surpass a time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline, suggesting that this hand-crafted single-detection formulation has not yet fully captured the complexity needed to outperform simpler heuristics.

Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data

Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri. Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether recent hints of non-standard cosmological expansion are accompanied by modifications to gravitational dynamics, going beyond simple dark energy parameterizations. The authors perform a joint, non-parametric reconstruction of the effective dark energy density and two gravitational response functions governing matter clustering and lensing, using a combination of CMB, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation, supernova, and large-scale structure datasets. They find a persistent feature in the dark energy density at intermediate redshifts corresponding to a crossing of the phantom divide, a result that is robust across different data combinations and persists when gravitational modifications are allowed. The gravitational response functions show suggestive enhancements over a similar redshift range, though these are less statistically significant, and including large-scale structure data instead favors a suppressed lensing response. The authors conclude that current deviations from the standard cosmological model are most clearly localized in the expansion history, while correlated modifications to gravitational dynamics remain plausible but require future Stage IV surveys to confirm or rule out.

Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction

Abhilekha Dalal, Avishek Das, Samatha Ereshi Akkamahadevi. Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated how convolutional neural networks exploit contextual background information when classifying agricultural images, and whether the internal representations of hidden neurons can be made interpretable through a technique called Concept Induction. The researchers applied this approach across multiple agricultural datasets spanning different levels of contextual bias, examining several CNN architectures at varying network depths and patch locations within images. Quantitative image descriptors were encoded in a knowledge graph and used as background knowledge for Concept Induction, which generated logical expressions characterizing when individual neurons activate, with these expressions then statistically validated on held-out images. The study found that a substantial proportion of neuron-feature relationships were validated, with intermediate network depths showing the highest validation rates and texture-related features such as edge, Gabor, and Tamura descriptors being most frequently associated with neuron activations. Notably, stronger contextual bias in a dataset did not correspond to higher rates of interpretable neuron-feature relationships, suggesting that a network's behavioral reliance on background context and the semantic interpretability of its hidden neurons reflect distinct and separable aspects of CNN behavior.

KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids

Ivan Kuznetsov, Alexey Androsov, Dmitrii Pantiukhin et al.. KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces KELP, a Lagrangian particle tracking software designed for unstructured ocean model meshes and HEALPix equal-area grids, with a particular emphasis on compatibility with coding agents driven by language models. The software computes two- and three-dimensional particle trajectories on triangular, quadrilateral, and mixed meshes, as well as on the HEALPix grid format used by kilometre-scale ocean digital twins, and includes structured documentation and task instructions that enable language models to autonomously set up, run, and process tracking experiments. Validation showed that KELP reproduced particle distributions consistent with existing trackers, and that trajectories computed on HEALPix grids converged toward those on the native model mesh as grid resolution increased, demonstrating that HEALPix output is suitable for Lagrangian ensemble statistics when original mesh data are unavailable. The agent-based workflow substantially improved the completeness of study configurations generated by multiple language models, and most experiment preparation was completed within hours. Practical scalability was demonstrated through a multi-decade Arctic ocean transport calculation and a stress test tracking an extremely large number of particles on a high-resolution HEALPix mesh.

Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection

Donghoon Lee, Jusung Park, Shinjin Kang. Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the problem of detecting out-of-distribution objects in real-world object detection systems, framing the decision for each detected bounding box as a statistical hypothesis test where the null hypothesis assumes the box contains an in-distribution object. The authors identify that the null distribution over box features varies with box size and border contact, so valid comparisons must be made only against in-distribution boxes sharing these same geometric properties, yielding distribution-free bounds on false-alarm rates. They further argue that features extracted after class-specific classification layers are suboptimal for this test, since those layers discard information about unknown objects, and instead propose reading features from the final backbone stage before class-specific processing. Based on these principles, they introduce PLUM, a training-free scoring method that pools backbone features within a box and measures their distance to in-distribution classes conditioned on matching box geometry. PLUM achieves leading out-of-distribution detection performance on standard benchmarks without requiring external models, outperforming compared methods on both PASCAL-VOC and BDD100K datasets.

On numerical issues of divergences between nearly equal distributions

Brandon Amos. On numerical issues of divergences between nearly equal distributions. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates numerical instability problems that arise when computing divergences between categorical distributions that are nearly identical. Building on prior work identifying similar issues in softmax and soft cross-entropy operations, the authors demonstrate that forward and reverse Kullback-Leibler divergences, generalized Jensen-Shannon divergence, Monte Carlo KL estimates, and entropy all suffer from rounding and cancellation errors under these near-equality conditions. These instabilities affect both the forward computation and gradient calculations, with practical consequences for machine learning applications that use divergences in optimization objectives, including knowledge distillation, reinforcement learning, and proximal methods. The authors derive numerically stable reformulations for each of these operations and provide concrete implementations validated through property-based testing.

Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.

Walzl G, McHugh TD, Chakaya J et al.. Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.. The Lancet. Microbe. 2027

This paper reviews advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarker research over roughly a five-year period, synthesizing the scientific evidence that has informed updated WHO policy guidance consolidating recommendations for detecting tuberculosis disease and drug resistance. The review covers expanded deployment of decentralised molecular diagnostic tools, including low-complexity automated and manual nucleic acid amplification tests, as well as digital chest radiography supported by computer-aided detection as a core screening and triage tool. Innovations highlighted include novel specimen types such as tongue swabs, sputum pooling for programmatic efficiency, and improved bacteriological confirmation in children through non-sputum specimens like stool and nasopharyngeal samples. Rapid molecular drug-resistance testing and targeted next-generation sequencing have also advanced, while urine lipoarabinomannan assays offer diagnostic gains specifically in people with HIV and advanced disease. Despite these developments, significant performance, implementation, and equity gaps persist, and no biomarker yet meets WHO target product profile criteria for a point-of-care test capable of reliably distinguishing infection from disease, including subclinical tuberculosis.

Microfluidic Profiling of an Immune Cell-Associated Extracellular Vesicle Signature for Active Tuberculosis Diagnosis and Treatment Monitoring.

Lee EJ, Nie C, Park S et al.. Microfluidic Profiling of an Immune Cell-Associated Extracellular Vesicle Signature for Active Tuberculosis Diagnosis and Treatment Monitoring.. Journal of extracellular vesicles. 2026

This paper investigated whether extracellular vesicles carrying innate immune cell-associated surface proteins could serve as blood-based biomarkers for active tuberculosis, addressing the diagnostic gap in cases where conventional pathogen detection fails. The researchers used whole-blood transcriptome profiling to identify four innate immune cell-associated surface markers, then validated an extracellular vesicle signature using bead-based immunoprecipitation before implementing it on an eight-channel microfluidic chip capable of analyzing a small plasma volume in under thirty minutes. Testing this platform in a clinical cohort demonstrated excellent discriminatory performance for distinguishing active tuberculosis from healthy controls and from other respiratory diseases, while also providing complementary diagnostic information when conventional tests yielded negative results. Longitudinal tracking further revealed that the signature levels declined markedly following anti-tuberculosis treatment, suggesting utility for monitoring therapeutic response. The study concludes that profiling these tuberculosis-associated immune cell extracellular vesicles represents a rapid, minimally invasive approach with strong potential for both diagnosis and treatment monitoring.

Diagnosis of Extrapulmonary Tuberculosis at the Interface of Pathogen Detection and Host Response: Molecular, Microbiological, and Histopathological Advances.

Liu Q, Jiang Y. Diagnosis of Extrapulmonary Tuberculosis at the Interface of Pathogen Detection and Host Response: Molecular, Microbiological, and Histopathological Advances.. Pathogens (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This review examines the persistent diagnostic challenge posed by extrapulmonary tuberculosis, which disproportionately affects children and immunocompromised individuals and is complicated by low bacterial burden, diverse anatomical sites, and nonspecific clinical presentations. The authors synthesized evidence across microbiological, molecular, sequencing-based, and histopathological approaches, integrating these with an understanding of site-specific immunopathology including granuloma biology and caseous necrosis. Diagnostic tools reviewed include established molecular assays such as Xpert MTB/RIF and Ultra, metagenomic next-generation sequencing, and emerging host-response biomarkers alongside artificial intelligence-assisted histopathology, with comparative analysis spanning osteoarticular, lymph node, pleural, central nervous system, and disseminated disease. The review proposes tiered diagnostic algorithms tailored to resource-limited settings where most cases occur, and identifies gaps in current international guidance. The authors argue that aligning diagnostic modality selection with anatomical site, specimen quality, pathological subtype, and available laboratory capacity can reduce time to diagnosis and support host-directed diagnostic strategies.

Mycobacteriophages: An Integrated Platform for Rapid Diagnosis and Targeted Therapy of Tuberculosis Infection.

Avdeev VV, Vladimirsky MA, Eliseev PI et al.. Mycobacteriophages: An Integrated Platform for Rapid Diagnosis and Targeted Therapy of Tuberculosis Infection.. Microorganisms. 2026

This paper developed and evaluated an integrated platform using the lytic mycobacteriophage D29 to address the growing challenge of multidrug-resistant and extensively drug-resistant tuberculosis. The platform combined a rapid diagnostic module that uses multiplex real-time PCR to detect phage replication in drug-resistant bacterial isolates, thereby assessing antimycobacterial susceptibility within five days, with a liposomal therapeutic formulation designed to deliver the phage directly to the site of infection. Therapeutic efficacy was tested in a mouse model of pulmonary tuberculosis using both aerosol and intranasal delivery routes, with bacterial burden in the lungs assessed at multiple timepoints. The diagnostic component demonstrated high sensitivity and specificity across multiple anti-tuberculosis drugs, and the lyophilised components remained stable for an extended period. Both delivery routes significantly reduced bacterial burden compared to controls, with aerosol administration showing a trend toward greater efficacy, and pharmacokinetic analysis revealed exclusive lung tropism with evidence of in vivo phage replication in infected tissue and no significant toxicity or immunogenicity, supporting potential clinical translation.

The changing landscape of diagnosing HIV-associated tuberculosis.

Maartens G. The changing landscape of diagnosing HIV-associated tuberculosis.. South African medical journal = Suid-Afrikaanse tydskrif vir geneeskunde. 2026

This narrative review examines how the diagnosis of HIV-associated tuberculosis has evolved from 1990 to the present, focusing on adults living with HIV in whom tuberculosis remains the leading cause of hospitalisation and death. The authors describe the inherent diagnostic challenges in this population, including lower bacillary load in sputum, high rates of extrapulmonary and disseminated disease, and nonspecific clinical features that overlap with other opportunistic infections. Historically, smear microscopy was the only rapid diagnostic tool available, but its reduced sensitivity in people with HIV led to both significant underdiagnosis and overdiagnosis with consequent harms. The introduction of nucleic acid amplification tests and the urine lipoarabinomannan lateral flow assay, alongside wider antiretroviral therapy use, has substantially improved diagnosis and contributed to reductions in tuberculosis incidence and mortality. Nevertheless, the review concludes that no currently available rapid test achieves ideal sensitivity, and only urine lipoarabinomannan detection combines affordability with point-of-care suitability, underscoring the continued need for novel accurate and accessible diagnostic tools.

Flooding spread of manipulated knowledge in LLM-based multi-agent communities

Tianjie Ju, Yiting Wang, Yi Hua et al.. Flooding spread of manipulated knowledge in LLM-based multi-agent communities. Science China Information Sciences. 2026

This paper investigated security vulnerabilities in large language model-based multi-agent systems, specifically focusing on how manipulated knowledge can propagate through networks of communicating agents. The researchers designed a two-stage attack methodology combining Persuasiveness Injection and Manipulated Knowledge Injection, which causes agents to spread both counterfactual and toxic information during normal communication without relying on explicit adversarial prompt attacks and without degrading the agents' general capabilities. Experiments demonstrated that these manipulations can persist through retrieval-augmented generation frameworks, where benign agents store and later retrieve corrupted conversation histories, amplifying the spread of harmful content. To counter these risks, the authors proposed two defense strategies involving system prompts that encourage critical knowledge verification and the introduction of supervisory agents to oversee agent interactions, both of which measurably reduced the rate of manipulation spread. The work concludes that LLM-based multi-agent communities harbor critical and underexplored security vulnerabilities that demand prompt adoption of targeted defenses by platform developers.

Efficient differential expression analysis of large-scale single-cell transcriptomics data using Dreamlet

Gabriel E. Hoffman, Donghoon Lee, Jaroslav M. Bendl et al.. Efficient differential expression analysis of large-scale single-cell transcriptomics data using Dreamlet. Nature Communications. 2026

This paper addresses the challenge of performing differential expression analysis on large-scale single-cell and single-nucleus transcriptomics datasets spanning hundreds of subjects and millions of cells. The authors developed an open-source R package called dreamlet, which employs a pseudobulk approach based on precision-weighted linear mixed models to identify cell-type-specific genes differentially expressed in association with traits across subjects. The tool was designed to handle complex statistical models while controlling false positive rates, and it achieves substantially faster runtimes and lower memory usage compared to existing workflows. Performance was validated on published datasets as well as a novel large-scale dataset of over a million single nuclei from postmortem brain tissue of Alzheimer's disease cases and controls. The work demonstrates that dreamlet enables statistically rigorous and computationally scalable differential expression analyses in large cohort-based single-cell studies of human disease.

MedicalAgentsBench for complex medical reasoning: Comparing internalized reasoning models versus externalized agent-based frameworks

Yanjun Shao, Xiangru Tang, Jiwoong Sohn et al.. MedicalAgentsBench for complex medical reasoning: Comparing internalized reasoning models versus externalized agent-based frameworks. Patterns. 2026

This paper examined whether internalized reasoning in large language models and externalized agent-based scaffolding represent competing or complementary approaches to complex medical question answering. The researchers constructed a benchmark of challenging clinical questions drawn from multiple medical datasets using difficulty-aware curation and contamination screening, then systematically evaluated a range of internalized reasoning models, base models, and agent-based frameworks against it. They found that both approaches independently improve performance on complex medical reasoning tasks, and crucially, that their benefits are additive rather than exclusive. The best performance was achieved by combining an internalized reasoning model with an agent workflow, while cost-performance analysis revealed this combination also dominated the efficiency frontier. The study additionally notes that lightweight optimization strategies can make these gains accessible in resource-constrained settings.

Nanopore direct RNA sequencing and the epitranscriptome: Advances in mapping native RNA landscapes

Tianyuan Zhang, Jia Li, Chao Tang et al.. Nanopore direct RNA sequencing and the epitranscriptome: Advances in mapping native RNA landscapes. iMeta. 2026

This review examines nanopore direct RNA sequencing as a transformative technology for studying native RNA biology and epitranscriptomics. Unlike short-read RNA sequencing or complementary DNA-based long-read approaches, the technique sequences individual RNA molecules without reverse transcription or amplification, allowing simultaneous capture of RNA modifications, full-length transcript structures, alternative splicing, and poly-adenylation tail features from single molecules. The authors trace the biophysical principles and technological evolution of the method, compare it systematically against alternative sequencing strategies, and survey optimized experimental workflows for diverse RNA types including messenger RNA, transfer RNA, circular RNA, and small non-coding species. They also review state-of-the-art computational algorithms for modification detection, isoform reconstruction, and poly-adenylation tail inference, while emphasizing the importance of benchmarking and orthogonal validation to distinguish direct signal measurements from computational predictions. The review concludes by highlighting emerging applications in resolving complex transcriptomes, characterizing coordinated epitranscriptomic regulation, identifying disease-associated RNA signatures, and outlining remaining technical challenges for deploying the technology in precision medicine and broader biological research.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of monitoring maritime activities by fusing two complementary data sources: the Automatic Identification System, which provides vessel identification but has known vulnerabilities, and space-based radio frequency observations, which detect vessel activity independently but cannot identify ships. Following a structured data science methodology, the researchers developed a Pair Hidden Markov Model to align individual radio frequency detections with AIS vessel trajectories by jointly modeling temporal and spatial information along with noise and timing discrepancies. The approach was evaluated on real AIS data combined with synthetic radio frequency detections, benchmarked against nearest-neighbor baselines, and analyzed for systematic biases related to trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and spatial separation. A log-odds scoring variant that compares the full model against a null model without match transitions substantially improved both precision and the ability to detect vessels behaving covertly, while also reducing certain biases. However, a time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline requiring no model fitting still outperformed the proposed Pair Hidden Markov Model, indicating that this particular single-detection formulation approaches but does not surpass simpler heuristic methods.

Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data

Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri. Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether recent hints of non-standard cosmological expansion are accompanied by modifications to gravitational dynamics, going beyond simple dark energy parameterizations. The authors perform a non-parametric joint reconstruction of the effective dark energy density and two gravitational response functions governing matter clustering and gravitational lensing, using a combination of CMB, baryon acoustic oscillation, supernova, and large-scale structure datasets. They find a persistent feature in the dark energy density around a redshift of roughly half to three-quarters, consistent with a crossing of the phantom divide, which remains robust across different data combinations and when gravitational modifications are freely varied. The gravitational response functions show suggestive enhancements over a similar redshift range, though these are less statistically significant, and including large-scale structure data instead favors a suppressed lensing response. Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction

Abhilekha Dalal, Avishek Das, Samatha Ereshi Akkamahadevi. Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated how convolutional neural networks exploit contextual background information when classifying agricultural images, and whether a technique called Concept Induction can reveal what hidden neurons actually represent when processing such contextual patches. The researchers applied Concept Induction to five agricultural datasets varying in degree of contextual bias, analyzing multiple CNN architectures across different network depths and patch locations by encoding quantitative image descriptors in a knowledge graph to derive logical expressions characterizing neuron activations. A substantial portion of tested neuron-feature pairs were statistically validated, with validation rates being highest at intermediate network depths and driven most strongly by edge, Gabor, and texture-based features. Notably, the degree of contextual bias in a dataset did not straightforwardly predict how interpretable the hidden neuron representations were, suggesting that a network's behavioral reliance on background context and the semantic interpretability of its internal representations are distinct phenomena. The authors conclude that Concept Induction can usefully complement existing patch-based methods for detecting contextual bias by providing insight into what CNN hidden layers encode, rather than only measuring classification accuracy.

KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids

Ivan Kuznetsov, Alexey Androsov, Dmitrii Pantiukhin et al.. KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces KELP, a Lagrangian particle tracking software designed to follow virtual particles through ocean model velocity fields in order to study transport pathways, connectivity, and dispersion. The tool is built to operate on unstructured ocean-model meshes as well as HEALPix equal-area grids, which are used by kilometre-scale ocean digital twins, and is specifically designed to be operated by coding agents that use language models to write and execute code. The authors demonstrate that structured documentation and short task instructions called skills substantially improved the ability of multiple language models to correctly set up and run Lagrangian studies. KELP successfully reproduced particle distributions from other established trackers, and trajectories computed on HEALPix grids converged toward those on the original model mesh as grid resolution increased, supporting the use of HEALPix output for ensemble Lagrangian statistics when the native mesh is unavailable. A multidecadal simulation tracking millions of particles in the Beaufort Gyre showed that transport to Fram Strait takes years to decades, confirming the tool's applicability to large-scale oceanographic questions.

Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection

Donghoon Lee, Jusung Park, Shinjin Kang. Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the problem of detecting out-of-distribution objects in real-world object detection systems, where a model encounters objects from classes not seen during training. The authors reframe the decision on each detected bounding box as a hypothesis test, arguing that the null distribution of in-distribution boxes varies systematically with box size and contact with image borders, so each box should be compared only against in-distribution boxes sharing the same geometric properties. This box-conditional framing provides a distribution-free bound on false-alarm rates and reveals discriminative structure that a single pooled null distribution would obscure. Building on this, the authors propose PLUM, a training-free scoring method that extracts features from the last backbone stage within a box and ranks their distance to in-distribution classes relative to geometrically matched in-distribution boxes, deliberately reading features before class-specific layers to preserve information about unknown objects. PLUM achieves state-of-the-art out-of-distribution detection performance on standard benchmarks without requiring external models, outperforming compared methods on both PASCAL-VOC and BDD100K datasets.

sexually transmitted infection point-of-care testing

Topic mode · generated November 16, 2026 at 21:54 UTC · 17 items

Cost and operational impact of a molecular point-of-care test for sexually transmitted infections in the emergency department.

Paul-Ledesma A, Dashler G, Hsieh YH et al.. Cost and operational impact of a molecular point-of-care test for sexually transmitted infections in the emergency department.. The American journal of emergency medicine. 2026

This study compared centralized laboratory testing with molecular point-of-care testing for sexually transmitted infections among female emergency department patients suspected of infection, examining both costs and operational outcomes. Using decision analysis through cost tables derived from a pre-post implementation study, researchers assessed direct testing costs, follow-up costs, overtreatment and undertreatment costs, and emergency department return visits over a twelve-month horizon from both hospital and societal perspectives. Point-of-care testing carried substantially higher direct costs than centralized laboratory testing, and this difference was not offset by savings from reduced undertreatment follow-up or societal costs associated with diagnostic errors. From a societal standpoint, point-of-care testing increased overall costs and reduced hospital revenue compared with centralized testing under current pricing and reimbursement structures. However, threshold analysis suggested that hospitals with long wait times could potentially achieve net revenue gains through improved patient throughput and more efficient use of examination room capacity.

Interventions to Improve Care for Bacterial Sexually Transmitted Infections in Emergency Departments: A Narrative Review.

Andonian KS, Giri H, Goodman GR et al.. Interventions to Improve Care for Bacterial Sexually Transmitted Infections in Emergency Departments: A Narrative Review.. Sexually transmitted diseases. 2026

This narrative review examined interventions designed to improve the diagnosis, treatment, and follow-up of bacterial sexually transmitted infections—specifically chlamydia, gonorrhea, and syphilis—within emergency department settings in the United States. The authors searched the published literature over a multi-decade period and identified studies evaluating a range of approaches including universal screening, electronic health record prompts, point-of-care testing, and patient-directed tools. Universal screening substantially increased testing and case detection, electronic prompts enhanced screening uptake, and point-of-care testing improved same-day treatment while reducing unnecessary antibiotic use. Bundled interventions that addressed multiple steps in the care pathway appeared to yield the greatest overall impact, though heterogeneity among studies made direct comparisons difficult. The authors conclude that future research should prioritize implementation feasibility, sustainability, health equity, and cost-effectiveness across diverse emergency department contexts.

Community peer-led STI/HIV point-of-care testing and knowledge, attitude and practice (KAP) survey in men who have sex with men (MSM) attending an NGO-led clinic in the outskirts of Beirut (Lebanon).

Abou Shahla W, Rossoni I, Padovese V. Community peer-led STI/HIV point-of-care testing and knowledge, attitude and practice (KAP) survey in men who have sex with men (MSM) attending an NGO-led clinic in the outskirts of Beirut (Lebanon).. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This pilot study examined the utility of point-of-care testing for HIV, syphilis, and hepatitis among men who have sex with men attending a non-governmental organisation-led clinic in Beirut, Lebanon, while also assessing their sexual health knowledge, attitudes, and practices. Using a cross-sectional design over four months, researchers conducted sexual health consultations, administered STI/HIV point-of-care tests, and surveyed participants about demographics, condom use, chemsex, sexual violence, and perceived HIV-related stigma. The cohort was predominantly young, educated Lebanese men, with a notable proportion of Syrian participants and a high prevalence of HIV among attendees. Despite high levels of STI and HIV awareness, condomless sex and recreational drug use were common, and perceived stigma and community beliefs emerged as significant barriers to testing. The study concludes that integrating point-of-care testing into sexual health services enables same-day diagnosis, counselling, and linkage to care, and underscores the need to address HIV-related stigma to reduce risky behaviours in this population.

Mean-Field Limit of Non-exchangeable Multi-agent Systems Over Hypergraphs with Unbounded Rank

Nathalie Ayi, Nastassia Pouradier Duteil, David Poyato. Mean-Field Limit of Non-exchangeable Multi-agent Systems Over Hypergraphs with Unbounded Rank. Journal of Nonlinear Science. 2026

This paper studies multi-agent interacting particle systems that feature higher-order interactions, meaning individuals can interact simultaneously in groups rather than only in pairs, with the underlying network structure represented by a hypergraph rather than a standard graph. The authors consider dense hypergraphs that are nonuniform and of unbounded rank, meaning the size of interacting groups is not fixed and can grow without bound. To derive the large-population behavior of these systems, they developed a mathematical framework combining tools from partial differential equations, stochastic analysis, kinetic theory, and graph limit theory. The central result is a rigorous derivation of a mean-field limit for this class of systems, showing that when the interaction kernels are sufficiently regular, the limiting behavior is governed by a Vlasov-type equation in which the hypergraph structure is encoded by a novel object called a UR-hypergraphon (unbounded-rank hypergraphon). This limiting equation captures interactions of infinitely many orders simultaneously, extending mean-field theory to a substantially broader class of multi-agent systems than previously treated in the literature.

INSPECT-SR tool for assessing trustworthiness of randomised controlled trials

Jack Wilkinson, Calvin Heal, Ella Flemyng et al.. INSPECT-SR tool for assessing trustworthiness of randomised controlled trials. BMJ. 2026

The paper addresses the threat posed by problematic randomised controlled trials to the integrity of evidence synthesis, where flawed or fraudulent studies can distort the conclusions of systematic reviews and clinical guidelines if left undetected. To tackle this problem, the authors developed a tool called INSPECT-SR, designed to help researchers assess the trustworthiness of randomised controlled trials by identifying concerns arising from research misconduct, fraud, or honest critical errors. The paper describes the development process of INSPECT-SR and presents both the tool itself and an accompanying guidance document intended to support its use. The intended outcome is to enable researchers to detect and exclude problematic trials from systematic reviews, thereby safeguarding the reliability of evidence-based recommendations.

Flatline constructs : Gothic materialism and cybernetic theory-fiction

Mark Fisher. Flatline constructs : Gothic materialism and cybernetic theory-fiction. Warwick Research Archive Portal (University of Warwick). 2026

This thesis examines the claim that cyberpunk fiction is the supreme literary expression of postmodernism or late capitalism, subjecting it to a critical rethinking through three interrelated frameworks: cybernetics, the Gothic, and fiction. The author argues that postmodernism has been deeply preoccupied with cybernetic themes while cybernetics itself has been haunted by the Gothic, and that Baudrillard's theories of simulation suggest fiction takes on a new cultural role in an era dominated by cybernetic processes. Drawing on Deleuze and Guattari's materialist reconception of the Gothic — derived from Worringer's work on barbarian art rather than conventional literary history — the thesis develops a "hypernaturalist" framework centred on the nonorganic continuum and the breakdown of boundaries between the animate and inanimate. Key cyberpunk texts including Blade Runner, Videodrome, Neuromancer, and The Atrocity Exhibition are analysed through this lens, revealing their central concern with catatonic states, bodies that exceed their own skin, and agency without subjectivity. The thesis introduces the concept of the "flatline" — signifying both a state of unlife or undeath and a condition of radical immanence — as the convergence point where cyberpunk brings cybernetics and the Gothic together, with four chapters exploring this concept across the domains of postmodernism, the body, reproduction, and fiction itself.

The anthropogenic fingerprint on emerging infectious diseases

Rory Gibb, Sadie J. Ryan, David M. Pigott et al.. The anthropogenic fingerprint on emerging infectious diseases. Nature. 2026

This paper investigated the environmental and social drivers shaping the geographic distribution of outbreak risk across a broad range of emerging infectious diseases in the context of human-driven ecological change. The researchers compiled a large dataset of outbreak event records spanning dozens of diseases and systematically evaluated how multiple hypothesized drivers, including land use change, climate variables, agricultural intensification, and healthcare access, influence where outbreaks occur, while correcting for detection and reporting biases. They found that outbreak risk tends to be highest in mosaic landscapes where human populations and livestock coexist near forests and fragmented ecosystems, and that declining precipitation is a shared driver across several vector-borne diseases, whereas directly transmitted zoonoses share few common drivers. Healthcare access emerged as a consistently important factor, with outbreak reporting declining substantially for each additional hour of travel time from a health facility. The authors conclude that disease emergence is inherently multi-causal, that no universal prevention strategy is adequate, and that ecosystem-based public health interventions must be tailored to specific disease systems and paired with strengthened health infrastructure and One Health surveillance.

Addressing benchmarking gaps in large language models for health and medicine with dynamic red-teaming

Jiazhen Pan, Bailiang Jian, Paul Hager et al.. Addressing benchmarking gaps in large language models for health and medicine with dynamic red-teaming. Nature Health. 2026

This paper examined the safety and reliability of large language models used in health and medical contexts, arguing that current evaluation methods based on static benchmarks are inadequate for capturing real-world risks. The researchers developed a Dynamic, Automatic and Systematic red-teaming framework that uses adversarial agents to continuously probe models across four critical dimensions: robustness, privacy, bias, and hallucination. Applying this framework to a set of state-of-the-art language models revealed a stark "benchmarking gap," whereby models that performed well on standard medical question-answering benchmarks failed dramatically when subjected to dynamic adversarial testing. Failures were pervasive across all evaluated safety dimensions, with the vast majority of previously correct answers breaking down under robustness testing, and high failure rates also observed for privacy leakage, cognitive bias susceptibility, and hallucination. The authors conclude that strong performance on established static benchmarks likely reflects superficial memorization rather than genuine medical competence, and they propose their framework as a scalable, living audit system for identifying latent risks before clinical deployment.

APASL clinical practice guidelines on the management of chronic hepatitis B infection: a 2026 update

Hong You, Rakhi Maiwall, Jing Chen et al.. APASL clinical practice guidelines on the management of chronic hepatitis B infection: a 2026 update. Hepatology International. 2026

These guidelines address the ongoing public health burden of chronic hepatitis B infection in the Asia Pacific region, where the disease continues to drive significant rates of liver failure, cirrhosis, and hepatocellular carcinoma despite decades of scientific progress. The Asia Pacific Association for the Study of the Liver convened a dedicated Viral Elimination Taskforce, bringing together key opinion leaders from member countries and regions to develop updated clinical practice guidance. The taskforce incorporated evolving epidemiological trends, socioeconomic factors, and technological advances into their deliberations. The resulting guidelines provide recommendations across thirteen major areas encompassing screening, vaccination, antiviral treatment, and hepatocellular carcinoma surveillance, aimed at supporting healthcare professionals, policymakers, and patients in making practical and cost-effective decisions. The overarching goal is to reduce the disease burden of chronic hepatitis B and advance progress toward its elimination across the Asia Pacific region.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the challenge of monitoring maritime vessels by combining two complementary data sources: the Automatic Identification System, which provides vessel identification but has known vulnerabilities, and space-based radio frequency observations, which detect vessel activity independently but cannot identify ships on their own. Following a structured data science methodology, the researchers applied a Pair Hidden Markov Model to align individual radio frequency detections with AIS trajectories by jointly modeling temporal and spatial information along with noise and timing discrepancies. The approach was evaluated on real AIS data paired with synthetic radio frequency detections, with performance assessed against nearest-neighbor baselines and analyzed for systematic biases related to trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and spatial separation. A log-odds scoring variant that contrasts the full model against a null model without match transitions substantially improved both vessel-matching precision and the detection of "dark" vessels operating without AIS transponders. Despite these improvements, the proposed model approaches but does not surpass a time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline that requires no parameter fitting, suggesting that the single-detection Pair Hidden Markov Model formulation has not yet fully exploited the potential of the probabilistic framework.

Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data

Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri. Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether recent hints of non-standard cosmological expansion are accompanied by modifications to how gravity behaves, going beyond a simple dark energy equation-of-state test. The authors performed a joint, non-parametric reconstruction of the effective dark energy density alongside gravitational response functions governing matter clustering and lensing, using a combination of CMB, DESI DR2 BAO, supernova, and large-scale structure data. A persistent feature emerges in the dark energy density around intermediate redshifts, consistent with a crossing of the phantom divide, and this signal is robust across different data combinations and whether or not gravitational modifications are included. The gravitational response functions show suggestive but less significant enhancements over the same redshift range, though including large-scale structure data shifts the lensing response toward values below the standard expectation. The study concludes that current tensions with the standard cosmological model are most clearly localized in the expansion history, while correlated modifications to gravitational dynamics remain plausible but await confirmation from next-generation surveys.

Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction

Abhilekha Dalal, Avishek Das, Samatha Ereshi Akkamahadevi. Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how convolutional neural networks exploit contextual background information when classifying agricultural images, and what this behavior means for the interpretability of hidden layer representations. The researchers applied Concept Induction to hidden neuron activations on contextual image patches across five agricultural datasets, four CNN architectures, three network depths, and five patch locations, encoding quantitative image descriptors in a knowledge graph to derive logical expressions characterizing neuron behavior. These expressions were statistically validated on held-out images, with over a quarter of tested neuron-feature pairs confirmed, and validation rates were highest at intermediate network depths and for edge, Gabor, and texture features. Notably, the degree of contextual bias in a dataset did not predict how interpretable its hidden neurons were, as the moderately biased dataset yielded the highest validation rate while the most biased dataset yielded the lowest. The authors conclude that behavioral context dependence and semantic interpretability of hidden neurons reflect distinct aspects of CNN behavior, and that Concept Induction offers a valuable complement to accuracy-based methods for detecting and understanding contextual bias.

KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids

Ivan Kuznetsov, Alexey Androsov, Dmitrii Pantiukhin et al.. KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces KELP v1.0, a Lagrangian particle tracking software designed to follow virtual particles through ocean model velocity fields in order to study transport pathways, connectivity, and dispersion. The tool is specifically built to operate on unstructured ocean-model meshes and HEALPix equal-area grids, including those used by kilometre-scale digital twin ocean models, and is architected for use by coding agents — programs in which a language model writes, executes, and checks code autonomously. The authors demonstrate that structured documentation and short task instructions called "skills" substantially improved the ability of multiple language models to correctly set up and run Lagrangian studies. KELP reproduced particle distributions consistent with existing trackers, and trajectories computed on HEALPix grids converged toward those on native model meshes as grid resolution was refined, supporting the use of HEALPix output for ensemble Lagrangian statistics when the original mesh is unavailable. The software was validated through multi-decadal simulations involving tens of millions of particles, and stress-tested at exceptionally large scales, demonstrating both scientific fidelity and computational efficiency.

Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection

Donghoon Lee, Jusung Park, Shinjin Kang. Box-conditional Hypothesis Testing for Out-of-Distribution Object Detection. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the problem of detecting out-of-distribution objects in real-world object detection systems, where a model must identify objects belonging to unknown classes rather than only those seen during training. The authors reframe the OOD detection decision as a hypothesis test, recognizing that the null distribution of in-distribution boxes varies with box size and border contact, and that conditioning on these properties yields distribution-free false-alarm rate bounds while revealing separations that a single pooled null distribution obscures. Rather than reading class-specific layers that discard information about unknown objects, their method extracts features from the final backbone stage before class-specific processing. The resulting training-free scoring method, called PLUM, pools these backbone features within a detected box and ranks their distance to in-distribution classes against boxes sharing the same size and border contact characteristics. PLUM achieves leading detection performance on standard benchmarks without relying on external models, outperforming compared methods on both PASCAL-VOC and BDD100K datasets.

Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space

Chanasorn Kongprachaya, Nicola Bartolo, Daniele Bertacca et al.. Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether Redshift Space Distortions (RSD) can mimic parity violation signals in the isotropic galaxy four-point correlation function (4PCF), motivated by recent observational claims of cosmological parity violation. The authors analyze the perturbative relativistic galaxy number density using Standard Eulerian Perturbation Theory to third order, incorporating wide-angle effects, tidal bias, and an arbitrary cosmological background. By expanding the 4PCF in spherical harmonics, they demonstrate that angular momentum selection rules encoded in Wigner 3-j symbols force all parity-odd contributions from RSD to vanish when averaged over all line-of-sight orientations, in both the plane-parallel and full wide-angle cases. This result holds up to the leading order of general relativistic corrections and extends previous trispectrum analyses in Fourier space. The finding is significant for large-scale structure analyses because it confirms that any observed parity-violation signal in the isotropic 4PCF cannot be attributed to RSD as a systematic artifact.

Clinical utility of nucleic acid amplification testing for detection of sexually transmitted infections in women with symptomatic bacterial vaginosis and vulvovaginal candidiasis identified by conventional versus molecular diagnoses.

Getman D, Seña AC, Nenninger A et al.. Clinical utility of nucleic acid amplification testing for detection of sexually transmitted infections in women with symptomatic bacterial vaginosis and vulvovaginal candidiasis identified by conventional versus molecular diagnoses.. Journal of clinical microbiology. 2026

This study investigated whether the method used to diagnose bacterial vaginosis and vulvovaginal candidiasis influences the rate of co-detected sexually transmitted infections in women with symptomatic vaginitis. Researchers compared STI detection rates using nucleic acid amplification testing for four pathogens across women whose vaginal conditions had been diagnosed by clinical assessment, conventional laboratory methods, or molecular diagnostic methods. Women with a molecular diagnosis of bacterial vaginosis had significantly higher STI co-infection rates than those diagnosed by conventional laboratory or clinician-based methods, and a positive molecular vaginitis result was associated with substantially elevated relative risk for any STI. The findings indicate that an entirely molecular testing approach to vaginitis and STI diagnosis yields better STI case-finding than combining molecular STI tests with conventional or clinician-based vaginitis diagnoses. The authors conclude that unified molecular testing on a single specimen improves risk stratification, supports guideline-aligned care, and streamlines laboratory workflows.

Pre-exposure prophylaxis adherence and risks of HIV and sexually transmitted infection among adult men before vs during the COVID-19 pandemic.

Unigwe IF, Jenjai C, Goodin AJ et al.. Pre-exposure prophylaxis adherence and risks of HIV and sexually transmitted infection among adult men before vs during the COVID-19 pandemic.. Journal of managed care & specialty pharmacy. 2026

This study examined how the COVID-19 pandemic affected pre-exposure prophylaxis (PrEP) adherence patterns and subsequent HIV and sexually transmitted infection rates among adult men who newly initiated PrEP. Using a retrospective cohort analysis of commercially insured individuals from an administrative claims database spanning before and during the pandemic, the researchers applied group-based trajectory modeling to characterize adherence patterns and statistical modeling to compare HIV incidence and STI rates between the two groups. Those who initiated PrEP during the pandemic showed markedly worse adherence trajectories, with early discontinuation being the most common pattern, compared to a predominantly high-adherence pattern seen in the pre-pandemic group. Initiating PrEP during the pandemic was associated with a significantly higher risk of HIV acquisition, while STI rates were similar between the two periods. The findings underscore the need for targeted strategies to sustain access to and continuity of HIV prevention services during public health emergencies.
