Next-generation CRISPR-based molecular diagnostics for veterinary parasitology: From laboratory innovation to one health surveillance.

Basit A, Hanif M. Next-generation CRISPR-based molecular diagnostics for veterinary parasitology: From laboratory innovation to one health surveillance.. Molecular and biochemical parasitology. 2026

This review examines the application of CRISPR-based diagnostic technologies to veterinary parasitology, addressing limitations of conventional methods such as poor sensitivity, low specificity, and reliance on specialized laboratory infrastructure. The authors critically survey how CRISPR systems utilizing Cas12, Cas13, and emerging Cas14 effectors can detect protozoan, helminthic, and arthropod-borne parasites with high sensitivity and speed. The review describes how these platforms are enhanced through integration with isothermal amplification techniques, lateral flow assays, fluorescence detection, and portable biosensors to improve field deployability. Current obstacles to wider adoption—including assay standardization, multiplex detection, regulatory approval, and commercialization pathways—are systematically discussed alongside future directions such as amplification-free assays and artificial intelligence-assisted diagnostics. The authors conclude that CRISPR-based diagnostics hold strong potential to advance point-of-care parasite surveillance and support One Health disease control strategies across livestock, companion animals, wildlife, and public health contexts.

Scaffold RNA Reengineering for a Leakage-Suppressed CRISPR/Cas12a Cascade System Toward Precise Nucleic Acid Diagnostics.

Li X, Hu H, Jiang X et al.. Scaffold RNA Reengineering for a Leakage-Suppressed CRISPR/Cas12a Cascade System Toward Precise Nucleic Acid Diagnostics.. Advanced science (Weinheim, Baden-Wurttemberg, Germany). 2026

This paper addressed the challenge of high background signal (leakage) in amplification-free CRISPR-based nucleic acid diagnostics, which limits sensitivity and precision. The researchers engineered the CRISPR/Cas12a guide RNA by adding a DNA extension to the scaffold RNA's three-prime end, creating a conformational switch that introduces steric hindrance to keep the system inhibited until a specific target is present. This redesigned guide RNA acts as a threshold-controlled molecular switch that shifts Cas12a from an inhibited to an activated state only upon target recognition, suppressing the self-activation that typically causes background accumulation in cascaded CRISPR systems. The resulting cascade system, called CALCas, achieved femtomolar sensitivity and enabled rapid detection of HPV16 and HPV18 in under thirty minutes without nucleic acid amplification. The authors conclude that this DNA-mediated allosteric regulation strategy provides a new avenue for reprogramming Cas12a enzymatic activity with stable baseline signals suitable for precise quantitative diagnostics.

Point-of-care testing in the diagnosis of infectious diseases.

Fang Q, Lin Y, Hu G et al.. Point-of-care testing in the diagnosis of infectious diseases.. Frontiers in microbiology. 2026

This review examines point-of-care testing as a diagnostic strategy for infectious diseases, motivated by the growing global burden of such diseases and the escalating threat of antimicrobial resistance. The authors surveyed the core technical platforms underpinning current point-of-care technologies, including lateral flow assays, chemiluminescence immunoassays, isothermal amplification, CRISPR-based diagnostics, and microfluidic chip systems, as well as their application across respiratory, bloodstream, gastrointestinal, urinary tract, and sexually transmitted infections. They assessed the clinical applicability of both immunochromatographic and molecular diagnostic modalities, while also cataloguing limitations related to detection sensitivity, quality control, cost, and integration into clinical workflows. The review finds that advances in nanomaterials, microfluidic engineering, and artificial intelligence, alongside improved industry standardization, are driving enhanced performance and intelligent detection capabilities. The authors conclude that point-of-care testing holds strong promise as a core diagnostic tool for precision clinical management and public health epidemic control.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

Yuqing Liu, Kun Zhu, Weijun Peng et al.. Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines how the integration of multiomics technologies and artificial intelligence is reshaping precision drug discovery and translational medicine. The authors synthesize how genome-wide association studies, transcriptomics, proteomics, and metabolomics generate deep insights into disease mechanisms, while deep learning architectures are uniquely suited to extract meaningful patterns from these complex, high-dimensional datasets. The combined framework is shown to accelerate target identification, lead optimization, and clinical trial design across therapeutic areas including oncology, neurodegenerative diseases, and cardiovascular conditions. The review also addresses persistent challenges such as harmonizing disparate data streams, ensuring reproducibility, and reducing algorithmic bias. The authors argue that this technological convergence enables more precise therapeutic interventions and represents a foundational shift toward data-driven, bench-to-clinic translational medicine.

Cuproptosis-immunity crosstalk informs strategy to overcome immunotherapy resistance

Guang Lei, Zhengze Lu, Zhihao Xu et al.. Cuproptosis-immunity crosstalk informs strategy to overcome immunotherapy resistance. Cell. 2026

This paper investigated the relationship between cuproptosis, a copper-dependent form of cell death mediated by ferredoxin 1 and protein lipoylation, and antitumor immune responses. The researchers found that CD8+ T cell-mediated immunity increases tumor cell susceptibility to cuproptosis, while cuproptotic tumor cells reciprocally stimulate antitumor immunity by releasing damage-associated molecular patterns that activate dendritic cells. A key mechanistic finding is that interferon-gamma produced by CD8+ T cells upregulates ferredoxin 1 expression in tumor cells through the STAT1-IRF1 signaling axis, thereby sensitizing those cells to cuproptosis. Building on this bidirectional crosstalk, the authors demonstrated across multiple preclinical models that combining a cuproptosis inducer with anti-PD-L1 therapy can overcome resistance to immune checkpoint blockade. These findings establish cuproptosis as a form of immunogenic cell death and propose targeting this pathway as a strategy to enhance immunotherapy efficacy in resistant tumors.

Migration, Displacement, and Health Inequities: A Comparative Study of Refugee Populations and Access to Healthcare Services

Muhammad Riaz, Neelam Tariq, Madiha Iqbal et al.. Migration, Displacement, and Health Inequities: A Comparative Study of Refugee Populations and Access to Healthcare Services. Social science review archives.. 2026

This paper examined healthcare inequities experienced by refugee and displaced populations across different host-country settings, with a focus on how varying levels of healthcare integration affect health outcomes. Rather than collecting empirical data, the study constructed a predictive modeling framework using published epidemiological evidence, migration theories, and humanitarian health frameworks, applying machine learning algorithms including Random Forest, XGBoost, and LightGBM to simulated data representing hypothetical refugee households distributed across four healthcare accessibility scenarios ranging from minimal to comprehensive universal access. All numerical findings are explicitly simulated and intended as a methodological template rather than as empirically derived results. The framework predicts that greater healthcare integration is associated with improved utilization, maternal and child health, chronic disease management, mental health access, and reduced inequities, with XGBoost performing best at identifying populations at risk of healthcare exclusion. The authors present this as a blueprint for future empirical research integrating migration studies, public health policy, epidemiology, and artificial intelligence to address disparities in refugee healthcare access.

Point of care molecular cancer diagnostics

Seemesh Bhaskar, Saurabh Umrao, Hankeun Lee et al.. Point of care molecular cancer diagnostics. Lab on a Chip. 2026

This review examines the emerging field of point-of-care molecular diagnostics for cancer, focusing on how decentralized testing can overcome the limitations of centralized laboratory settings to accelerate clinical decision-making. The authors comprehensively survey advances in sample preparation, nucleic acid and protein biomarker detection, and imaging modalities compatible with point-of-care deployment, illustrating their relevance through clinical case studies drawn from hospital, outpatient, and oncology practice settings. Key enabling technologies evaluated include microfluidics, lab-on-a-chip platforms, nanomaterial-enhanced biosensors, and smartphone-integrated devices, alongside a critical assessment of their limitations. The review also analyzes the funding landscape, intellectual property considerations, and regulatory frameworks that govern the translation of these technologies from academic research into commercial products. The authors conclude by projecting future directions that emphasize interdisciplinary innovation, equitable global access, and integration into precision medicine for cancer detection, therapy selection, and treatment monitoring.

Aminopeptidase N is a receptor for hedgehog merbecoviruses

Min Jin, Victoria A. Jefferson, Zhe Zhao et al.. Aminopeptidase N is a receptor for hedgehog merbecoviruses. Nature Microbiology. 2026

This paper investigated how hedgehog merbecoviruses — relatives of Middle East respiratory syndrome coronavirus circulating in European and Asian hedgehogs — enter host cells. Using cell-based assays, pseudotype experiments with a vesicular stomatitis virus reporter system, and protein binding assays, the researchers identified aminopeptidase N (APN) as the cellular receptor that these viruses use for entry. Hedgehog coronavirus spike proteins showed greater infectivity when produced at temperatures matching hedgehog physiology rather than human body temperature, and a broad screen of mammalian APN orthologues revealed highly restricted cross-species receptor use, with human APN unable to support infection. Electron cryomicroscopy of the glycoprotein–receptor interface uncovered a structurally distinct binding mode compared to other known coronavirus spike–APN interactions, providing a molecular explanation for the species barrier. These findings expand knowledge of receptor utilization across merbecoviruses and betacoronaviruses while informing assessments of the zoonotic risk posed by hedgehog merbecoviruses.

Modulation of Gut Microbiota Through Functional Foods to Reduce Low-Grade Chronic Inflammation

Pakistan Journal of Medical & Cardiological Review. Modulation of Gut Microbiota Through Functional Foods to Reduce Low-Grade Chronic Inflammation. Pakistan Journal of Medical & Cardiological Review. 2026

This paper reviewed the relationship between gut microbiota dysbiosis and chronic low-grade inflammation, which underlies conditions such as obesity, cardiovascular disease, metabolic syndrome, and cancer. The authors examined how the diverse microbial community of the gastrointestinal tract regulates immune responses, nutrient metabolism, and intestinal barrier integrity, and how disruptions to this community contribute to inflammatory and metabolic dysfunction. The review focused on dietary interventions using functional foods, particularly probiotics, prebiotics, and bioactive compounds, as strategies to restore microbial balance and suppress harmful inflammatory activity. Advances in microbiome sequencing technologies were highlighted as enabling more targeted nutritional approaches by deepening understanding of host–microbe interactions. The authors conclude that modulating gut microbiota through functional foods represents a promising and non-invasive strategy for reducing chronic inflammation and improving overall metabolic and immune health.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the challenge of monitoring maritime vessels by fusing two complementary data sources: the Automatic Identification System, which provides vessel identification but has known vulnerabilities, and space-based radio frequency observations, which are independent but lack identification information. Following a structured data science methodology, the researchers developed a Pair Hidden Markov Model to probabilistically align individual radio frequency detections with AIS vessel trajectories, accounting for temporal and spatial noise as well as timing discrepancies. The model was evaluated on real AIS data combined with synthetic radio frequency detections, benchmarked against nearest-neighbor baselines, and analyzed for systematic biases related to trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and spatial separation. A log-odds scoring variant that compares the full model against a null model substantially improved both precision and the ability to detect vessels that have disabled their AIS transponders, reducing several bias issues though a tendency to favor candidates farther from the radio frequency detection remained. Despite these improvements, a time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline without any model fitting still outperformed the Pair Hidden Markov Model, indicating that the proposed formulation approaches but does not yet surpass simpler heuristic methods for this single-detection scenario.

FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices

Jonathan D. McCart, Pranav I. Deevi, Mehdi Azabou et al.. FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated how to make brain-to-text decoding systems faster, more portable, and practical for real-world use in people with speech-related neurological conditions. The researchers developed FETTUCCINE, an end-to-end framework that repurposes automatic speech recognition model architectures to decode neural activity recorded from an intracortical speech neuroprosthesis into text, replacing the computationally heavy language modeling approaches used in prior systems. The framework was evaluated on publicly available data from a neuroprosthesis user and demonstrated competitive word error rates while running efficiently on a commercially available mobile device with dramatically faster-than-real-time throughput, representing the first demonstration of brain-to-text decoding on mobile hardware. The system also includes mechanisms for adapting to neural signal changes over time and uses gradient-based saliency maps to identify which neural features drive decoding, with these features aligning with established physiological knowledge. Overall, the work addresses key infrastructural barriers to deploying speech neuroprostheses outside of laboratory settings.

Training Reasoning Models in Simulation to Natively Use Real Robots

Jiaming Tang, Zhuoyang Zhang, Junxian Guo et al.. Training Reasoning Models in Simulation to Natively Use Real Robots. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether open-weight reasoning models can be trained to control real robots through reinforcement learning conducted entirely in simulation, without requiring human demonstrations. The authors developed a Post-training Engine for Agentic Robotics and applied robot-use reinforcement learning to train a large open-weight model on simulated manipulation tasks, where the model observes the environment, reasons via chain of thought, and directly outputs end-effector poses. Training on simulated benchmarks produced dramatic improvements in task success rates and, critically, the learned skills transferred to an unseen real dual-arm robot and to novel instructions not encountered during training. The trained model proved substantially more robust to task variations than a vision-language-action model fine-tuned on demonstrations, succeeding on novel tasks in familiar scenes at a far higher rate. Importantly, the robotics training did not degrade the model's general capabilities across a range of reasoning, coding, and vision benchmarks, suggesting that robot-use reinforcement learning is a viable component of broad foundation-model post-training.

On the pure part of the cohomology of character varieties

Olivier Schiffmann, Eric Vasserot. On the pure part of the cohomology of character varieties. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper resolves the purity conjecture of Hausel and Rodriguez-Villegas, which relates the graded dimension of the pure part of the cohomology of a twisted character variety for the general linear group to the cohomology of a quiver variety associated to a specific quiver with one vertex and multiple loops. The proof proceeds by transferring the conjecture, via the previously established P=W theorem, into a statement about the perverse filtration on the cohomology of a stack of Higgs bundles on a curve. The authors then deploy the theory of BPS Lie algebras together with several recent results—including chi-independence, a stack version of P=C, and an analytic neighborhood theorem—to reduce the problem to a comparison between BPS Lie algebras associated to Higgs bundle stacks and to representation stacks of a preprojective algebra. This final comparison is achieved through a quiver analogue of Markman's tautological generation theorem for BPS Lie algebras. As a consequence, the work yields an explicit formula for the pure part of the mixed Hodge polynomial of the character variety in terms of Kac polynomials.

Polynomial-Time Solvability of the Multi-Dimensional Slope Change Problem

Apoorva Narula, Santanu S. Dey. Polynomial-Time Solvability of the Multi-Dimensional Slope Change Problem. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies the problem of detecting slope changes in multidimensional time series by fitting continuous piecewise-linear functions that share a single breakpoint, examining both a dense setting where any dimension may change slope and a sparse setting where at most a fixed number of dimensions may do so. Despite the resulting optimization problems being nonconvex mixed-integer nonlinear programs that can even yield irrational solutions for rational inputs, the authors prove that all six problem variants across three common loss functions are exactly solvable in polynomial time. They developed efficient algorithms for the least-squares case and implemented them, testing on simulated data as well as real-world datasets spanning climate, motion tracking, and engine sensor domains. The dense algorithm handles very large instances in well under a second on a laptop, and both algorithms are orders of magnitude faster than a leading commercial solver, which frequently fails to reach near-optimal solutions within generous time limits on larger instances. These results resolve a seemingly hard class of optimization problems and provide practical tools for scalable changepoint detection in high-dimensional settings.

Modeling the Frictional Contact of Continuum Robots with Environments through Linear Complementarity Problem

Jia Shen, Junhyoung Ha, Yue Chen. Modeling the Frictional Contact of Continuum Robots with Environments through Linear Complementarity Problem. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of accurately modeling friction in continuum robots interacting with their environments, where existing approaches inadequately capture static friction and motion hysteresis. The authors developed a novel friction model based on complementarity constraints within the Coulomb friction framework, formulated as a Linear Complementarity Problem. The model accounts for both static and sliding friction, hysteresis behavior, and the robot deformation resulting from environmental contact, while remaining computationally efficient enough for real-time implementation. Experimental validation with a tendon-driven continuum robot demonstrated a substantial reduction in tip position error compared to a frictionless model, confirming the practical utility of the approach for motion planning and control applications.

Multiplexed Nucleic Acid Detection Using Sequence-Specific Cleavage Mediated by Repair Endonucleases.

Garden PM, Li Y, Murugan V et al.. Multiplexed Nucleic Acid Detection Using Sequence-Specific Cleavage Mediated by Repair Endonucleases.. ACS sensors. 2026

This paper introduced TIMBER, a non-CRISPR nucleic acid diagnostic platform that uses repair endonucleases to achieve sequence-specific cleavage of probes containing abasic sites when hybridized to a matched target sequence. Unlike CRISPR-based diagnostics, TIMBER avoids nonspecific collateral nuclease activity, which simplifies multiplexing and integration with amplification methods. The platform achieves single-nucleotide polymorphism discrimination and can detect targets at very low concentrations without preamplification, or at the single-copy-per-microliter level when paired with RT-PCR or RT-LAMP, with results readable by fluorescence or lateral flow. The authors validated the approach by detecting SARS-CoV-2 in clinical saliva samples and demonstrated simultaneous detection of four targets including EGFR mutations relevant to lung cancer genotyping. Overall, TIMBER is presented as a flexible, low-instrumentation diagnostic solution applicable to both infectious disease surveillance and precision oncology.

Beyond blood: a narrative review of advancements in saliva-based diagnostics.

Mondal S, Das Sharma A, Mandal SK et al.. Beyond blood: a narrative review of advancements in saliva-based diagnostics.. Expert review of molecular diagnostics. 2026

This narrative review examined the potential of human saliva as a noninvasive diagnostic biofluid capable of replacing or supplementing conventional blood-based testing. The authors surveyed salivary biomarkers across a broad range of clinical domains, including metabolic disease, cardiovascular risk, sepsis, endocrinology, infectious diseases, cancer liquid biopsy, and neurodegenerative conditions, while also evaluating the analytical technologies used to detect them, from immunoassays and mass spectrometry to wearable electrochemical biosensors. Existing regulatory-cleared platforms were distinguished from investigational discovery-phase panels to clarify the current state of clinical readiness. Despite substantial promise, the review identifies persistent barriers to mainstream adoption, including unstandardized collection protocols, interference from the oral matrix, and the absence of validated normative reference intervals. The authors conclude that broad clinical translation will require universal sampling standardization, matrix-matched biosensor engineering for ultra-low-concentration detection, and rigorous multicenter prospective trials to establish clinical cutoffs and validate the kinetic relationship between blood and salivary analyte concentrations.

Consented Universal Newborn Screening for Congenital Cytomegalovirus Infection Using Dried Blood Spot and Dried Saliva Spot Specimens: Implementation and Relative Programmatic Accuracy.

Santander E, Reuvers E, Lacaria M et al.. Consented Universal Newborn Screening for Congenital Cytomegalovirus Infection Using Dried Blood Spot and Dried Saliva Spot Specimens: Implementation and Relative Programmatic Accuracy.. International journal of neonatal screening. 2026

This study evaluated whether dried blood spot or dried saliva spot specimens perform better for universal newborn screening of congenital cytomegalovirus infection. Using a prospective paired-sample pilot across multiple birthing centers, researchers collected both specimen types from tens of thousands of newborns and tested them with quantitative PCR assays, with positive screens confirmed by urine testing. Dried saliva spots did not significantly improve sensitivity for detecting congenital cytomegalovirus compared to dried blood spots, while producing a substantially higher false positive rate. Additionally, dried saliva spot screening frequently exceeded the timeframes required to offer confirmatory diagnostic testing within the critical early window of life. Based on this performance profile, the authors concluded that dried saliva spots do not support population-wide implementation in their newborn screening program.

Vitamin D&lt;sub&gt;3&lt;/sub&gt; oral rinsing reduces salivary SARS-CoV-2 antigen detectability: a clinical trial with public health implications.

Feteih SMN, Heaphy ELG, Aljagthmi A et al.. Vitamin D&lt;sub&gt;3&lt;/sub&gt; oral rinsing reduces salivary SARS-CoV-2 antigen detectability: a clinical trial with public health implications.. Frontiers in oral health. 2026

This clinical trial investigated whether rinsing the mouth with a Vitamin D₃ solution could affect the accuracy of saliva-based rapid antigen tests for SARS-CoV-2. Adults with confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection provided paired saliva samples immediately before and after a one-minute Vitamin D₃ oral rinse, with samples analyzed by both rapid antigen test and rapid RT-PCR under blinded conditions. The rinse converted a large majority of antigen-positive samples to antigen-negative, substantially reducing overall antigen test positivity, while RT-PCR positivity and viral RNA levels remained largely unchanged. This discordance between antigen and molecular results indicates that the viral RNA was still present in saliva after rinsing, suggesting the rinse interfered with antigen detection rather than eliminating the virus. The findings identify Vitamin D₃ oral rinsing as a previously unrecognized pre-analytical variable that can produce false-negative results in saliva-based rapid antigen testing, with potential implications for public health surveillance and infectious disease screening protocols.

Sample-to-answer automated centrifugal microfluidics with computer-vision readout for point-of-care molecular diagnostics

Akhtar AS, Lapins N, Moura JM et al.. Sample-to-answer automated centrifugal microfluidics with computer-vision readout for point-of-care molecular diagnostics. 2026

The paper addressed the need for automated, decentralized diagnostic tools capable of handling infectious disease outbreaks without relying on centralized laboratories. The researchers developed a fully automated centrifugal microfluidic platform that integrates loop-mediated isothermal amplification with computer-vision-based optical readout, requiring only a single button press to execute the complete sample-to-answer workflow. The system was validated for SARS-CoV-2 detection using RNA fragments spiked into water and artificial saliva, achieving consistent detection at low copy numbers per reaction while processing dozens of samples within under an hour. The platform requires no user intervention during operation and is designed to be broadly adaptable to other infectious disease targets, positioning it as a template for rapid molecular diagnostics in resource-limited or point-of-care settings.

Diagnostic Accuracy of Molecular Testing on Saliva and Oral Swabs for Pulmonary Tuberculosis.

Vargas DA, Fuertes-Bucheli JF, Sanchez-Hidalgo A et al.. Diagnostic Accuracy of Molecular Testing on Saliva and Oral Swabs for Pulmonary Tuberculosis.. Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America. 2026

This study evaluated whether molecular testing on saliva and oral swabs could serve as accurate, non-sputum alternatives for diagnosing pulmonary tuberculosis. Using a nested case-control design within a prospective cohort of adults and children at primary care centers in Colombia, the researchers collected paired saliva and nylon-flocked oral swab samples from participants with culture-confirmed tuberculosis and matched culture-negative controls, then tested these samples using Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra. Saliva demonstrated high sensitivity and specificity against sputum culture, surpassing the World Health Organization's target sensitivity threshold for a non-sputum diagnostic test, while oral swabs showed high specificity but meaningfully lower sensitivity than saliva. Saliva sensitivity significantly exceeded that of oral swabs, though the two specimen types did not differ significantly in specificity. Both collection methods were well accepted by participants, supporting their potential as practical, low-complexity alternatives to sputum-based tuberculosis diagnosis.

Andes virus outbreak linked to expedition cruise ship travel, multi-country investigation and response, April to June 2026

Oda E. van den Berg, UKHSA ANDV Team, Ettore Severi et al.. Andes virus outbreak linked to expedition cruise ship travel, multi-country investigation and response, April to June 2026. Eurosurveillance. 2026

The abstract describes an active outbreak investigation rather than a completed study with findings, but enough detail is present for a brief summary. This report describes a multi-country investigation into an outbreak of Andes orthohantavirus linked to an expedition cruise ship operating internationally. Cases arose among both passengers and crew members representing dozens of nationalities, resulting in a notable case fatality proportion. The response required medical evacuation, repatriation, international contact tracing, and coordinated isolation and quarantine measures across multiple jurisdictions. Investigations were ongoing at the time of reporting, with the source of the outbreak, associated risk factors, and routes of transmission not yet fully determined.

Point of care molecular cancer diagnostics

Seemesh Bhaskar, Saurabh Umrao, Hankeun Lee et al.. Point of care molecular cancer diagnostics. Lab on a Chip. 2026

This review examines the current state and future trajectory of point-of-care molecular diagnostics for cancer, addressing the need to move beyond centralized laboratory testing toward decentralized, rapid, and accessible approaches. The authors synthesize clinical requirements, technological advances, and translational considerations through case studies drawn from oncology settings including hospitals, outpatient clinics, and dental practices. Key technologies evaluated include microfluidics, lab-on-a-chip platforms, nanomaterial-enhanced biosensors, smartphone-integrated devices, and advances in nucleic acid and protein biomarker detection alongside imaging modalities. The review also analyzes funding landscapes, intellectual property considerations, and regulatory frameworks that govern the transition of these technologies from research into commercial use. The authors conclude that interdisciplinary innovation, equitable access, and integration with precision medicine will be central to realizing the potential of point-of-care diagnostics for cancer detection, therapy selection, and treatment monitoring.

Hantavirus Emergence in a Changing World: Virology, Pathogenesis, Surveillance, and One Health Preparedness

Maria E. Ramos‐Niño, Nicolette Chiem, Prakash V. A. K. Ramdass. Hantavirus Emergence in a Changing World: Virology, Pathogenesis, Surveillance, and One Health Preparedness. Microorganisms. 2026

This review examines hantaviruses as emerging rodent-borne pathogens responsible for two serious syndromes, hemorrhagic fever with renal syndrome and hantavirus cardiopulmonary syndrome, in the context of a rapidly changing global environment. The authors synthesize current knowledge spanning virology, epidemiology, pathogenesis, clinical presentation, diagnostics, and surveillance to provide a comprehensive overview of the field. A central focus is placed on how climate change, urbanization, and land-use transformation alter rodent reservoir dynamics and increase the risk of spillover to humans, while also highlighting significant surveillance and diagnostic gaps in tropical and Caribbean regions where the burden may be underestimated. The review discusses recent advances including molecular and genomic diagnostics, vaccine candidates, monoclonal antibody therapies, and climate-based early warning systems as tools for improved outbreak response. The authors conclude that integrated One Health surveillance frameworks combining human, animal, and environmental monitoring, alongside strengthened laboratory capacity and regional collaboration, are essential for reducing the global burden of hantavirus disease and enhancing preparedness for future zoonotic threats.

Advances and Challenges in Wearable Sensors for Health Monitoring

Laís Canniatti Brazaca, Alvaro Moreno Lozano, Beatriz Mayol et al.. Advances and Challenges in Wearable Sensors for Health Monitoring. ACS Applied Materials & Interfaces. 2026

This mega-review examines the current state of wearable sensor technologies for health monitoring, bringing together leading researchers to synthesize advances across the field. The authors cover chemical and biological sensors applied to multiple biofluids such as sweat, saliva, tears, interstitial fluid, and cerebrospinal fluid, alongside physical sensors measuring parameters including heart rate, blood pressure, and body temperature. Device formats reviewed range from smart contact lenses and textiles to mouthguards, wristbands, and implantable systems, with attention to soft electronics, materials science, and energy harvesting strategies that enable practical wearability. The review further addresses signal acquisition and transmission, and the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning to manage the large data volumes generated and enhance analytical performance. The authors conclude by identifying promising research directions and discussing the business, regulatory, and ethical considerations that must be navigated to translate these technologies into clinical and consumer health applications.

Integrated microfluidic biosensors: shaping the future of quantitative life sciences and on-chip molecular diagnostics

Ty Downing Naquin, Chloe M. Naquin, Qian WU et al.. Integrated microfluidic biosensors: shaping the future of quantitative life sciences and on-chip molecular diagnostics. Lab on a Chip. 2026

This review paper examines the integration of microfluidic technologies with biosensor platforms and their applications across biomedical and environmental fields. The authors survey a broad range of microfluidic approaches, including inertial separation, acoustofluidics, dielectrophoresis, optofluidics, and immunoassays, alongside biosensor modalities encompassing electrochemical, electrophysiological, plasmonic, Raman, and quantum sensing. These integrated systems are capable of detecting analytes spanning a wide size range, from nanometer-scale molecules such as DNA, RNA, and proteins to micrometer-scale exosomes and cells and even small organisms. The review identifies key advantages of such integrated platforms, including enhanced sensitivity, speed, portability, and real-time monitoring, with applications in human and animal health diagnostics, environmental sensing, and point-of-care testing. The authors also address existing technical challenges, future development opportunities, and highlight commercially successful products that have emerged from these integrated microfluidic technologies.

Human infection with Andes hantavirus: an update for the general physician

Emanuele Durante‐Mangoni, Iolanda Cafarella, Silvia Mercadante et al.. Human infection with Andes hantavirus: an update for the general physician. European Journal of Internal Medicine. 2026

Andes virus (ANDV), a zoonotic RNA virus transmitted through inhalation of aerosols from infected rodent excreta, is unique among hantaviruses in its capacity for sustained human-to-human transmission via respiratory droplets and close contact. This paper reviews the clinical, epidemiological, and public health features of ANDV infection to update general physicians on its presentation and management. The most severe manifestation is Hantavirus cardiopulmonary syndrome, characterized by acute respiratory failure, haemodynamic instability, organ involvement, and dysregulated immune responses, with disease severity linked to specific haematological and biochemical markers. No approved antiviral treatment or vaccine exists, leaving supportive care as the only management strategy, and case fatality rates remain high. A recent cruise ship outbreak involving passengers of multiple nationalities illustrates how ANDV's combination of human-to-human transmissibility and international travel can rapidly give a regionally endemic pathogen global reach, highlighting the need for clinician awareness and coordinated outbreak preparedness.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of monitoring maritime activities by fusing two complementary data sources: the Automatic Identification System, which identifies vessels but has known vulnerabilities including spoofing, and space-based radio frequency observations, which detect vessel activity independently but cannot identify ships on their own. Following a structured data science methodology, the researchers developed a Pair Hidden Markov Model to align individual radio frequency detections with AIS vessel trajectories by jointly modeling temporal and spatial information along with noise and timing discrepancies. The approach was evaluated on real AIS data combined with synthetic radio frequency detections, with performance assessed against nearest-neighbor baselines and analyzed for systematic biases related to trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and spatial separation. A log-odds scoring variant that compares the full model against a null model substantially improved precision and the ability to detect "dark" vessels that appear in radio frequency data but not in AIS. Despite these improvements, a time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline without any model fitting remained superior, indicating that the single-detection Pair Hidden Markov Model formulation approaches but does not surpass a well-designed heuristic comparator.

FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices

Jonathan D. McCart, Pranav I. Deevi, Mehdi Azabou et al.. FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of deploying speech neuroprostheses — systems that decode neural activity into text for people who have lost the ability to speak — in practical, real-world settings. The authors developed FETTUCCINE, an end-to-end brain-to-text decoding framework that repurposes automatic speech recognition model architectures to replace the computationally heavy language modeling stacks used in existing systems. When evaluated on data from an intracortical neuroprosthesis user, the framework achieved low word error rates and demonstrated successful fine-tuning to maintain performance across future recording sessions. Critically, the system ran on a commercially available mobile device with dramatically faster-than-real-time throughput, representing the first demonstration of brain-to-text decoding on mobile hardware. The authors also showed that gradient-based saliency maps from their models identify neural features consistent with established physiological knowledge, adding interpretability alongside the practical performance gains.

Training Reasoning Models in Simulation to Natively Use Real Robots

Jiaming Tang, Zhuoyang Zhang, Junxian Guo et al.. Training Reasoning Models in Simulation to Natively Use Real Robots. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether open-weight reasoning models can be trained to control real robots through reinforcement learning conducted entirely in simulation, without requiring human demonstrations. The authors developed a Post-training Engine for Agentic Robotics and applied robot-use reinforcement learning to train a large open-weight reasoning model on simulated manipulation tasks, following an approach analogous to how frontier models are trained for computer use. The trained model observes its environment, reasons through a chain of thought, and directly outputs end-effector poses without additional scaffolding. Training in simulation produced skills that transferred substantially to real-world settings, including an unseen robot and novel instructions, with the model succeeding on a real dual-arm robot in the majority of trials compared to complete failure from the base model. The work also demonstrates that this approach yields considerably greater robustness to novel task instructions than a vision-language-action model fine-tuned on demonstrations, while preserving the model's general reasoning, coding, and vision capabilities.

On the pure part of the cohomology of character varieties

Olivier Schiffmann, Eric Vasserot. On the pure part of the cohomology of character varieties. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses a conjecture of Hausel and Rodriguez-Villegas concerning the pure part of the mixed Hodge structure on the cohomology of twisted character varieties for the general linear group. The conjecture predicts that the graded dimension of this pure part can be expressed in terms of the cohomology of a quiver variety associated to a specific quiver with one vertex and multiple loops, yielding an explicit formula involving Kac polynomials. The authors prove this conjecture by translating it, via the previously established theorem equating perverse and weight filtrations, into a problem about the perverse filtration on the cohomology of the stack of Higgs bundles on a curve. They then draw on several recent advances—including the theory of BPS Lie algebras with their independence property, the stack version of the correspondence between perverse and cohomological filtrations, and an analytic neighborhood theorem—to reduce the problem to comparing BPS Lie algebras arising from Higgs bundle stacks and from representation stacks of preprojective algebras of the relevant quiver. The final comparison is resolved through a quiver analogue of Markman's tautological generation theorem for BPS Lie algebras.

Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data

Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri. Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether recent hints of non-standard cosmological expansion are accompanied by modifications to gravitational dynamics beyond the standard model. Using a non-parametric joint reconstruction approach, the authors simultaneously infer the effective dark energy density and two gravitational response functions from a combination of CMB, baryon acoustic oscillation, supernova, and large-scale structure datasets. They find a persistent feature in the dark energy density at intermediate redshifts consistent with a phantom divide crossing, a result that proves robust across different data combinations and when gravitational modifications are allowed to vary freely. The gravitational response functions show suggestive enhancements over a similar redshift range, though these are less statistically significant, and including large-scale structure data instead favors a suppressed lensing response. The authors conclude that current departures from the standard cosmological model are most compelling in the expansion history, while correlated modifications to gravity remain possible but await confirmation from future surveys.

Modeling the Frictional Contact of Continuum Robots with Environments through Linear Complementarity Problem

Jia Shen, Junhyoung Ha, Yue Chen. Modeling the Frictional Contact of Continuum Robots with Environments through Linear Complementarity Problem. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of accurately modeling friction in continuum robots interacting with their environments, noting that existing approaches inadequately capture static friction and motion hysteresis. The authors developed a novel friction model based on complementarity constraints within the Coulomb friction framework, formulated as a Linear Complementarity Problem. The model accounts for both static and sliding friction, hysteresis behavior, and the resulting robot deformation caused by environmental contact, while remaining computationally efficient enough for real-time implementation. Experimental validation with a tendon-driven continuum robot demonstrated a substantial reduction in tip position error compared to a frictionless model, confirming the practical utility of the approach for motion planning and control.

Antimicrobial-laden PerOssal beads display in vitro inhibition and killing of extensively drug-resistant Gram-negative bacteria associated with conflict wounds in Ukraine

Henry Adam Claireaux, Edward J. A. Douglas, Scott J C Pallett et al.. Antimicrobial-laden PerOssal beads display in vitro inhibition and killing of extensively drug-resistant Gram-negative bacteria associated with conflict wounds in Ukraine. Bone and Joint Research. 2026

This study investigated the use of antimicrobial-laden PerOssal hydroxyapatite and calcium sulphate beads as a local drug delivery strategy against extensively drug-resistant Gram-negative bacteria isolated from conflict wounds in Ukraine. The researchers tested these beads, loaded with various antimicrobials including gentamicin, tobramycin, cefazolin, rifampicin, and co-amoxiclav, using zone-of-inhibition testing and time-kill analysis against bacterial isolates that were resistant to multiple standard systemic antimicrobials. Single antimicrobial agents such as gentamicin, tobramycin, or cefazolin alone showed only weak growth inhibition, whereas gentamicin combined with rifampicin achieved consistent broad-spectrum bacterial inhibition and killing, and co-amoxiclav-loaded beads inhibited all tested strains. The findings suggest that local delivery of antimicrobials via these beads can achieve sufficient drug concentrations to overcome resistance that would render systemic therapy ineffective. The authors propose that this approach could reduce infection risk in heavily contaminated open fractures when surgical wound excision is delayed, with relevance to both conflict and civilian disaster scenarios, though in vivo validation is still needed.

Modulation of Gut Microbiota Through Functional Foods to Reduce Low-Grade Chronic Inflammation

Pakistan Journal of Medical & Cardiological Review. Modulation of Gut Microbiota Through Functional Foods to Reduce Low-Grade Chronic Inflammation. Pakistan Journal of Medical & Cardiological Review. 2026

This paper examines the relationship between gut microbiota dysbiosis and chronic low-grade inflammation, which underlies numerous long-term conditions including obesity, cardiovascular disease, metabolic syndrome, and cancer. The authors review how the gastrointestinal microbial community regulates immune responses, nutrient metabolism, pathogen resistance, and intestinal barrier integrity, and how disruptions to this community drive inflammatory and metabolic dysfunction. The paper focuses on dietary interventions using functional foods — specifically probiotics, prebiotics, and bioactive compounds — as tools to restore microbial balance by promoting beneficial bacteria and suppressing harmful microbial activity. The authors also highlight how advances in microbiome sequencing technologies have deepened understanding of host–microbe interactions and enabled more targeted nutritional strategies. Overall, the paper argues that modulating gut microbiota through functional foods represents a promising non-invasive approach to reducing chronic inflammation and improving both metabolic and immune health.

Development and Application of a Dual-Readout RPA-PfAgo System for Rapid Detection of Streptococcus agalactiae in Bovine Milk

Xujie Zhao, Yuying Du, Qianlei Zhu et al.. Development and Application of a Dual-Readout RPA-PfAgo System for Rapid Detection of Streptococcus agalactiae in Bovine Milk. Veterinary Sciences. 2026

This study developed and validated a rapid molecular detection platform for *Streptococcus agalactiae*, the primary causative agent of contagious bovine mastitis, in dairy milk samples. The researchers designed a dual-readout system combining recombinase polymerase amplification with *Pyrococcus furiosus* Argonaute protein activity, targeting the conserved *cfb* gene, with results readable either by real-time fluorescence or lateral flow dipstick formats. The platform was optimized through systematic screening of primer and guide DNA combinations, achieving a detection limit as low as ten copies per microliter with high analytical specificity and no cross-reactivity against non-target bacteria. Field validation using bovine milk samples from multiple dairy farming regions demonstrated complete concordance between both readout formats and a standardized quantitative PCR assay, while all molecular methods outperformed conventional bacteriological culture in identifying positive cases. The authors conclude that this dual-readout platform offers a rapid, reliable, and fieldable diagnostic tool for detecting *S. agalactiae* in bovine milk, with potential utility in dairy herd surveillance and management.

Editor's Choice – European Society for Vascular Surgery (ESVS) 2026 Clinical Practice Guidelines on the Management of Vascular Graft and Endograft Infections

Gut Microbial Metabotypes Shape Polyphenol Bioactivity: Toward Precision Nutrition Strategies for Human Health

Pasquale Perrone, Stefania D’Angelo. Gut Microbial Metabotypes Shape Polyphenol Bioactivity: Toward Precision Nutrition Strategies for Human Health. Nutrients. 2026

This review examined how gut microbial metabolism shapes the biological effects of dietary polyphenols, focusing on the concept that individuals differ meaningfully in their capacity to convert polyphenols into bioactive metabolites. Because many polyphenols are poorly absorbed in the upper gut and reach the colon largely intact, intestinal microorganisms transform them into smaller compounds such as urolithins, equol, and enterolignans, which may have distinct biological activities compared to their parent molecules. The review catalogued major microbial metabotypes—classifications of individuals based on which of these metabolites they produce—and assessed the evidence linking them to health-relevant processes including mitochondrial function, inflammation, endothelial homeostasis, and gut-brain communication, carefully distinguishing human evidence from preclinical findings. The authors concluded that while metabotype-based stratification holds promise for improving the design of nutritional studies and identifying responders, clinical translation is currently hampered by inconsistent methodology, non-standardized analytical thresholds, and insufficient long-term intervention data. Microbial metabotypes are therefore characterized as valuable research and trial-design tools rather than established instruments for personalized dietary prescription.

ECCO Guidelines on the Prevention, Diagnosis, and Management of Infections in Inflammatory Bowel Disease

Henit A. Yanai, Pierre Ellul, Bram Verstockt et al.. ECCO Guidelines on the Prevention, Diagnosis, and Management of Infections in Inflammatory Bowel Disease. Journal of Crohn s and Colitis. 2026

This paper presents updated clinical guidelines from the European Crohn's and Colitis Organisation addressing infection-related risks in patients with inflammatory bowel disease. The guidelines were developed in response to the rapid expansion of immunosuppressive and biologic therapies used to treat Crohn's disease and ulcerative colitis, including newer small molecules and advanced biologics that carry distinct infection risk profiles compared to older agents. The authors synthesized current evidence on risk stratification and the prevention, diagnosis, and management of viral, bacterial, mycobacterial, and vaccine-preventable infections in this immunocompromised population. The update places particular emphasis on preventative strategies tailored to the contemporary treatment landscape, recognizing that potent immune modulation increases susceptibility to opportunistic infections that can cause significant morbidity or mortality. The guidelines supersede prior iterations and are intended to provide clinicians with structured, evidence-based guidance reflecting the most recent developments in both therapeutics and vaccination strategies for inflammatory bowel disease.

Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion

Daniel Grimm, Kyra Jongman, Renato Cordeiro Ferreira et al.. Pairing AIS with RF: Analysis of a Markovian Approach for Maritime Data Fusion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of monitoring maritime activities by fusing two complementary data sources: the Automatic Identification System, which identifies vessels but has known vulnerabilities including spoofing and deliberate deactivation, and space-based radio frequency observations, which detect vessel activity independently but cannot identify ships on their own. Following a structured data science methodology, the researchers developed a Pair Hidden Markov Model framework to align individual radio frequency detections with AIS vessel trajectories by jointly modeling temporal and spatial information alongside noise and timing discrepancies. The approach was evaluated on real AIS data combined with synthetic radio frequency detections, benchmarked against nearest-neighbor baselines, and analyzed for systematic biases related to trajectory length, sampling density, stationarity, and spatial separation. A log-odds scoring variant that compares the full model against a null model without match transitions substantially improved both precision and the ability to detect vessels operating without AIS, and largely removed biases toward stationary trajectories. However, the best Pair Hidden Markov Model formulation still did not surpass a simple time-weighted nearest-neighbor baseline, indicating that this hand-designed single-detection approach approaches but does not yet exceed straightforward geometric methods.

FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices

Jonathan D. McCart, Pranav I. Deevi, Mehdi Azabou et al.. FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addressed the challenge of deploying brain-to-text speech neuroprostheses in real-world settings, where existing systems rely on computationally intensive language models that produce variable latencies and are impractical outside laboratory environments. The researchers developed FETTUCCINE, an end-to-end framework that adapts automatic speech recognition architectures to decode neural activity from an intracortical speech neuroprosthesis into text, while incorporating mechanisms to handle neural signal drift over time. When evaluated on publicly available data from a neuroprosthesis user, the system achieved word error rates as low as approximately four percent and demonstrated successful fine-tuning to maintain performance across future recording sessions. Critically, the framework was shown to run on a commercially available mobile device at speeds far exceeding real-time, representing the first demonstration of mobile brain-to-text decoding. The approach also provided interpretability through gradient-based saliency maps that highlighted neural features consistent with established physiological knowledge, suggesting the models capture meaningful neural representations of speech.

Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data

Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri. Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether recent hints of non-standard cosmological expansion are accompanied by modifications to gravitational dynamics beyond the standard model. Using a non-parametric joint reconstruction approach, the authors simultaneously inferred the effective dark energy density and two gravitational response functions from a combination of CMB, DESI DR2 BAO, supernova, and large-scale structure datasets, with and without a theory-motivated prior from stable Horndeski scalar-tensor models. A persistent feature in the dark energy density around intermediate redshifts was found, corresponding to a crossing of the phantom divide, and this feature proved robust across different data combinations and when gravitational modifications were allowed. The gravitational response functions showed suggestive but less significant enhancements over a similar redshift range, though including large-scale structure data instead favored a suppressed lensing response. The authors conclude that current departures from the standard cosmological model are most clearly manifested in the expansion history, while correlated modifications to gravitational dynamics remain plausible but await confirmation from future surveys.

Does Dark Energy Evolve $\textit{and}$ Cluster?

Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri. Does Dark Energy Evolve $\textit{and}$ Cluster?. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates whether the apparent dynamical evolution of dark energy inferred from recent observations is accompanied by corresponding modifications to gravitational dynamics governing clustering and lensing. Using a non-parametric, joint reconstruction of the effective dark-energy contribution to cosmic expansion and the gravitational response functions, without assuming model-specific relationships between these sectors, the authors combine cosmic microwave background data, baryon acoustic oscillation measurements, supernova observations, and large-scale structure surveys. They find a broad maximum in the effective dark-energy density at intermediate redshift, alongside suggestive low-redshift enhancements in the gravitational responses relative to general relativity predictions that are approximately coincident with this background feature. A covariance-mode analysis reveals that the leading observational constraints are dominated by the background expansion history, while the gravitational sector remains comparatively underconstrained. The authors conclude that current data cannot yet establish whether the features seen in the expansion and gravitational sectors share a common physical origin, leaving this determination to future Stage-IV surveys.

Towards invertibility of derived categories of sheaf-theoretic line operators

Claudia Scheimbauer, Jackson Van Dyke. Towards invertibility of derived categories of sheaf-theoretic line operators. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates conditions under which braided monoidal infinity-categories of sheaf-theoretic line operators, arising from three-dimensional topological quantum field theories, are invertible in the higher Morita category, which would make them determine invertible four-dimensional anomaly theories. The authors examine several classes of derived examples, showing that a symmetric monoidal category of quasi-coherent sheaves is invertible only when the underlying space is a point, and that convolution categories on loop stacks are Morita trivial when the stack satisfies appropriate descent conditions. A key finding is that non-degeneracy does not imply invertibility in the derived setting, in contrast to the finite braided tensor category case: a specific category of two-shifted D-modules associated to an odd vector space is three-dualizable and non-degenerate yet fails to be factorizable or cofactorizable, with connections to linear Rozansky–Witten theory. The authors also introduce a notion of anomaly for group actions on invertible braided monoidal infinity-categories as a Witt class, and demonstrate that representations of the quantum group of a torus at a root of unity are invertible relative to the quantum Frobenius.

WeightBench: Evaluating Language Models as Weight-Space Programmers

Aayush Marasini, Barsat Khadka, Nick Rahimi. WeightBench: Evaluating Language Models as Weight-Space Programmers. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the emerging capability of large language models to operate directly on the weight parameters of neural networks, a paradigm called Weight-Space Programming, wherein models can interpret, repair, or generate network weights without gradient-based training. To evaluate this capability systematically, the authors developed a benchmark comprising dozens of tasks organized into five tracks covering weight reading, fixing, writing, recognizing impossible edits, and data-free compression, tested across networks ranging from very small control policies to multi-billion parameter language models. Experiments revealed that frontier models can already interpret small networks and write functional control policies from scratch, but consistently fail at patching large language models, compressing networks without access to data, and distinguishing genuinely impossible edits from merely difficult ones. Performance varied substantially across model families, with the strongest tested agents achieving scores roughly around half the maximum on all tasks combined, indicating meaningful but incomplete mastery of this paradigm. The benchmark, task registry, and leaderboard are released publicly to support continued evaluation of this nascent capability.

Revisiting the use of adenosine deaminase in cerebrospinal fluid analysis for infectious meningoencephalitis.

Farias LABG, Neto OMV, Sobrinho EPL et al.. Revisiting the use of adenosine deaminase in cerebrospinal fluid analysis for infectious meningoencephalitis.. Arquivos de neuro-psiquiatria. 2026

This study investigated the diagnostic utility of adenosine deaminase levels measured in cerebrospinal fluid across a range of central nervous system infections, with particular attention to tropical diseases and HIV-infected patients, populations in which this marker had been insufficiently studied. Researchers analyzed cerebrospinal fluid adenosine deaminase levels alongside other laboratory parameters in over two hundred patients with various microbiologically confirmed diagnoses, comparing them against a non-infectious control group and defining diagnostic cutoff values. Elevated cerebrospinal fluid adenosine deaminase levels were found specifically in tuberculous meningitis and meningococcal meningitis, with the tuberculous group showing the highest levels compared to all other infectious etiologies. The marker demonstrated modest sensitivity and specificity for tuberculous meningitis, with higher cutoff values identified in the setting of central nervous system coinfections, and levels correlated positively with cerebrospinal fluid protein and weakly negatively with cerebrospinal fluid glucose. The authors conclude that cerebrospinal fluid adenosine deaminase may serve as a supportive, though not standalone, diagnostic tool in suspected tuberculous meningitis, meningococcal meningitis, and central nervous system coinfections.

Diagnostic Performance of the FilmArray BCID2 Multiplex PCR Panel Applied to Excised Heart Valve Specimens from Patients with Infective Endocarditis.

Bilewska A, Skrzeczyńska J, Dorota M et al.. Diagnostic Performance of the FilmArray BCID2 Multiplex PCR Panel Applied to Excised Heart Valve Specimens from Patients with Infective Endocarditis.. Microorganisms. 2026

This study evaluated whether the FilmArray BCID2 multiplex PCR panel, designed for blood culture identification, could be applied off-label to excised heart valve tissue from patients undergoing surgery for infective endocarditis. Researchers compared BCID2 results against both blood cultures and conventional valve cultures from a surgical cohort. When benchmarked against blood cultures, the assay showed moderate sensitivity and a high positive predictive value, but poor specificity and a low negative predictive value, with overall agreement between methods being weak. Against conventional valve culture as the reference, specificity was perfect but sensitivity was very low. The authors conclude that while the BCID2 panel may serve as a complementary diagnostic tool in selected patients, a negative result cannot exclude infective endocarditis, and larger multicentre studies are needed to clarify its clinical utility.

Rapid identification of porcine bacterial pathogens using an artificial intelligence Raman microbial analyzer for biology.

Lu C-H, Ke N-J, Liu C-C et al.. Rapid identification of porcine bacterial pathogens using an artificial intelligence Raman microbial analyzer for biology.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

This study evaluated whether an artificial intelligence-driven Raman spectroscopy platform could rapidly and accurately identify bacterial pathogens isolated from diseased pigs in a clinical veterinary diagnostic setting. Bacterial isolates were collected from diseased pig tissues, with species-level identity confirmed by polymerase chain reaction before Raman spectral data were acquired, providing reliable reference labels for assessing system performance. The commercial system, which combines Raman spectroscopy with deep learning, was tested against fourteen clinically relevant swine bacterial pathogens and delivered identification results in approximately two minutes per isolate. The system achieved perfect recall for some of the most common species tested and high recall exceeding ninety percent for several other important pathogens. The authors conclude that this approach offers a rapid, label-free, and highly automated alternative to conventional culture-based and molecular methods, with practical value for timely decision-making in routine veterinary diagnostics and potential relevance to public health given the zoonotic nature of some swine pathogens.

A Rapid Molecular Detection Method for Streptococcus agalactiae Based on Bacteriophage Lysin Lysis and Real-Time PCR.

Zhu H, Zeng X, Huang X et al.. A Rapid Molecular Detection Method for Streptococcus agalactiae Based on Bacteriophage Lysin Lysis and Real-Time PCR.. European journal of clinical microbiology & infectious diseases : official publication of the European Society of Clinical Microbiology. 2026

This study developed a rapid point-of-care molecular diagnostic method for detecting Group B Streptococcus, a major cause of neonatal infections, using a bacteriophage lysin to lyse bacterial cells and release nucleic acids for real-time PCR detection. The approach targeted the GBS cylA gene and a human internal control gene, achieving complete lysis and nucleic acid extraction within five minutes and total turnaround in approximately thirty-five minutes, compared to the eighteen to forty-eight hours required for conventional culture. The assay demonstrated no cross-reactivity and achieved high sensitivity and specificity in a clinical cohort of vaginal swab samples, with specificity further improving after sequencing-based resolution of discordant results. In a second validation cohort, the assay markedly outperformed antigen-based testing in sensitivity. The authors conclude that this bacteriophage lysin-based rapid PCR method shows strong diagnostic performance and potential utility for intrapartum or near-patient GBS screening, pending further real-world validation.

Temporal trends in pediatric group A streptococcal pharyngitis across the coronavirus disease 2019 era: A large retrospective cohort study.

Nas Y, Gözüküçük S, Bayırlı H et al.. Temporal trends in pediatric group A streptococcal pharyngitis across the coronavirus disease 2019 era: A large retrospective cohort study.. The Journal of international medical research. 2026

This study examined how the COVID-19 pandemic and its associated non-pharmaceutical interventions affected rates of Group A Streptococcus pharyngitis in children over time. Using a large retrospective cohort of pediatric patients presenting with suspected acute tonsillopharyngitis at a tertiary outpatient clinic, researchers compared Group A Streptococcus positivity rates across pre-pandemic, pandemic, and post-pandemic periods spanning roughly seven years. Positivity rates rose progressively across all three periods, with the lowest rates seen before the pandemic and the highest in the post-pandemic period, a trend that persisted after adjustment for age, sex, and diagnostic method. Older age and male sex were also independently associated with a greater likelihood of testing positive. The authors conclude that these trends likely reflect a combination of shifting healthcare utilization patterns, diagnostic practices, age distributions, and transmission dynamics, and call for continued surveillance to better characterize pediatric Group A Streptococcus pharyngitis.

Epidemiological surveillance reveals major pathogen dynamics in Brazilian Nile tilapia aquaculture from 2023 to 2025.

Cardoso-Guimarães M, Fernandez-Alarcon MF, Correa R et al.. Epidemiological surveillance reveals major pathogen dynamics in Brazilian Nile tilapia aquaculture from 2023 to 2025.. Frontiers in veterinary science. 2026

This study investigated the major pathogen dynamics threatening Nile tilapia aquaculture across Brazil's three primary producing regions over a two-year surveillance period. Researchers analyzed tens of thousands of diagnostic samples using bacteriological methods, real-time PCR, MALDI-TOF mass spectrometry, and antimicrobial susceptibility testing, applying generalized linear models, time-series analysis, co-detection network analysis, and pharmacological modeling to characterize pathogen occurrence and behavior. The surveillance revealed that the disease landscape is broader than previously recognized, with Infectious Spleen and Kidney Necrosis Virus, *Lactococcus* spp., *Flavobacterium* spp., and *Streptococcus* spp. all representing major concerns rather than streptococcosis alone. Most pathogens displayed stationary, endemic dynamics, while *Streptococcus* spp. showed non-stationary patterns consistent with active geographic expansion into the Northeast region, and antimicrobial resistance was structured by both pathogen identity and antibiotic, with fry exhibiting higher minimum inhibitory concentrations. The authors conclude that these findings provide a data-driven baseline to support regionalized early-warning systems and more rational antimicrobial stewardship in Brazilian tilapia production.

Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.

Walzl G, McHugh TD, Chakaya J et al.. Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.. The Lancet. Microbe. 2027

This paper reviewed advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarker research over a five-year period, synthesizing the evidence base that has informed updated WHO policy guidance consolidating recommendations for tuberculosis detection and drug-resistance testing. The review covers progress across screening, diagnosis, and treatment-monitoring tools, encompassing digital chest radiography with computer-aided detection, decentralised molecular platforms including low-complexity nucleic acid amplification tests, and expanded use of non-sputum specimens to improve diagnosis in children and sputum-scarce populations. Drug-resistance testing has advanced through rapid molecular assays and targeted next-generation sequencing, while urine-based lipoarabinomannan assays offer diagnostic gains specifically in people with HIV and advanced disease. Host blood transcriptomic and other biomarkers show promise for prognosis and triage, yet no biomarker has yet met WHO criteria for a point-of-care test capable of reliably distinguishing infection from disease, including subclinical tuberculosis. The paper concludes that despite meaningful progress, substantial gaps in diagnostic performance, implementation, and equity remain, underscoring ongoing research and programmatic priorities.

Point-of-care testing in the diagnosis of infectious diseases.

Fang Q, Lin Y, Hu G et al.. Point-of-care testing in the diagnosis of infectious diseases.. Frontiers in microbiology. 2026

This review examined point-of-care testing as a diagnostic approach for infectious diseases, motivated by the growing global burden of such diseases and the escalating threat of antimicrobial resistance. The authors surveyed the core technical platforms underlying current point-of-care technologies, including lateral flow assays, chemiluminescence immunoassays, isothermal amplification, CRISPR-based diagnostics, and microfluidic chip systems, alongside their application across a range of clinical infection types spanning respiratory, bloodstream, gastrointestinal, urinary, and sexually transmitted infections. The review also addressed existing limitations of these technologies, including constraints in detection sensitivity, quality control, cost, and integration into clinical workflows. The authors argue that ongoing advances in nanomaterials, microfluidic engineering, artificial intelligence, and industry standardization are driving improvements in performance and systematic deployment. Based on this trajectory, the review concludes that point-of-care testing holds strong potential to become a central diagnostic tool in both precision clinical care and broader public health efforts to control infectious disease outbreaks.
